# 10 — Roadmap and Task List

Ordering reflects the owner's priorities: **performance → observability → Kubernetes + Pi deployability → clustering/HA → breadth**. Each task lists the requirement IDs it satisfies and objective acceptance criteria (AC). A task is done only when its AC pass in CI. Tick boxes as you go and keep this file current.

**Every surface ships with its UI (owner rule 2026-11-16; API-901, ADR-914).** Every task that adds a setting, route or tool ships its UI control, help topic and Playwright test in the same task; a task without them isn't done. Exceptions are inherently offline commands, each with a one-line reason. The control is a form field, page, card or action. Role gating matches the API and is enforced by the server: viewers read, and operators or admins change. Each control has a `?` help topic that passes `check:help`, and at least one Playwright test names it (mocking API answers with `page.route` where the e2e server has no such source). A credential is only ever entered through `SecretField` (write-only, never shown or pre-filled; ADR-903 §9), and a `<redacted>` value is never shown as if it were the value. Security gates and bootstrap settings (keys, listeners, API address and TLS, trusted proxies, cluster init and join) get admin controls with the guardrails of ADR-914: admin only on the server, never agents, a typed confirmation, audit, and an automatic rollback for address changes; a key is never typed into the store it protects. A node-local section (`shared.rs` `NODE_LOCAL`) becomes editable only through `node_managed` (T23.10), as an entry for the node it applies to. The file wins over every UI value and is shown as "set by file". A task that adds no setting, route or tool (a deploy bundle, a benchmark, an internal fix, an outside integration) says "No UI: <reason>". This applies to all wave work, including tasks already ticked in M22 and M24, which T24.12–T24.18 catch up; M27 catches up the pre-fork features.

**Upstream (fork rule).** The fork's differences from `paimonsoror/telltaledns`, its last sync, the sync procedure and the upstream issues it triages are in [`spec/upstream.md`](upstream.md). A daily watch (`.github/workflows/upstream-watch.yml`) reports new upstream commits, conflicts and issue changes to the fork's "Upstream watch" issue. Upstream ADR numbers are kept; fork ADRs take 901 and up; colliding milestone or task IDs are renamed (M22/M23 became M28/M29 at the 2026-11-17 merge).

## M0 — Foundations (week 1)
- [x] **T0.1 Workspace scaffold.** Crates per `02 §2`, `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` where required, rust-toolchain pinned, CI (fmt, clippy, test, cargo-deny), `LICENSE` (Apache-2.0 OR MIT). *AC:* CI is green on an empty skeleton; `cargo deny check` passes. *Done 2026-10-02: local gate green (fmt/clippy/test/deny); GitHub Actions run pending until a remote exists.*
- [x] **T0.2 Config skeleton.** `telltale-config` with the TOML schema for listeners/upstreams/cache/telemetry, env overrides, `telltale config check`, JSON Schema output. *(OPS-005)* *AC:* unknown keys error with path; env override test. *Done 2026-10-02. Deferred: node.toml key allow-list (T5.5/CLU-006), GitOps mode (OPS-005, with API in M3), CLI-flag layer (with `telltale run`), config migrations (none needed at v1).*
- [x] **T0.3 Bench harness skeleton.** `bench/` with dnsperf runner, corpora generator, and JSON results. *(NFR-001)* *AC:* `make bench-smoke` runs against a stub server. *Done 2026-10-03: `bench/bench.py` (dnsperf runner: warmup, saturating run, fixed-rate runs at 25/50/75% of max; percentiles from dnsperf's latency histogram; server RSS/CPU from /proc; results JSON schema 1; `compare` regression gate at ±5%, using p99 at 50% load), `corpus.py` (seeded, byte-reproducible cache-hot and miss-heavy; blocked once `bench/lists/` exists), `stub_upstream.py` (fake authoritative upstream with optional delay), `make bench-smoke`/`bench-full`, CI job (fails on loss/errors only). First smoke (WSL, 8 threads, dnsperf on the same host, 4 workers): cache-hot 221k qps, p99 623 µs at 50% load; miss-heavy p50 447 µs through the Python stub; idle RSS 7.8 MiB, ready in 24 ms. Same-host numbers aren't release-gate numbers. Deferred: realistic-home (T2.x), resperf ramp, flamethrower DoT/DoH, cgroup memory.peak, netem, `bench/compare/` (T4.7), Pi 4 runner.*
- [x] **T0.4 Container build.** Multi-arch `FROM scratch` image, non-root, published on main as `edge`. *(OPS-001)* *AC:* image ≤ 15 MiB compressed; runs on arm64 under QEMU in CI. *Done 2026-10-03: `Dockerfile` cross-compiles static musl binaries on the build host with cargo-zigbuild (pinned and checksum-verified zig 0.15.2 / cargo-zigbuild 0.23.4) for amd64, arm64, and arm/v7; `FROM scratch` + binary + CA bundle + tzdata, `USER 65532:65532`, `/var/lib/telltale` volume. Release images: 2.57 / 2.44 / 2.37 MiB compressed; 3-platform fat-LTO build 11.4 min locally. `deploy/image/smoke.sh` (read-only rootfs, `--cap-drop ALL`, no-new-privileges, default :53 inside a bridge netns; UDP + TCP answers, /metrics, user, --version) passes for all three platforms, arm ones under QEMU. `deploy/image/size.py` gate. `image` workflow: build → size gate → per-platform smoke → push `edge` + `sha-<short>` to GHCR on main. mimalloc (`02` §3) added as the global allocator with `no_thp`: musl's allocator roughly halved cache-hit qps, and THP tripled idle RSS (ADR-015, Proposed). Musl idle RSS 6.4 MiB. Found: non-root + `cap_add NET_BIND_SERVICE` can't bind :53 with host networking (Docker has no ambient caps), so the `08` §3 compose example needs changing before T4.3 (ADR-015). Deferred: SBOM + signing (release only, `09` §4), PGO (P1).*

## M1 — Fast forwarding resolver (weeks 2–4)
- [x] **T1.1 telltale-proto hot path.** Header/question/OPT parse, name normalization + hash, response TTL-offset scan, ID/TTL patching, synthesis helpers (NXDOMAIN/NODATA/A/AAAA/EDE). *(DNS-005, DNS-013, DNS-019)* *AC:* proptest round-trips; fuzz target runs for 10 min clean; differential test vs hickory. *Done 2026-10-02: 2000-case proptests + hickory 0.26 differential both directions; 3 fuzz targets × 10 min, 0 crashes (69.9M execs). Micro-bench (x86 WSL): parse+hash 53 ns, blocked answer 56 ns, cache-hit patch 5 ns.*
- [x] **T1.2 UDP workers.** SO_REUSEPORT per worker, recvmmsg/sendmmsg, PKTINFO. *(DNS-001)* *AC:* answers `kdig`; multi-core scaling test shows ≥ 3.2× qps at 4 workers vs 1. *Done 2026-10-02: verified with `dig` (kdig not installed; equivalent). `examples/udp_scaling`: 3.58× at 4 workers with 20 µs/query synthetic work. With 2 µs/query the in-process generator saturates first (2.16×), so the authoritative number must come from the dnsperf harness (T0.3) on separate hardware. Non-Linux fallback compiles but is untested here.*
- [x] **T1.3 TCP listener.** Pipelining, limits, idle timeouts. *(DNS-001)* *Done 2026-10-02: RFC 7766 pipelining (reader/writer split, ready for out-of-order async replies), 10 s idle timeout, 64 in-flight per connection, 1024 connection cap (constants for now; config keys come with T1.10/OPS tuning). Per-thread response scratch keeps idle connections cheap.*
- [x] **T1.4 Cache.** Sharded S3-FIFO, wire storage, TTL clamps, negative caching, singleflight. *(DNS-006)* *AC:* zero-alloc test passes (NFR-002); hit ratio on the Zipf corpus ≥ LRU baseline. *Done 2026-10-02: 0 allocations over 100k hits + 100k blocked answers. S3-FIFO vs LRU (100k names, 1M Zipf requests): s=1.0 59.7%/50.5% (1% cache), 77.8%/73.4% (10%); s=0.8 31.4%/20.4%, 54.5%/46.6%. Full hit path 153 ns single-thread. Also includes serve-stale lookup (DNS-007) and the prefetch signal (DNS-008); the background refresh itself lands with upstreams. Not wired into `telltale run` until T1.5. Decision: negative answers without SOA are not cached (RFC 2308 §5); responses whose OPT isn't the last record are not cached.*
- [x] **T1.5 Upstreams v1.** UDP/TCP/DoT/DoH(h2) transports with pooling; strategies (failover, round_robin, weighted, fastest, parallel); health + breaker; bootstrap; loop detection. *(UPS-001, 005, 006, 008, 009)* *AC:* chaos test with toxiproxy: one upstream at 100% loss → no client-visible failures; p99 within 1.5× of the healthy baseline. *Progress 2026-10-02 — **T1.5a done**: UDP (+TCP fallback on TC) and TCP transports with anti-spoofing validation; all five strategies; health (EWMA, 50-sample window, breaker) and active checks; 2 s budget; hedged attempts (ADR-013); routes; pipeline wired with cache, singleflight, serve-stale, prefetch, SERVFAIL+EDE. Chaos AC met in-process (fake upstreams, one blackholed): 0 failures, p99 1.04–1.15× baseline across failover/round_robin/fastest/parallel; toxiproxy version pending Docker. Verified live against 1.1.1.1/9.9.9.9 with a dead member. **T1.5b done**: DoT and DoH (HTTP/2) over rustls (ring, Mozilla roots built in), pooled pipelined TCP/DoT connections with ID demux, h2 multiplexing, bootstrap resolution for hostnames (per-upstream servers or /etc/resolv.conf minus our listeners), loop tag (EDNS 65429) with drop + error log. Verified live: DoT 1.1.1.1, DoH dns.quad9.net and dns.google. Not yet: spki_pins, proxy, ECS, DoH3 (startup errors if configured).*
- [x] **T1.6 Presets catalog.** *(UPS-004)* *AC:* every Pi-hole preset present; each entry resolves `example.com` in an online smoke test (nightly, allowed to be flaky-tolerant). *Done 2026-10-02: 18 presets (all 8 Pi-hole presets + Cloudflare security/family, AdGuard x3, Mullvad x2, Control D free/custom, NextDNS) in `presets/upstreams.toml`; `telltale presets list|show`; online test: 58/58 reachable endpoints resolve (IPv6 skipped without a v6 route, DoQ and account-templated entries skipped); nightly workflow. The test caught one wrong entry (Control D DoT needs its hostname), fixed.*
- [x] **T1.7 Local records + conditional forwarding/routes.** *(DNS-010, DNS-017)* *Done 2026-10-02: `[[record]]` (A, AAAA, CNAME, PTR, TXT, MX, SRV, `*.` wildcards), hosts-file import, auto PTR, AA answers before cache, local CNAME chasing, NODATA; routes (DNS-017) landed with T1.5a. Verified with dig.*
- [x] **T1.8 Rate limiting, special names, allowed_networks.** *(DNS-014)* *Done 2026-10-02: token-bucket limiter (sharded, alloc-free for known clients, v6 per /64, REFUSED or drop), allowed_networks with safe defaults, special names (localhost, .invalid, Firefox canary, CHAOS, private PTR with route override) per ADR-014. Verified with dig. Pod/service CIDR detection deferred to T4.1.*
- [x] **T1.9 Metrics core.** Thread-local counters + HDR per path; `/metrics`; `/healthz` `/readyz` `/livez`. *(OBS-005 partial, OPS-006)* *Done 2026-10-02: per-thread cache-line-aligned slots with relaxed atomics (0 allocations, tested); Prometheus histograms 50 µs–5 s per path (HDR percentiles come with the M3 aggregator); 28 metric families incl. cache, upstream health, listeners, RSS; axum endpoints gated by allowed_networks; readiness flips on after binding and off before draining. Debug-build RSS 17.8 MB with a live DoT upstream.*
- [x] **T1.10 Graceful shutdown + hot reload.** *(OPS-007, OPS-009)* *Done 2026-10-02: SIGHUP validates then atomically swaps routing/policy (ArcSwap), applies listener changes bind-before-close, keeps the last good config on errors, warns for restart-only sections ([cache], [telemetry], [node]); verified 300 queries across 4 reloads with 0 failures. Shutdown: not-ready, TCP drain, ≤ 3 s upstream drain, UDP stop. Deferred: cache persistence on shutdown (DNS-009, P1), file-watch reload, config API reload (M3).*
- [x] **T1.11 Project site skeleton (GitHub Pages).** *(DOC-001, DOC-002, DOC-004, DOC-005)* Scheduled right after T1.5, when TelltaleDNS first resolves queries, so the quick start is real. Landing page (non-technical pitch + comparison visual), "Start here" quick start, Standards page driven by `site/data/standards.json` covering everything implemented so far, Pages deploy workflow, link/HTML checks. *AC:* deployed from `main`; Lighthouse ≥ 90 in all categories; every page has at least one diagram; Standards page lists every RFC referenced by a ticked task. *Done 2026-10-02: 4 pages (landing, start, how-it-works, standards with 12 diagrams), build.py (partials, standards render, roadmap coverage + link + page-weight checks), Pages workflow with Lighthouse CI (≥ 0.9 all categories). Deployment needs Pages enabled once (Settings → Pages → Source: GitHub Actions); Lighthouse scores come from that first workflow run.*
- **M1 gate:** `cache-hot` ≥ 150k qps on the 4-core reference box; idle RSS ≤ 20 MiB. *Status 2026-10-03: idle RSS **met** — release build 7.3 MiB (2 workers) / 9.1 MiB (8 workers), 13.6–14.4 MiB after 300 distinct lookups over DoT; release binary 4.4 MiB (x86-64 WSL2, dev machine, not the reference box). qps half pending the dnsperf harness (T0.3).*

## M2 — Filtering engine (weeks 4–6)
- [x] **T2.1 List fetcher** (ETag, caps, retries, stored sources). *(FLT-004)* *Done 2026-10-03: `[[list]]` (url / path / inline rules; kind, match, enabled, per-list refresh and size) and `[filter]` config with validation; `telltale_filter::fetch`: HTTP/1.1 over rustls with conditional GETs (ETag + Last-Modified), redirects (no https→http), streaming size cap, per-attempt timeout, retries with jittered backoff (5xx/408/429/network only), HTML/empty rejection, bounded concurrency, failing lists retried 5 min → 1 h; sources stored atomically as `lists/<name>.src.zst` + `.meta.json`; last good copy always kept; removed lists pruned; content-change `watch` signal for the compiler. Server runs it after listeners bind (role all/controller), reloads list changes on SIGHUP, exports 4 per-list gauges; `telltale lists fetch` CLI. Live: StevenBlack + HaGeZi Pro + OISD big (13 MB, 551k lines) in 9 s, stored as 4.2 MB; second run all 304/unchanged. Decisions in ADR-016 (Proposed). Deferred: gzip transfer and per-list headers/auth (P1), `POST /lists/refresh` (API, M3; `Fetcher::request_refresh` is ready), list stats beyond fetch state (T2.3/§6).*
- [x] **T2.2 Parsers** for all formats in `05 §2`, with golden fixtures. *(FLT-001)* *Done 2026-10-03: `telltale_filter::parse`: per-line detection of hosts (incl. zone IDs), plain, `*.`/`.` wildcards, AdBlock/AdGuard (`||`, `|`, `@@`, `name^`, `/regex/`; `$important`, `$badfilter`, `$client`, `$dnstype`, `$denyallow`, `$dnsrewrite`), and Pi-hole regex (`;querytype=`, `;invert`); IDNA → punycode normalization; regexes validated with `regex-syntax` (no backreferences/lookaround); per-list stats with invalid/unsupported samples. 13 unit tests, 7 `insta` golden snapshots over synthetic fixtures, `parse_list` fuzz target (4 min clean), `telltale lists check` CLI, nightly online test over real lists: StevenBlack, HaGeZi Pro (3 formats), OISD small/big, AdGuard DNS filter all parse with 0 invalid lines at 40–95 MB/s (x86). `rtype::from_name` moved into telltale-proto. Decisions in ADR-017 (Proposed), incl. synthetic fixtures instead of vendored GPL lists. Deferred: `$client` resolution and `$badfilter` matching (compiler, T2.3).*
- [x] **T2.3 Compiler:** external-sort merge → FST (subtree/exact), ListSetTable, regex meta set, modifier rules, `$badfilter`. *(FLT-002, FLT-003)* *AC:* 1.5M-domain fixture ≤ 12 B/domain; compile ≤ 8 s on Pi 4. *Done 2026-10-03: `telltale_filter::compile` (parallel parse by list size → arena external sort with spill runs → allocation-free k-way merge → subtree/exact/subdomains FSTs + interned 4-tier `ListSetTable`; modifier rules indexed by `modrules.fst`; regex set compile-checked with the matcher's engine settings; `$badfilter` across lists; manifest with BLAKE3 per blob) and `telltale_filter::snapshot` (verified loader, `domain_hits`, `modrules_for`). Server compiles at startup, on list changes, and on reload (debounced, skipped when unchanged, never empty, last 3 kept, failures keep the previous snapshot), at nice 10 on a dedicated thread; `[filter] compile_threads` (1) and `compile_memory` (128 MiB); `telltale lists compile`; `telltale_filter_*` and `telltale_list_entries` metrics. **AC size: met**, 8.68 B/name on the 1.2M-name synthetic fixture and 8.98 B/name on 2.6M real names (HaGeZi TIF + Pro + OISD big). **AC time on Pi 4: met with 2 threads**, measured on the owner's Pi 4 (2026-10-03): 1.5M names 8.6 s / 6.2 s / 3.7 s and 2M names 11.6 s / 6.4 s / 4.7 s on 1 / 2 / 3 threads. That needed FST shards per compile thread and chunked parsing of large lists, and the default `compile_threads = 0` (auto: half the cores, 1–4), which changes `05` §3.4's "default 1 on Pi" (ADR-018). Peak RSS 73 MiB for the 1.2M fixture. bench-smoke A/B vs previous commit: no measurable change (this machine's run-to-run spread is ±30%). Deferred: `$client` resolution to client IDs (with T2.5), mmap loading (T2.4 if needed).*
- [x] **T2.4 Matcher + precedence + overlay for manual rules.** *(FLT-003, ADR-003)* *AC:* precedence table tests; lookup p99 ≤ 1 µs (no regex) on x86. *Done 2026-10-03: `telltale_filter::matcher` (allocation-free `decide()` over wire-format names: domain rules through a query-time bucketed hash index built from the FSTs (ADR-020), with the FST walk as an instant-start fallback; modifier rules with `$dnstype`/`$client`/`$denyallow` predicates; regex sets incl. `;invert`, with a prefilter; four-tier precedence and FLT-013 attribution: exact > deepest suffix > regex > lowest list ID; `Overlay` of manual rules consulted at the same precedence). Server publishes each snapshot (walk first, then indexed) via ArcSwap; pipeline step 6 answers blocked names with NXDOMAIN + EDE 15 naming the list; `status="blocked"` and `telltale_filter_lookup_index_bytes` metrics. **AC met:** precedence-table tests (8, both lookup modes) plus a pipeline test; **lookup p99 0.93–0.94 µs** with 2.6M real names and no regex on native x86 (x86-64 laptop-class test host; p50 0.32 µs), 0.61 µs on the WSL laptop. Bench harness `blocked` corpus fixed (AdBlock syntax) and wired to load `bench/lists`. Deferred: groups/client masks (T2.5), block modes (T2.6), CNAME inspection (T2.6), overlay API (M3), dnsperf on a test host for end-to-end gates.*
- [x] **T2.5 Groups/clients:** identification chain incl. neighbor table, DoH path / SNI client IDs, EDNS MAC. *(FLT-005, FLT-006)* *Done 2026-10-03: `[[group]]` (lists, priority), `[[client]]` (IP / CIDR / MAC / `id:` keys, groups), `[clients]` (neighbor table, refresh, `trust_edns_mac_from`) with validation; `telltale_policy::ClientTable` identifies in spec order (client ID → EDNS MAC from trusted forwarders only → neighbor-table MAC → IP → most specific CIDR → default), allocation-free; rtnetlink neighbor dump (ARP + NDP) in `telltale-net` (unsafe in `sys.rs`, safe parser), refreshed only while some client is matched by MAC; per-client list masks (union of groups) precomputed per snapshot and per config; `$client` matches device name / client ID; routes get client groups; upstream resolution by selected view (fixes group routes). `telltale_neighbors` gauge. Tests: identification chain, group priority, pipeline group masks incl. MAC via neighbors, netlink parsing + live dump. Homelab A/B (8 interleaved runs, dnsperf in a container): p50 @50% load 34 µs both, qps 171k vs 164k (within noise). Decisions in ADR-021 (Proposed). Deferred: client IDs (with DoH/DoT listeners, T4.5), hostname matching (DHCP leases / rDNS), CIDR trie.*
- [x] **T2.6 Block modes + EDE + CNAME inspection + pause.** *(FLT-007, FLT-008, FLT-009)* *Done 2026-10-03: per-group `block_mode` (null_ip default, nxdomain, nodata, refused, custom_ip + `block_ips`), `block_ttl`, `ede` (15/17), `ede_text`; CNAME deep inspection on cache hits, upstream and stale answers (allocation-free records walk); global/per-group pause (`telltale_policy::Pause`, lock-free read, survives reloads) with `telltale_filter_paused_until_seconds`. Tests: every block mode incl. EDE 17 bytes, CNAME block on a cache hit, pause/resume/expiry. Live: null IP + EDE 15, NXDOMAIN + EDE 17 for a strict group, www.microsoft.com blocked via its akamaiedge.net CNAME (fresh and cached). Homelab A/B (8 rounds, alternating order): 170.4k vs 171.4k qps, p50 33 µs both. Decisions in ADR-022 (Proposed). Deferred: pause API (T3.4).*
- [x] **T2.7 Atomic swap under load.** *AC:* recompile during the `realistic-home` run → p99 regression ≤ 10%, zero errors. *Done 2026-10-03: `bench.py swap` (quiet vs swap windows in alternating order, list-content trigger via `SIGUSR1` or config reload via `SIGHUP`, liveness check per round, server log kept). **AC met** on a test host (x86-64 laptop CPU, 2.7M names from 6 real lists, 20k qps `realistic-home`, 10 rounds, default config): median p99 124 → 102 µs (within noise), p50 36 → 35.5 µs, 0 lost, 0 SERVFAIL, all 4M answers NOERROR. Getting there: `SCHED_IDLE` background threads, deferred frees of swapped-out state, list processing off the runtime, router reuse on reload, and one compile thread for recompiles under a serving snapshot (4 threads measured +143% p99). Fixed a crash: prefetch spawned without a runtime on UDP workers. `SIGUSR1` refreshes lists. Decisions in ADR-023 (Proposed). Deferred: the same measurement on the Pi 4 (not load-tested there: production Technitium).*
- [x] **T2.8 Explain engine.** *(FLT-013)* *Done 2026-10-03: `Matcher::matches` (every rule in every list that matches, each marked enabled for the client, in `decide`'s precedence order; `decide` now shares the passes through a generic sink, `matcher_bench` A/B unchanged), `telltale_filter::explain` (list + line + text per rule: domain rules located by re-parsing only the matched lists' stored sources, modifier/regex rules by their stored line; notes for changed or missing sources), and `Pipeline::explain` (client identity and groups, access/ANY/special/local stages, filter, pause, route incl. RFC 6303 private PTRs) as a serializable `Explanation`, the future `GET /api/v1/explain` and `explain_decision` body. `telltale explain NAME --client IP [--mac|--client-id] [-t TYPE] [--json]` runs it offline from the config and data directory. Tests: first enabled match = `decide` for every mask (both lookup modes); explain outcome agrees with the real pipeline across 8 names × 3 clients; line attribution incl. duplicates. Verified on 2.7M real names (e.g. `stevenblack:7102 0.0.0.0 ad.doubleclick.net` under an allowlist winner). Site: new "Blocking you can explain" section (also covers T2.1–T2.7, which hadn't updated the site). Decisions in ADR-024 (Proposed). Deferred: the API endpoint and MCP tool (T3.4/T3.x), schedule state (FLT-010, P1), manual-rule (overlay) line numbers until the overlay has a store (M3).*
- **M2 gate:** `blocked` corpus ≥ 150k qps; RSS with the bench lists ≤ 64 MiB. *Passed 2026-10-03 on a test host (x86-64 laptop CPU, 4 workers, dnsperf on the same host, static musl build of `15d34fd`), with the `09` §2 lists (HaGeZi Pro + OISD big + StevenBlack + 500 synthetic regexes): `blocked` **157,334 qps** (median of 154k / 157k / 169k, 0% loss); RSS **30.5 MiB** idle with the filter loaded and **35 MiB** under full load. Notes: these lists dedupe to 430k names, not `09` §2's "~1–1.5M"; steady RSS grows about 20 B per name, which projects about 36–45 MiB at 1M. The first run reported 110 MiB because `bench.py` sampled idle RSS 1 s after the index was ready, during the compile's transient peak; fixed to sample after 6 s. Compile peaks (106 MiB here, 297 MiB at 2.7M names) are ADR-025. Fixed-rate p99 at 50% of max was 0.6–1.7 ms on the shared host; the `00` §5 latency gate (≤ 250 µs) needs a dedicated load generator and is re-measured at M5.*

## M3 — Observability core + API + auth (weeks 6–9)
- [x] **T3.1 QueryEvent rings + aggregator + rollups + top-K + HDR.** *(OBS-001, 002, 004)* *AC:* drop counter is 0 at 100k qps sustained on x86 with default ring sizes. *Done 2026-10-03: `telltale_telemetry` `event` (QueryEvent / UpstreamEvent records with the full lowercased qname), `ring::Hub` (per-thread wait-free `rtrb` byte rings, registered on first use, per-ring emitted/dropped counters, wall clock derived from the query's `Instant`), the aggregator thread (25 ms drains) and `agg::Aggregates` (per-second 15 min and per-minute 48 h windows by status/qtype/rcode/proto/group/upstream; Space-Saving top-K over an indexed min-heap for domains, blocked, NXDOMAIN, clients, and per-client domains for the 64 most recent clients; HDR latency by path×proto, qtype, client (256 + other), upstream stage, and upstream; current + previous hour). The pipeline emits on every answer (sync, drops, deferred) with client, group, status, rcode, flags, timings, and FLT-013 rule attribution for blocks, allows, and CNAME blocks; `resolve_upstream` emits UpstreamEvents. `telltale_telemetry_{events,dropped}_total{ring}` metrics; `bench.py sustain`. **AC met** on the homelab: 99,991 qps sustained for 60 s, 6.0M events, **0 dropped**, RSS 41 MiB. A/B: p50/p99 at 50% load unchanged (34 / 751 µs), saturated cache-hot qps −2.4% / −3.7% in two A/Bs, within run-to-run spread. Default `ring_slots` 65,536 → 4,096, 2-digit HDR, byte records: ADR-026 (Proposed). Deferred to T3.2: interning, SQLite rollup persistence (`06` §3); `QueryEvent.upstream` (upstream analytics come from UpstreamEvents); stage timings for filter/cache (sub-µs, not worth two clock reads per query yet).*
- [x] **T3.2 Segment store:** writer, block index, bloom, dictionary-first search, retention, privacy levels; plus, moved from T3.1, qname/client interning and SQLite rollup persistence (`06` §3, ADR-026). *(OBS-003)* *AC:* 50M-row synthetic dataset search ≤ 2 s on Pi 4; format fuzzed. *Done 2026-10-03: `telltale-store::qlog`: hourly segments with self-describing blocks (dictionary deltas, per-section xxh3 + length table, status/RCODE masks, max latency, content-hash bloom) and a footer (block index + per-segment name filter); builder on the aggregator thread (interning = segment dictionary, privacy levels 0–3, part rotation) and a writer thread (bounded queue, drop + count, footers, retention by days and bytes); search (exact/suffix/substring/glob/regex names, client, status, qtype, rcode, upstream, group, client ref, min latency, time range) newest first with cursors, dictionary-first, column-at-a-time selection, sparse name resolution, and a 4-thread idle-priority pool. Server writes it by default (`[telemetry.qlog]`), `telltale_qlog_*` metrics, `telltale qlog search` CLI (text or JSON lines). **AC met on the Pi 4:** 50M rows / 30 days / 686 MiB, every query in the suite ≤ 1.92 s with 4 idle-priority threads beside production Technitium (exact rare name 0.21 s, rare substring 1.78 s, full column scan 0.75 s, latency ≥ 1 s 1.92 s); **format fuzzed** (`qlog_segment`, 3 modes, ~670k runs). 13.9–14.4 B/row. Decisions in ADR-027 (Proposed): name filter, section table, xxh3, content-hash blooms, 4 idle threads instead of 1 on a Pi. Deferred: SQLite rollup persistence to T3.3 (its first consumer); multi-node merge and per-group privacy (cluster/config work).*
- [x] **T3.3 Full Prometheus metric set + Grafana dashboard** *(after T3.9 at the owner's request, ADR-028);* plus SQLite rollup persistence (`06` §3, moved from T3.2 per ADR-027). *(OBS-005, OBS-011)* *Done 2026-10-04: aggregator-built series (stage and per-upstream latency histograms, blocks by group/list, opt-in capped per-client counts), `upstream_requests_total{outcome}`, `cache_prefetch_total`, `ratelimited_total`, `node` on build info; hot path unchanged except one prefetch increment (bench-smoke 191,533 qps cache-hot, 0% loss). Grafana dashboard (29 panels) generated by `deploy/grafana/build.py` with a CI staleness check; every query uses exported metrics. `rollups.db`: minute (7 d), hour (400 d), day (forever), hourly top-100 lists and latency summaries; once-a-minute flusher with late-event re-flush and hourly retention; API `step=hour|day`, minute merge, long summaries; UI 7/30-day ranges. Verified end to end (metrics, flush, hour/day reads, restart persistence). Details and label deviations in ADR-031 (Proposed). Deferred: `node` label on every series (M5), DNSSEC/cluster series (with their features), past-hour top-K via the API (analytics pages).*
- [x] **T3.4 API skeleton:** axum, OpenAPI generation, problem+json, pagination, scope param (local-only for now). *(API-001, API-002)* *Done 2026-10-04: `telltale-api` (routes, camelCase types with units in names, RFC 9457 problem+json with a `code` enum and hints, RFC 3339/relative time parameters, `scope` (cluster|node:local), cursor pagination on the query log) behind a `Backend` trait implemented by the binary over the pipeline, aggregates, query log, list status, and router; OpenAPI 3.1 via utoipa 6 at `/api/v1/openapi.json`, committed as `docs/api/openapi.json` with a drift test and an AGT-001 test (every operation has a summary and description). Read-only first slice: `system/info`, `stats/{summary,timeseries,top,latency}`, `queries`, `explain`, `lists`, `groups`, `clients`, `upstreams`. New `[api]` listener (default `0.0.0.0:8053`) also serving `/metrics` and probes, restricted to `allowed_networks` until T3.5. Verified end to end against a running server (every endpoint, paging, errors). Decisions in ADR-028 (Proposed), incl. the owner-requested order: T3.5 → T3.9 next, T3.3 after. Deferred: mutations with dry-run/idempotency (T3.5+, AGT-002/003), MCP tools (AGT-006), `If-Match` config versions, federated scopes (M5).*
- [x] **T3.5 Auth:** local users + Argon2id + sessions + HTTP Basic (opt-in) + tokens + TOTP + RBAC; setup-token first run. *(API-003)* *Done 2026-10-04: `telltale-store::state` (SQLite `state.db`: users, recovery codes, sessions, tokens; migrations) and `telltale-api::auth` (Argon2id m=19 MiB/t=2/p=1, session cookie + CSRF header, `tt_` bearer tokens hashed with BLAKE3 and scope-capped, opt-in Basic refused over plain HTTP and cached 60 s, TOTP with replay protection and recovery codes, per-user and per-address lockout, viewer/operator/admin middleware). 13 new endpoints (`/auth/*`, `/tokens`, `/users`) in the OpenAPI document; all data endpoints and `/metrics` on the API port now need a sign-in. First admin via setup token (logged, `<data_dir>/setup-token`, `telltale auth setup-token`) or `TELLTALE_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_*` from a secret (`telltale auth hash-password`). `[auth]` config section. Tests: crypto vectors (RFC 6238, RFC 4648), service rules, an HTTP story (setup, cookie, CSRF, tokens, roles, last admin, sign-out), setup-token persistence; verified end to end against a running server. Details in ADR-029 (Proposed). Deferred: TLS on the API listener (cookie `Secure` is conditional until then; done 2026-10-18 as `[api] tls`, ADR-149), OIDC (API-004), group-scoped tokens (with the `family` role), audit log of auth events.*

Tests: fake-provider flow tests (RS256, nonce, PKCE, replay, other-browser, role change, logout URL, break-glass) and the AC: Playwright against real Keycloak 26.4 and Authentik 2025.10.1 (`ui/tests/oidc/`, CI `oidc` job), verified locally. `rsa` advisory RUSTSEC-2023-0071 ignored with justification (verification only). A/B bench with two subscribers: no throughput loss or packet loss, p99 within noise, small median cost at 50% load (ADR-032, Proposed). Deferred: WebSocket transport, `node` filter (M5).*

ADR-040 (Proposed). Deferred: name suggestions from DHCP leases/rDNS/mDNS (no source yet; M7/M8), the MCP tool `plan_rename_client` (T6.6).*

- [x] **T3.13 Device anomaly engine v1 (proposed, owner request 2026-10-03).** In the aggregator, after T3.1: per-client rate anomaly, per-(client, eTLD+1) volume anomaly with TTL-violation flag, behavior drift against the learned domain set, beaconing detection; findings API + anomalies view + alert-rule source + `find_anomalies` MCP tool. *(OBS-013, OBS-009)* *AC:* replaying a recorded 14-day fixture yields byte-identical findings across runs and architectures; injected scenarios (TV telemetry burst, plug contacting 30 new domains, 5-minute beacon with ±3 s jitter) are each found with evidence and no other findings on the clean baseline; per-client state ≤ 4 KiB; aggregator CPU overhead ≤ 5% at 50k qps. *Done 2026-10-04: `telltale_telemetry::anomaly` (rate spike per hour of day, domain volume per tracked device/eTLD+1 pair, drift in new registrable domains per day, beaconing with a learned-periodic exemption; EWMA + mean absolute deviation, event time, ordered state, FNV-1a, f32 IEEE math; explained spikes reported once); an aggregator sink with hourly + shutdown persistence (`anomaly.json`); `[telemetry.anomaly]` (enabled, learning_days, sensitivity, max_clients, ignore_domains); `GET /api/v1/analytics/anomalies`, the Anomalies page with evidence, `telltale_anomalies_total{kind}` + chart alert, help topic. **AC:** a deterministic 14-day, six-device fixture: the clean baseline yields no findings; the injected TV burst (4,100 q/h vs 119 ± 30), plug drift (30 new domains vs 0 ± 2), and camera beacon (300 s ± 2 s for 4 h) are each found with evidence and nothing else; findings byte-identical to a committed golden file (checked on x86-64 and arm64 in CI) and across a save/reload; per-device state ≤ 4 KiB (tested, also for a device with 5,000 domains); the fixture (~140k events) replays in 0.08 s in a release build, well under 1 µs per event, so under 5% of a core at 50k qps. ADR-043 (Proposed). Deferred: TTL-violation flag (needs answer TTLs in events), `find_anomalies` (T6.6), mute/acknowledge, per-group sensitivity.*
- [x] **T3.14 Network groups + groups as an analytics dimension (proposed, owner request 2026-10-05; ADR-050).** `[[group]] networks` (IPs and CIDRs, most specific wins; explicit client entries win; naming a device keeps its network group); a group color; the dashboard's "Traffic by group" chart and group filter; group filters and chips in the query log; a Groups page with each group's networks, active devices, queries, block rate, and top domains and blocked names; `group=` on stats, top, and query APIs; `telltale_group_queries_total{group,status}`; group named in anomaly findings. *(FLT-005, FLT-006, OBS-004, API-005)* *AC:* with the owner's seven VLANs as groups, a query from 10.20.2.40 is attributed to IOT in the log, charts, and metrics; naming that device keeps it in IOT; overlapping networks at the same prefix are rejected; groups without lists don't change any block decision (golden explain output unchanged); every chart can be filtered by group. *Done 2026-10-05: `[[group]] networks` + `color`; clients without `groups` inherit their network group (policy: one allocation-free scan, A/B shows no change in bench-smoke); per-group masks; aggregator per-group top domains/blocked/clients per hour, per-group blocked counts, `telltale_group_queries_total{group,status}`; API `group=` on top and queries (400 naming the groups for unknown names), `byGroup`/`blockedByGroup` in timeseries, group networks/color/24 h stats/devices in `/groups`; UI: Groups page cards, dashboard Traffic-by-group chart + group selector, query-log group chips + filter; Playwright `flt_005` test. Deferred: rollup-backed (> 48 h) ranges have no per-group split; the live tail ignores the group filter; router VLAN import (M8).*

## M4 — Kubernetes + Pi deployment (weeks 8–10, overlaps M3)
- [x] **T4.1 Helm chart** (allInOne, scaled, daemonSet shapes; Services with ETP=Local; PDB; HPA; probes; NetworkPolicy; ServiceMonitor; cert-manager; existingSecret everywhere). *(OPS-002, OPS-003)* *AC:* `ct install` on kind + k3d arm64; DNS answers via the LB; client IP preserved in the query log in an e2e test. *Done 2026-10-04 (allInOne): `deploy/helm/telltale`: StatefulSet with volume, one UDP+TCP DNS LoadBalancer with ETP=Local (NOTES warns on Cluster), API Service + optional Ingress, metrics Service, startup/readiness/liveness probes, hardened security context, `hostNetwork` option (root with only NET_BIND_SERVICE), NetworkPolicy, ServiceMonitor, PrometheusRule (5 alerts), Grafana dashboard ConfigMap, bootstrap-admin Secret and generic Secret mounts, values schema. `deploy/helm/e2e.sh` + CI on kind (amd64) and k3d (arm64): ct lint/install, DNS via a MetalLB LoadBalancer over UDP and TCP, the sender's address in the query log; also verified on the homelab (real LAN clients through Cilium LB IPAM). ADR-035 (Proposed). Deferred to M5: `scaled` and `daemonSet` shapes with HPA and PDB; DoT/DoH Certificate to T4.5.*
- [x] **T4.2 Masked-client-IP detector + NOTES warning.** *(OPS-003)* *Done 2026-10-04: 10-minute per-client windows in the aggregator (`telltale_telemetry::recent`, bounded, off the query path); `masking` in the binary: > 90% of ≥ 100 queries from ≤ 3 infrastructure addresses (loopback, default gateways from `/proc/net/*route`, `TELLTALE_NODE_IPS` from the chart's downward API, `[clients] infrastructure`); `clientIpsMasked` in `/system/info`, a UI banner linking to the new "Seeing real client IPs" docs, `telltale_client_ips_masked` + a `TelltaleDNSClientIPsMasked` chart alert, one log line per change; NOTES mention the runtime check. Tests: window roll/expiry and cap, assessment rules (threshold, 3-source cap, IPv6, too few queries), `/proc` route parsing, metrics + API end to end, Playwright banner. ADR-037 (Proposed). Also fixed: renamed the duplicate task IDs (anomaly engine T3.13, backup/restore T6.7).*
- [x] **T4.3 Pi Compose bundle + docs; native binary + systemd + install.sh + self-update.** *(OPS-004)* *Done 2026-10-04: `deploy/compose/` (host network, read-only, root with only NET_BIND_SERVICE and `./data`, `telltale health` healthcheck, starter `telltale.toml`); `deploy/systemd/` (sandboxed unit, `install.sh`: arch detection, minisign signature + SHA-256 verification, system user, config kept, atomic swap, systemd-resolved stub handling, readiness wait); release assets from the image build (`edge` prerelease on main, releases + versioned image tags on `v*`), `SHA256SUMS` signed with minisign; `telltale self-update` (signature, hash compare, test-run, atomic swap, `.old` backup, `--check`, `--restart`, refused in containers). Tests: self-update against signed fixtures (good, wrong key, swapped binary, missing signature); CI job `native` runs both e2e scripts (Compose verified on the homelab too, which caught the root-without-DAC_OVERRIDE data-dir bug). Docs: "Raspberry Pi and Linux"; site install panels. ADR-038 (Proposed). Needs the `MINISIGN_SECRET_KEY` repository secret before binaries are published. Deferred: cluster min-version check in self-update (T5.11).*
- [x] **T4.4 PROXY protocol v2 on TCP/DoT/DoH.** *(DNS-020)* *Done 2026-10-04 (with T4.5): `telltale_net::proxy` (v2 parser, IPv4/IPv6/LOCAL/UNSPEC, TLVs skipped, 1 KiB cap, v1 and bare connections refused) read before TLS on `tcp`, `dot`, and `doh` listeners with `proxy_protocol = true`; `telltale_proxy_protocol_rejected_total`; Helm `encrypted.proxyProtocol`. Tests: parser (good and malformed headers), TCP listener uses the header address and drops connections without one, DoH over HTTP/1.1 through a v6 header. ADR-039 (Proposed).*
- [x] **T4.5 DoT + DoH (h2) listeners with cert hot-reload.** *(DNS-002, DNS-003)* *Done 2026-10-04: DoT = the TCP listener behind a TLS acceptor (ALPN `dot`, same pipelining and limits); DoH = hyper HTTP/2 + HTTP/1.1 (GET `?dns=`, POST `application/dns-message`, `/dns-query/<client-id>`, `Cache-Control: max-age` from the answer, 4xx without touching the resolver); `CertStore` (PEM chain + key, reloaded within 10 s of a change, last good kept); client IDs from SNI under the certificate's wildcard names (FLT-006, closes the T2.5 deferral); `dot`/`doh` transport labels in metrics, query log, and aggregates (rollup format v2, v1 rows still read); DoH/TLS metrics; Helm `encrypted.*` (ports on the DNS LoadBalancer, Secret or cert-manager Certificate, NetworkPolicy). Tests: cert load/reload/mismatch, SNI client IDs, DoT end to end with renewal, DoH GET/POST/client IDs/every error status, base64url; verified with `dig +tls`, `+https`, and `+https-get` against the binary. Also fixed the UDP test race that failed CI (counters read before the worker counted its reply). ADR-039 (Proposed). Deferred: DoQ/DoH3 (M7), DDR (P1).*
- [x] **T4.6 Site: deployment guides + technical track.** *(DOC-002, DOC-003)* Pi / Docker Compose / Helm install guides with topology diagrams (including the hybrid Pi + k8s cluster), architecture and pipeline pages, configuration reference generated from `telltale config schema`. *AC:* a new user follows the Pi guide to a working resolver without leaving the page; config reference is regenerated in CI. *Done 2026-10-04: `install.html` (Pi with Compose step by step from OS to dashboard, native systemd, Kubernetes, hybrid topology, router setup, troubleshooting; four topology diagrams), `config.html` (every key with type, default, and meaning, rendered by `site/build.py` from `docs/config-schema.json`; a telltale-config test fails when that file differs from `telltale config schema`, so CI keeps it current; the site redeploys when it changes); nav links; stale "next milestone"/"preview" text updated; Lighthouse now checks all six pages with 3 runs each (the single-run performance score flaked at 0.79). Architecture and pipeline: the existing How it works page. Deferred: screenshots and the performance page (T4.7).*
- [x] **T4.7 Site: dashboards and performance.** *(DOC-002, DOC-003)* UI screenshots once T3.9 lands; performance page fed by `bench/compare` results (harness numbers only). *AC:* screenshots regenerated by the Playwright suite; numbers link to the raw harness JSON. *Done 2026-10-04: `performance.html` renders the newest `site/data/bench/*-full.json` (x86-64 test host, commit c67ccba: cache-hot peak 172,580 qps and p50 37 µs / p99 1.6 ms at 50% load; blocked 154,381 qps; miss-heavy p50 103 µs; zero loss) with host, server, and load-generator details and a link to the raw JSON published beside it; screenshots written to `site/assets/shots/` (JPEG) by the Playwright `2-shots` spec (dashboard on the 15-minute range, "Why?" on a blocked query, node name `home-dns`). Deferred: comparison against Pi-hole and Technitium (`bench/compare`) for the release report.*

## M5 — Clustering and HA (weeks 10–14)
- [x] **T5.1 PKI + join tokens + mTLS channel** (protobuf, persistent streams, reconnect). *(CLU-001)* *Done 2026-10-04: `telltale-cluster` (Ed25519 CA and node certificates via rcgen, `tt_join_` tokens with CA pin, prost frames over HTTP/2 mTLS, Hello + 5 s heartbeats, dial-out with jittered backoff); `telltale cluster init|token create|join|status`; `[cluster] listen`; peers in `/api/v1/system/info` and `telltale_cluster_peers`; ADR-044. Deferred: cert renewal/CA rotation (T5.4), K8s auto-join (T5.10), cluster page and qps/lag (T5.9).*
- [x] **T5.2 Change log + signed snapshots + content-addressed blob sync.** *(CLU-003)* *AC:* a list change ships only the changed blobs; propagation ≤ 5 s p95 across simulated WAN (50 ms RTT). *Done 2026-10-04: signed whole-version manifests (Ed25519 with the cluster CA) naming BLAKE3 blobs (shared config JSON + filter snapshot); the primary republishes on change; replicas fetch missing blobs over mTLS, verify, install the snapshot, merge node-local sections (`telltale_config::shared`), and hot-reload; synced replicas stop fetching lists and refuse API writes (409). Tests: only the changed shard is fetched; p95 190 ms over a 50 ms RTT proxy; a foreign-signed manifest is ignored. ADR-047. Deferred: the per-change op log with authors and orphans (T5.4), write forwarding (T5.7), node.toml allow-list (T5.5). Also delivers T5.3's cold start from disk (a replica serves `applied.json` in ~30 ms with the primary down).*
- [x] **T5.3 Data-plane independence + snapshot persistence + cold start from disk.** *(CLU-004)* *AC:* primary killed → replicas keep 100% answer rate; replica restart without primary → serving in ≤ 500 ms. *Done 2026-10-05 (built with T5.2): replicas persist the applied manifest, config blob, and snapshot and serve them at startup without contacting the primary. `deploy/cluster/e2e.sh` (CI `native` job) runs a primary and a replica as processes: 1,061/1,061 answers while the primary was SIGKILLed mid-load; a cold start with the primary down answers 26 ms after launch; edits propagate in < 0.5 s.*
- [x] **T5.4 Epochs, leases, manual promote, witness + quorum election, fencing, conflicts UI.** *(CLU-005)* *T5.4b done 2026-10-05 (ADR-056):*
  - *The election protocol (`telltale_cluster::election`): one fsynced vote per epoch, voter-granted 15 s leases renewed every 5 s, the primary's lease timed from the request with a 2 s margin, pre-vote, and up-to-date candidates only.*
  - *`telltale-sim` (`tests/sim.rs`): 10k randomized partition/crash/drift schedules in CI. Never two writers; orphans always surfaced; worst recovery 24.75 s. Mutation-checked.*
  - *Runtime: `cluster set-failover auto|manual` in signed manifests, a vote-only `cluster witness` process (`join --witness`), a voter mesh, `elect.ask` RPC bound to the mTLS identity, lease-gated publishing and API writes (503), restart resume, and only key holders as candidates.*
  - *Cluster page Failover line and check; metrics for primary, epoch, failover_auto and lease_held.*
  - *`deploy/cluster/failover-e2e.sh`: kill the primary and the replica is elected in about 14 s, 100 % of DNS answered; the old primary follows.*
  - *CA rotation moved to T5.4c.* Split (ADR-051): **T5.4a** manual mode: explicit roles and epochs, signed member registry, signing key on eligible nodes, eligible-node mesh, `cluster promote` (CLI + UI), fencing, orphaned-version conflicts, ADR-048 config authority; **T5.4b** witness and quorum election with leases, CA rotation, and the `telltale-sim` partition checker (the AC below). *T5.4a done 2026-10-05: explicit roles and epochs (migrated from "holds the CA"), signed member registry in every manifest (backfilled from Hello), the CA key shared with eligible nodes over mTLS, dialing to the highest-epoch primary then registry URLs, live role switching (publisher/follower), `cluster promote` (CLI, and API/UI for admins, audited), fencing on any higher epoch, orphaned versions under Conflicts (API + UI), config authority (`--config-authority`, `set-authority`, `[cluster] config_source`, emergency primaries, `409 gitops_managed`). Proven in `deploy/cluster/e2e.sh` step 5: promote the replica while the primary is down, the old primary returns, steps down, follows, and records its unseen edit as a conflict.* Includes ADR-048 config authority: `gitops`/`api` authority in the signed cluster state, per-node `config_source`, authority-matching eligibility, emergency (publish-nothing) primaries, manifest provenance, and `cluster set-authority`. *AC:* `telltale-sim` 10k randomized partition schedules → never two writers in one epoch; orphaned writes always surfaced.
- [x] **T5.5 Node-local overrides.** *(CLU-006)* *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-052): `[[record]] node_only = true` stays on its node (not shared by a primary, kept by a replica); shared settings in a replica's own file are ignored with a warning at load and a failing `node_settings` Cluster check, instead of a startup refusal. Tests: `clu_006_node_only_records_and_ignored_settings`, and the cluster e2e checks a node-only record survives replication and never reaches the primary.*
- [x] **T5.6 Federated reads** (stats merge, top-K merge, HDR merge, k-way query-log merge, partial results). *(CLU-002, OBS-012)* *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-053):*
  - *Reads are RPCs over the existing cluster streams, with a 2 s deadline per peer.*
  - *Timeseries, summary, top lists (also per group) and latency merge across every node. The query log is a k-way merge with a per-node cursor, and each row is tagged with its node.*
  - *`missingNodes` is shown on the dashboard and in the query log. `scope=node:local` reads one node.*
  - *Covered by unit tests and a cluster e2e step (the primary sees the replica's queries).*
  - *Deferred: exact HDR latency merge (latency percentiles are count-weighted), `node:<id>`/`site:<name>` scopes, a federated live tail.*
- [x] **T5.7 Write forwarding to primary with identity propagation.** *(CLU-002)* *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-054):*
  - *Device, local-name and forwarded-domain writes (and dry runs) on a replica go to the primary as an RPC over the cluster stream.*
  - *The primary trusts the entry node's mTLS identity, stores `<user> via <site>`, and audits it as `cluster`.*
  - *The primary's answer and error codes come back unchanged, and a replica's ETag is the primary's version.*
  - *With the primary unreachable, writes get 503 and nothing changes.*
  - *Cluster e2e: a record PUT on the replica resolves on both nodes, the primary's audit shows `bob via r`, and a write with the primary down gets 503.*
  - *Deferred: user/token replication (ADR-045), re-applying conflicts from the UI, and making the "api"/"file" source labels on replicas reflect the primary's state.*
- [x] **T5.4c CA rotation.** *(CLU-001, CLU-005)* Rotate the cluster CA (new key, cross-signed transition, node certificate renewal over the channel, old CA retired), re-share the key with eligible nodes, and renew node certificates before expiry. Split from T5.4b (ADR-056). *Node certificate renewal done 2026-10-05 (ADR-057): nodes renew at under 30 days left, keeping the same key. CA holders self-issue; others use the `cert.renew` RPC, signed only for the asking peer with names from the registry. The new certificate is verified against the CA, and TLS settings are rebuilt for new connections. Tested over the channel (RPC path, refusal for another node, forged-CA rejection). Still open: CA rotation itself.*
  - *CA rotation done 2026-10-05 (ADR-066): `telltale cluster rotate-ca [--status]` on the primary.*
  - *Two trusted CAs in `ca.crt` instead of cross-signing; the bundle is carried in signed manifests (witnesses follow it too), and adopted only if it keeps a CA the node trusts. The next key is re-shared with eligible nodes; an early key is held until its CA is trusted.*
  - *Phases `trust` → `switch` → `finish`, each gated on every non-ephemeral member, from new heartbeat fields (`trust_fp`, `issuer_fp`). After the switch, nodes renew from the new CA within a minute.*
  - *Fixed on the way: configuration fetches used start-up TLS settings; witnesses never renewed certificates; key files were briefly readable by others.*
  - *Tests: unit tests, and `deploy/cluster/rotate-e2e.sh` in CI (primary + eligible replica + witness under DNS load, no query missed, replication and reconnection afterwards).*
- [x] **T5.8 Telemetry ship mode + store-and-forward.** *(CLU-007)* *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-055):*
  - *`[telemetry] mode = "ship"` with `[telemetry.ship] to / buffer_bytes / interval_secs`.*
  - *The writer closes parts on a timer, and the local log is the bounded buffer.*
  - *The shipper sends closed parts in chunks over the cluster channel. The receiver checks the hash and files them under `qlog-nodes/<id>/`, and the sender deletes its copy after confirmation.*
  - *The receiver's search includes shipped logs, labelled by node, with a per-source cursor.*
  - *The peer RPC handler is installed even without the API. New metrics for shipped, pending, errors and received.*
  - *Tests: chunked receive with checksum and path checks, closed-part selection, rotation; a cluster e2e where the replica in ship mode delivers to the primary and the shipped row is found on the primary.*
  - *Deferred: rollup shipping and `both` mode (ADR-055).*
- [x] **T5.9 Cluster health page, metrics, alerts.** *(CLU-008)* *Done 2026-10-05: a Cluster page in the main navigation (standalone explainer; summary; six checks with fixes; per-node role/link/RTT, config version + lag + behind-time, serving: ready/q/s/SERVFAIL %/upstream p90, version, uptime, cert expiry; event timeline; 5 s refresh) and `GET /api/v1/cluster`. Heartbeats gained echo-based RTT and serving stats (added proto fields, N-1 compatible), an immediate first heartbeat, and an event log. Metrics per peer (up, RTT, lag) and per node (seq, behind seconds, sync error, cert expiry); four PrometheusRule alerts (plus a CI lint of the monitoring templates, which caught an unquoted alert summary from T4.2). Deferred: clock-skew check and quorum/lease state (T5.4), cluster-wide federated serving stats (T5.6), the MCP tool (T6.6).* Owner request 2026-10-04: a **Cluster** item in the main navigation with deep technical detail for judging whether the cluster is active, healthy, and serving traffic:
  - **Topology:** cluster name/ID, epoch, election mode, which node is primary, nodes by site, eligible vs replica vs ephemeral.
  - **Per node:** up/down with last heartbeat age, link direction and RTT, version and protocol (N/N-1 warnings), uptime, certificate expiry (renewal due), advertise URLs, and whether its DNS is serving: QPS, answer rate, SERVFAIL rate, p90 latency, readiness.
  - **Sync:** applied config version vs the primary's, lag in changes and seconds, last sync time, propagation time p50/p95 over the last hour, snapshot/filter version per node, blobs pending, and orphaned changes (conflicts).
  - **Events:** a timeline of joins, disconnects/reconnects, promotions, epoch changes, and sync failures, with the reason.
  - **Diagnosis:** a "Cluster check" summary (every node heard from, all in sync, quorum/lease state, clocks within tolerance) with plain-language fixes; the same data at `GET /api/v1/cluster` and as an MCP tool.
  - **Metrics/alerts:** `telltale_cluster_peers`, peer RTT, sync lag (changes, seconds), cert expiry, epoch; alerts for a peer down > 1 min, lag > 30 s, a cert expiring in < 14 days, and a node not serving DNS.
  *AC:* the page and API agree with `telltale cluster status` on every node; a killed peer shows down within 15 s; a partition shows lag and recovers; Playwright covers the page.
- [x] **T5.10 Ephemeral k8s members + site grouping; Helm values for hybrid.** *(CLU-009)* *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-058):*
  - *Helm `mode: scaled`: a controller StatefulSet that creates the cluster on first start (`[cluster.init]`), resolver pods that join with a generated bootstrap Secret, a cluster Service, and `cluster.advertise` for nodes outside Kubernetes.*
  - *The bootstrap secret is verified by HMAC proof of possession before the CA is pinned.*
  - *Ephemeral members (never eligible or voters) expire after `ephemeral_ttl_secs`. Pods are ready only once synced, and their query logs ship to the controller.*
  - *The Cluster page groups pods by site.*
  - *Fixed: a follower missed a manifest that arrived before it subscribed.*
  - *`deploy/helm/scaled-e2e.sh` (CI, kind, this build): pods join and sync, DNS through a pod, an outside node joins through the cluster port, and a deleted pod expires.*
  - *Promote both ways is covered by the process-level failover suites.* *AC:* e2e: Pi-like container (outside kind) + kind cluster form one cluster; UI on either shows both; promote works both ways.
- [x] **T5.11 Version compatibility N/N-1 + rolling upgrade test.** *(CLU-010)* *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-059):*
  - *Peers within one protocol version interoperate. Manifests carry a config `schema`.*
  - *A replica that can't read a newer schema keeps its last version and says to upgrade.*
  - *Per-node protocol and a `versions` check on the Cluster page.*

- [x] **T5.12 Git as the cluster's config source (proposed, owner request 2026-10-04; ADR-049).** *Done 2026-10-05:*
  - *`telltale-git`: Git protocol v2 over HTTPS with no git binary. Packfiles with deltas, forward-only history, SSH-signed commits; tested against real `git upload-pack` and verified live against github.com.*
  - *`[cluster.git]` on the primary: poll and webhook (`/api/v1/hooks/git`, HMAC), then validate-then-publish with commit provenance in the signed manifest.*
  - *The source is pinned in replicas. Git-managed means `gitops` authority. Nothing is published before the first good commit.*
  - *The Cluster page shows the source, the commit, and each node's commit. Alerts and metrics.*
  - *`deploy/cluster/git-e2e.sh` (CI): a pushed commit is served by both nodes with the same SHA; an invalid commit is never published and is flagged; a force-push is refused; with the repository down both keep serving; the promoted replica keeps following the repository.*
  - *Deferred (ADR-049 notes): direct-pull fallback, GPG, "propose change", the chart reading the same file.* `[cluster.config] source = "git"` (repo, ref, path, credentials file, poll + webhook) on the primary; validate-then-publish with commit provenance (SHA, author, time) in the signed manifest; any Git-capable node eligible as primary under a `gitops` authority (ADR-048); guardrails: pinned source, optional signed-commits allow-list, forward-only history, bounded fetch, audit; optional direct-pull fallback for long partitions; `telltale/shared.toml` layout shared with the Helm chart; UI shows the commit per node and "propose change" instead of writes. *(CLU-003, CLU-005, OPS-005)* *AC:* a commit pushed to the repo is served by every node within poll + 5 s, all reporting the same SHA; an invalid commit is never published and raises an alert; with `require_signed`, an unsigned commit is refused; a force-push is refused; with GitHub unreachable, every node keeps serving the last commit; killing the primary and promoting the Pi keeps following the repo.

## M6 — Protocol depth + migration (weeks 13–16)
- [x] **T6.1 DNSSEC validation + NTAs + EDE.** *(DNS-011)* *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-060):*
  - *`[dnssec] mode = off|validate|permissive`, off by default until soaked. Negative trust anchors, including routes with `dnssec_nta`.*
  - *hickory-net's validating handle runs over our upstream groups (DO and CD upstream, per-group validation cache).*
  - *AD for secure answers to clients that ask. Bogus answers become SERVFAIL with EDE 6, never stale or cached. CD bypasses validation. DNSSEC records are stripped for clients without DO.*
  - *`telltale_dnssec_validation_total{result}`.*
  - *Fixed for all queries: a truncated UDP answer's TCP retry now gets its own timeout.*
  - *`deploy/dnssec-e2e.sh` (CI, real DNS tree): secure, insecure, bogus, CD, and signed denials.*
  - *Deferred: RFC 5011, RFC 8198, per-reason EDE codes, query-event field, Settings UI.*
- [x] **T6.2 Serve-stale, prefetch, cache persistence.** *(DNS-007, 008, 009)* *Done 2026-10-05:*
  - *Serve-stale (RFC 8767, EDE 3) and prefetch were already in the cache and pipeline.*
  - *New: `[cache] persist`. The cache is dumped to `<data_dir>/cache.bin` on shutdown (owner-only) and reloaded on start.*
  - *Entries are re-keyed for the new process's hash seed, aged by the downtime, and only those still servable are kept. A dump from different upstreams or routes (a config fingerprint) is refused. The file is removed once loaded.*
  - *Tests: dump and reload across seeds, expiry, foreign fingerprint, and garbage input. A live restart's first query was a cache hit.*
  - *bench-smoke: cache-hot 139.7k qps, inside the 112–166k range of earlier runs; the hit path is untouched.*
- [x] **T6.3 Pi-hole importer (v5 + v6 Teleporter).** *(API-007)* *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-061):*
  - *`telltale import pihole PATH [-o FILE]` reads a v6 zip, a v5 tar.gz, a directory, or a `gravity.db` (zip/tar.gz readers on `miniz_oxide`; SQLite via `rusqlite`, both already in the tree).*
  - *It maps upstreams, conditional forwarding (domain plus reverse zones, with an NTA), local DNS and CNAME records, adlists (v6 allowlists too), exact and regex domains (per set of groups), groups (exact list sets; disabled groups get none), clients, DHCP reservations as named devices, DNSSEC, the rate limit, and the blocking mode.*
  - *The report names every unmapped setting: v6 `### CHANGED` keys, v5 keys. Values are never copied.*
  - *Tests: unit tests on both formats; `deploy/pihole-import-e2e.sh` in CI exports from the official v6 (FTL 6.7.1) and v5 (5.18.3) images and checks the served answers.*
  - *Found by the e2e and fixed: the first blocking snapshot waited for every list's first fetch, so a dead adlist URL (retrying with backoff) delayed all blocking. The fetcher now tells the compiler 10 s after the first update at the latest, without waiting for the rest of the round (`flt_004_a_slow_list_does_not_delay_the_others`).*
  - *Open: a UI/API import (with T6.7 restore).*
- [x] **T6.4 Technitium importer.** Zones (as local names and routes), records, forwarders/conditional forwarders, block and allow lists, with a plain-language report of what was imported and what has no equivalent. *(API-007, API-011)* *Partial 2026-10-04: `telltale import zone FILE [--origin] [-o]` (RFC 1035 zone files incl. Technitium exports: $ORIGIN/$TTL, parentheses, relative names, A/AAAA/CNAME/PTR/TXT/MX/SRV; SOA/NS and unsupported types reported; output validated as config); used to move the owner's `home.example` zone. ADR-041 (Proposed). Open: forwarders and conditional forwarders, block/allow lists, reading from the Technitium API directly.*
  - *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-062): `telltale import technitium URL` reads a running server through its API (token from `TECHNITIUM_TOKEN` or `--token-file`). Technitium's backup files are a private binary format.*
  - *It maps forwarders (UDP/TCP/TLS/HTTPS, pinned addresses, concurrent → parallel), primary zones' records, forwarder zones → routes (NTA unless validated), block-list URLs (`!` = allow), blocked/allowed zones, and Advanced Blocking groups (catch-all network → `default`, other networks → `networks`, shared URL lists, regexes).*
  - *Also: the bypass list → a no-list group, the blocking answer, DHCP reservations → devices, DNSSEC, the per-address rate limit, and the saved cache.*
  - *The report covers secondary/stub zones, QUIC forwarders, listener-based groups, per-list answers, the DHCP server, encrypted listeners, ACLs, TSIG, and other apps.*
  - *Tests: unit tests, and `deploy/technitium-import-e2e.sh` in CI against `technitium/dns-server:15.6.0` (it found and fixed a one-forwarder `parallel` bug).*
- [x] **T6.7 Backup/restore archive.** *(API-007)* *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-063):*
  - *`telltale backup create [--include-qlog] [-o]`, `show`, and `restore [--data-dir] [--config-dir] [--force]`.*
  - *A `.ttbk` is zstd-compressed tar with a BLAKE3 manifest, owner-only. It holds the config files, `state.db` (copied live with `VACUUM INTO`, sessions removed), rollups, anomaly baselines, and optionally the query log.*
  - *The cluster identity and CA key are excluded. Restore checks everything in a staging directory before moving files, refuses to overwrite without `--force`, and keeps the data directory's owner.*
  - *Tests: unit tests, and `deploy/backup-e2e.sh` in CI (back up a running node, restore it as another, sign in, API-made name answers).*
  - *Also `GET /api/v1/backup` (admin, audited) and a Settings → System button, checked in the e2e.*
  - *Open: passphrase encryption; an opt-in cluster key; restore through the API; an MCP tool with T6.6.*
- [x] **T6.5 Agent-ready API hardening:** LLM-grade OpenAPI descriptions, dry-run + impact estimates on all mutations, idempotency keys, agent tokens/scopes, audit attribution, kill switch. *(AGT-001..005, AGT-009)* *AC:* every mutation has a dry-run test; OpenAPI lint (spectral) passes the description rules. *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-064):*
  - *Agent tokens (`kind: agent`) with AGT-004's scopes (read-only by default), an optional group, a rate limit, and expiry.*
  - *Deny-by-default route→scope map; a required `X-Telltale-Reason` on changes; attribution `agent:<token> (owner) via <client>`.*
  - *Per-token rate limits (429 + `Retry-After`) and the `[agents] enabled` kill switch (cluster-wide).*
  - *`impact` and `recentQueries` on every config dry run, plus a promote dry run (`PromotePlan`). Idempotency keys were already on every config write (T3.10).*
  - *Settings → API tokens has an agent form, with a help topic.*
  - *`.spectral.yaml` runs in CI; every response is now described.*
  - *Tests: unit and API tests, and `deploy/agent-e2e.sh` in CI (a dry run for each mutation changes nothing; attribution; kill switch via reload).*
- [x] **T6.6 MCP server (read-only tools) at `/mcp` + `telltale mcp --stdio`.** *(AGT-006, AGT-008 bearer part)* *AC:* MCP conformance test green; scope/privacy authorization tests; a scripted agent completes the "why is the TV slow" golden transcript. *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-065):*
  - *`POST /mcp` (Streamable HTTP, JSON-RPC, protocol 2025-06-18) on every node, with 11 read-only tools that call the REST routes in-process with the caller's credentials. Scopes, group restriction, rate limits, privacy level, and the kill switch apply unchanged.*
  - *`telltale mcp --stdio` relays to a node with a token.*
  - *The catalog is snapshotted in `docs/api/mcp-tools.json`. Results are capped at 32 KiB / 200 rows. The client name from `initialize` is passed on for attribution.*
  - *Tests: unit and API tests, and `deploy/mcp-e2e.sh` in CI with the official MCP SDK over HTTP and stdio, playing the "TV slow" story and checking that scopes are enforced inside tools.*
  - *Deferred: `test_resolution`, write tools (plan/apply), and OAuth for MCP.*
- [x] **T6.8 UI visual refresh (proposed, owner request 2026-10-04).** Bring the dashboard and pages to a more modern look inspired by the owner's reference design (patterns noted in `docs/design/visual-refresh.md`: dark sectioned sidebar with pill active state and badges, borderless cards with soft shadows and accent-bar headers, KPI tiles with deltas and sparklines, donuts and radial rings, rounded bars): keep the current color tokens, replace hard rectangular "classic admin" edges with softer shapes (larger radii, cards with depth, pill controls, rounded charts and stat tiles), and tune spacing and type scale. Svelte stays the framework; design tokens change in one place so light/dark both follow. *(API-005, DOC-006)* *AC:* before/after screenshots for every page regenerated by the shots suite; WCAG AA contrast kept in both themes; bundle stays ≤ 400 KiB gz; Playwright suite green; site screenshots updated.
  - *Done 2026-10-05, in two passes (notes in `docs/design/visual-refresh.md`). The owner approved pass 1 from before/after screenshots. Pass 2's screenshots went to the owner on 2026-10-05, so tweaks may follow.*
  - *Pass 1: the shell, the shared tokens and surfaces, and KPI tiles with changes and sparklines.*
  - *Pass 2: smooth and rounded charts with a dot legend; a status donut; progress rings; inline share bars; sidebar count badges.*
  - *AA contrast: `-strong` text tokens for tinted chips; light-theme badges that were already below AA are fixed too.*
  - *Bundle is 93 KiB gzipped. The shots suite covers every page and a phone view. The Playwright and OIDC suites pass.*
  - *Left out: breadcrumbs (every page is one level deep), an alerts bell (the badges cover it), and icons in table first columns.*
- [x] **T6.9 Version visibility and update status (proposed, owner request 2026-10-04; ADR-046).** CI-stamped build identity (version, commit, date, channel, target, install type) in `--version`, system info, the UI footer and an Updates panel, `telltale_build_info`, and the cluster Hello; a signed `releases.json` per channel; daily update check (`[updates] check`); `telltale_update_available`; per-install-type update instructions. *(OPS-004, CLU-010)* *AC:* amd64, arm64 and armv7 builds of one commit report the same version and commit; an older edge build reports "update available" against the published index on every arch; a tampered index is rejected; with the check off, nothing leaves the node.
  - *Done 2026-10-05 (ADR-046 addendum): `build.rs` stamps version, commit, date, and channel from CI. One identity for all architectures and the image, checked in `image.yml`. A local build says `dev`.*
  - *It shows in `--version`, system info (`build`, `update`), `telltale_build_info{version,commit,channel,target}`, the cluster Hello, the UI sidebar, and Settings → System → Version and updates, with per-install-type steps (`TELLTALE_INSTALL` set by the image, chart, and unit).*
  - *A signed `releases.json` is published with every release. Nodes check it daily (`[updates] check`, `index_url`), and only a verified index is trusted. `telltale_update_available` reports the result.*
  - *Tests: signature (good, tampered, wrong key) with throwaway-key fixtures; version comparison within a channel; per-install instructions.*
  - *Open: `self-update` reading the index instead of `SHA256SUMS`.*
