- [x] **T6.11 Host resources on the Cluster page (proposed, owner request 2026-10-05).** For monitoring and triage, each node reports the machine it runs on. *(CLU-008, OBS-005; a new requirement, proposed CLU-012, is to be added)*
  - **Memory:** host total, available, and swap. In a container, also its cgroup limit, its usage, and OOM kills. Telltale's own RSS.
  - **CPU:** core count, load average (1/5/15 min), and utilisation. In a container, the cgroup quota and throttled time.
  - **Disk:** free and total space on the data directory's filesystem, the query-log and snapshot sizes, and the write rate (SD-card wear).
  - **System:** host uptime, OS, kernel, and architecture; temperature where the board exposes it (Pi `thermal_zone`); clock offset against the other nodes.
  - **Process:** open file descriptors against the limit, threads, and restarts.
  - **Where it shows:**
    - per-node rows and small trend sparklines on the Cluster page; a 1-hour average and a peak beside the current value;
    - `GET /api/v1/cluster` and an MCP read tool;
    - `telltale_host_*` and `telltale_process_*` metrics;
    - alert rules for low disk space, memory pressure (cgroup near its limit, OOM kills), and high temperature.
  - **How it's collected:** read every 15 s, off the DNS path, straight from `/proc`, `/sys` and cgroup v1/v2 files (no new dependencies; this works in the `FROM scratch` image). Only the latest sample and a small history travel in heartbeats as new optional protobuf fields, which N−1 nodes ignore (CLU-010). A collector that can't read a source reports that source as "unavailable" (macOS dev builds, restricted containers) and never fails the node (CLU-004).
  - **AC:**
    - Pi, homelab pod, and a kind replica each show correct values. Memory and CPU match `free` and `/proc/loadavg` within 5%. A pod shows its cgroup limit.
    - Filling a test filesystem to under 10% free raises the alert.
    - A DNS benchmark with collection on stays within budget (`make bench-smoke`).
    - Docs and help panels are updated.
  - *Built 2026-10-05, not yet ticked.*
    - **Done:**
      - the collector in `crates/telltale/src/host.rs`: `/proc`, `/sys`, cgroup v2/v1, and `statvfs` through `telltale_net::filesystem_space`;
      - `HostStats` in heartbeats (tag 14, boxed, ignored by N−1), with a per-peer one-hour history and clock offset;
      - `HostReport` on `GET /api/v1/cluster` (per node, and for a standalone node), plus the `host_resources` check;
      - `telltale_host_*`, `telltale_cgroup_*` and `telltale_data_*` metrics;
      - Helm alerts, tested with `deploy/helm/alerts-test.sh` (promtool, run in CI);
      - Machines cards on the Cluster page;
      - help topic, docs, and the MCP `cluster_status` description.
    - **Checks:** workspace tests, clippy, the UI suite and bench-smoke all pass.
    - **Measured cost:** about 0.6 MiB idle RSS (22.9 vs 22.3 MiB, five runs each, bench-fast build on the dev laptop).
    - **Separately:** idle RSS without lists is now above the 20 MiB 1.0 gate on that build. That predates this task and should be revisited before the gate.
    - **Live check (rollout of 9d2cec8, 2026-10-05):**
      - Pi: memory total identical and available within 0.1% of `free`; temperature, OS, and data disk reported.
      - Homelab k3s pod: memory total identical and available within 0.06%; load averages identical to `/proc/loadavg`; its 512 MiB cgroup limit shown; OS name absent (the minimal image has no `/etc/os-release`), as designed.
    - **Notes:**
      - The low-disk alert is proven with `promtool` on synthetic series (`deploy/helm/alerts-test.sh`), not by filling a real filesystem.
      - A kind replica uses the same container code path as the homelab pod and wasn't checked separately.
    - **Deferred:** process restart counts (uptime shows restarts) and Grafana dashboard panels.
- [x] **T6.12 Quick rules: per-device and per-group allow/block, optionally expiring (proposed, owner request 2026-10-05).** For everyday cases:
  - unblock a game's server for one person's phone for two hours;
  - block a site for the kids' devices until tomorrow.

  *(FLT-005, FLT-006, FLT-013, AGT-004; a proposed ADR covers precedence and expiry)*
  - **A rule:**
    - allow or block a domain and its subdomains;
    - for chosen devices, groups, or everyone;
    - optional expiry (a duration or a time) and a note;
    - who made it and when.
  - **Storage:** rules are stored like other UI-managed entries (ADR-040) and replicated as shared configuration in a cluster.
  - **Matching:** applied through the matcher's manual-rules overlay, so a new rule takes effect immediately without recompiling the lists. Precedence: device rule > group rule > lists.
  - **Expiry:** enforced on the server when the rule expires; the audit log records adds, removals, and expiries.
  - **Surfaces:**
    - the query log ("Allow for this device: 1 hour / today / always", "Block for this device / group");
    - a device's page ("Block a site…");
    - a Rules page (active rules with a countdown, remove);
    - "Why?" and explain name the rule ("allowed by a quick rule for Mom's phone, expires 21:30");
    - the API and an agent scope, `config:write:rules`, so agents can do it with a reason header and impact estimate.
  - **Docs set honest limits:**
    - DNS sees whole sites (domains), not pages;
    - an app uses many domains (bundles come with FLT-012);
    - a rule follows the device only as well as the device is recognised (DHCP reservation; "private Wi-Fi address" off);
    - devices keep cached answers for a few minutes;
    - mobile data, VPNs, or hard-coded DNS bypass it unless the router blocks outside DNS.
  - **Not parental controls:** no screen time, no content categories.
  - *AC:*
    - a device-scoped allow beats a list block for that device only, and other devices stay blocked;
    - a group-scoped block applies to every device in the group;
    - an expiring rule stops applying within 5 s of its expiry with no restart, and the audit log shows it;
    - in a cluster, a rule made on the replica's UI applies on every node;
    - adding a rule doesn't trigger a list recompile;
    - `make bench-smoke` stays within budget with 1,000 rules;
    - the Playwright suite covers "allow for 1 hour" from the query log.
  - *Built 2026-10-05, not yet ticked.*
    - **Done:**
      - `[[rule]]` in the config, validated, plus the RFC 3339 parser;
      - `telltale_policy::QuickRules` (device > group > everyone, longer domain, then allow; allocation-free suffix probes);
      - the pipeline decides before the lists and in CNAME inspection; pause applies;
      - `RuleKind::Quick` in query events, with a stable 16-bit reference per rule;
      - explain names the rule;
      - `GET /api/v1/rules`, `PUT` and `DELETE /api/v1/rules/{id}` (dry run, If-Match, idempotency, audit, impact);
      - the `config:write:rules` agent scope and cluster write forwarding;
      - the expiry sweep (`rule.expire` in the audit log);
      - a Quick rules page and "Make a quick rule" in the "Why?" drawer;
      - help topic, docs, and ADR-067.
    - **Verified:**
      - unit tests (policy, pipeline, explain, config, API scopes);
      - Playwright: allow for 1 hour from "Why?", then remove; expiry and audit; bad input refused;
      - the cluster e2e: a rule made on the replica applies on both nodes and is removed from both.
    - **Performance (2026-10-05):**
      - `make bench-smoke` with 0 and 1,000 non-matching rules, alternating over four rounds: peak cache-hot throughput is within run-to-run noise (158–249k qps either way); "@50%" latencies are confounded by the different peaks.
      - Micro-measurement of a decision (`flt_005_quick_cost`): 4 ns with no rules, 120 ns with 1,000 rules using SipHash keys, 52 ns after switching to xxh3 keys with a pass-through hasher (hits confirmed byte for byte).
- [x] **T6.13 Cache tools: inspect and flush, across the cluster (proposed, owner request 2026-10-05).** The endpoints `spec/07` §1 already plans (`GET /cache/stats`, `POST /cache/flush` with `{name?, subtree?}`) and the `flush_cache` agent operation (`spec/13` §3, scope `ops:cache`). *(DNS-006, API-005, AGT-004, CLU-002)*
  - **Lookup:** what the cache holds for a name: answer, TTL left, stale or prefetched, DNSSEC status, and which upstream answered.
  - **Flush:** one name, a subtree, or everything. By default it goes to every node over the cluster channel; an option limits it to one node. Every flush is audit-logged.
  - **UI, with no new sidebar page:**
    - the query log's "Why?" and the Explain page show the cache state for that name, with "Flush this name";
    - a Cache card under Settings → System shows hit rate, entries, memory, evictions, stale answers served, and prefetches, plus a lookup box and the flush actions.
  - **Help text says plainly:**
    - flushing doesn't clear devices' own caches;
    - blocked answers are never cached, so unblocking needs no flush;
    - to change what a name resolves to, use Names on my network.
  - *AC:*
    - a flushed name is fetched from the upstream on the next query, on every node;
    - a subtree flush removes the name and its subdomains only;
    - viewers can look but not flush;
    - an agent token without `ops:cache` is refused;
    - flushing while the cache is under load causes no SERVFAIL and no measurable p99 change;
    - docs and help panels are updated.
  - *Done 2026-10-05: `Cache::inspect` (every cached variant of a name: type, DO/CD, rcode, answers, validated, TTL left or stale for, age, size, hits; read-only, no hit counted) and `flush_all_counted`; `GET /api/v1/cache/stats`, `GET /api/v1/cache/lookup?name=` (viewer, agents `analytics:read`), `POST /api/v1/cache/flush` `{name?, subtree?, node?}` (operator, agents `ops:cache`, audit `cache.flush`). In a cluster the federated backend fans each one out over the cluster channel (`api.read`), labels rows by node, lists unreachable nodes with the error, and `node` limits a flush to one. UI: a Cache card in Settings → System (per-node stats, lookup, flush name/subtree, flush everything with a confirmation, node picker) and a compact one in the query log's "Why?" drawer; help topic `cache-tools`; docs/running.md "Cache tools". Tests: cache inspect and counted flush, scope mapping, path count, Playwright (lookup through a stub upstream, flush, viewer 403). Deferred: which upstream answered and a prefetched flag (not stored per entry; would add bytes to every entry), the Explain page panel (the Why? drawer has it), the `flush_cache` MCP tool (MCP is read-only until plan/apply, `spec/13` §3.2), and a flush-under-load p99 measurement (a lookup or flush walks one shard at a time under its lock, like the existing name flush; to measure in the T6.14 scaled run).*
- [x] **T6.14 Multi-pod awareness and a live topology on the Cluster page (proposed, owner request 2026-10-05).** Running several pods should be safe, visible, and understandable at a glance. *(CLU-008, CLU-009, OPS-002)*
  - **Safety: one process per data directory.** On start a node takes an exclusive lock on its data directory. A second process pointing at the same directory refuses to start with a clear message. This guards the real risk of scaling a single-volume Deployment: shared identity, `state.db`, and query-log files across replicas.
  - **What each node reports** (new optional heartbeat fields, N−1 safe):
    - the Kubernetes node name (downward API `NODE_NAME`) and pod name;
    - cache hit rate and entries;
    - process start time (restarts).
  - The Cluster page computes each node's share of the cluster's queries.
  - **A live topology diagram** (SVG, no new dependencies, refreshed with the page every 5 s):
    - sites as boxes, with the controller or primary and the replicas;
    - resolver pods as chips coloured by health, grouped by Kubernetes node; more than six fold into "+N";
    - the primary, witnesses, and emergency primaries marked;
    - cluster links labelled with round-trip time: dashed and red when down, in amber when that node's config is behind;
    - each node's share of queries as line thickness, so uneven load balancing shows;
    - selecting a node or pod jumps to its row and its Machines card;
    - a standalone node shows itself and how to form a cluster.
  - **Per-pod views:**
    - load balance (queries share), with a check when one pod takes far more than its share;
    - per-pod cache hit rate, explaining that each pod has its own cache;
    - pod churn (joins and expiries) on the timeline.
  - **Docs:** a "Scaling in Kubernetes" section covering:
    - `mode: scaled` (controller plus resolver pods) versus `allInOne`;
    - why not to raise `replicas` on a single-volume Deployment;
    - per-pod caches and rate limits;
    - what a single k3s node does and doesn't protect against.
  - **Homelab:** move the live homelab from its single Deployment to the scaled shape (a controller plus 2–3 resolver pods) and tune from real data. The owner gave the go on 2026-10-05. The change is in gitops-repo, and the Pi stays a replica.
  - *AC:*
    - a second process on the same data directory exits with a clear error, and the first keeps serving;
    - the topology matches `GET /api/v1/cluster` for 1 node, 2 nodes, and 1 controller with 3 pods, and updates within 10 s when a pod is added or removed;
    - the kind scaled e2e checks node names and per-pod shares;
    - the homelab runs scaled for 24 h with no SERVFAIL increase and every pod in sync;
    - docs, help, and site updated.
  - *Soak passed:*the primary's cluster-wide query log shows 0 SERVFAIL in the 24 h after the move (about 1 in several thousand queries in the 24 h before), and every resolver pod applied the same configuration version (seq 124) after the day's rollouts.
- [x] **T6.15 The Cache page (owner request 2026-10-05).** The T6.13 card grows into its own page under System, worth it once each Kubernetes pod has its own cache. *(DNS-006, DNS-009, OBS-003, API-005, CLU-008)*
  - Per node: hit rate (last hour and since start), entries and memory against the budget, the last hour's lookups, prefetches, stale answers served, and evictions, the warm start, and the `[cache]` settings in effect.
  - Charts of hit rate and lookups per node over the last hour.
  - What each cache holds by kind and its top entries by hits, size, or nearest expiry; lookup and flush below.

Cluster rows are labelled by pod name for Kubernetes pods (they share a site), which also fixes the T6.13 node picker. UI: the Cache page (menu: System), Settings links to it, help topic `cache-page`; docs "Cache tools". Tests: cache top/makeup, history points, scope, Playwright (page, top entry, sort, lookup from a name, 400 on a bad sort, Settings link). Deferred: settings edits from the page (they stay in `[cache]`), per-pod history on the Cluster page.*
- [x] **T6.16 The dashboard survives restarts (owner report 2026-10-05).** After every rollout, traffic by group and the top lists started over while the other charts kept their history. *(OBS-004, OBS-003)*
  - *Cause:* the rollups stored every count except the per-group ones, and the current hour's top lists lived only in memory.
  - *Done 2026-10-05:* rollup buckets carry groups by name (a tagged section after the fixed columns, so the format version stays 2 and older builds still read the rows; merged by name into hours and days). At start, the current and previous hour's top lists and latency are replayed from this node's query log in the background (`Aggregates::replay_hours`), skipping the time windows, Prometheus counters, and masked-client windows; the writer doesn't rewrite the hour that closed before the start. Only with a local query log at privacy level 0. Tests: rollup round trip, compatibility, damaged section, roll-up by name; replay picks this node's rows from the two hours before the start only. Deferred: minutes saved before this release keep no group breakdown.

## M7 — v1.x (post-1.0, priority order)
MCP write tools with plan/apply + approval inbox, OAuth via OIDC, resources/prompts (AGT-007, 008, 010, 011) → DoQ + DoH3 listeners and upstreams (DNS-004, UPS-002) → schedules, safe search, services (FLT-010..012) → analytics suite + alerts (OBS-009, 010) → recursive resolver (DNS-012, UPS-012) → socket/exec upstream plugins + proxies (UPS-010, 011) → OTLP + dnstap + sinks (OBS-006, 007) → DHCP (OPS-008) → ECS/DNS64/local zones/rewrites/IP filter (DNS-015, 016, 018, FLT-014, 015) → DNSCrypt (UPS-003) → CLI (API-008).

Owner decision 2026-10-06: finish M6, then M7, before a v1.0 release.

- [x] **T7.1 Agent write tools with plan/apply, and an approval inbox.** *(AGT-007, AGT-004, AGT-005, FLT-009)*
  - Pause and resume blocking over the API (`spec/07`: `POST /blocking/pause`, `/blocking/resume`; globally or per group; on every node), with a UI control. Agents: `ops:pause`, at most 60 minutes.
  - Plans: a write tool computes the change with the REST dry run and stores a plan (`planId`, the diff, the impact, the config version, the reason, who asked, 10-minute expiry). `apply_plan` replays the write with `If-Match` on that version (a changed config makes the plan stale) and the plan ID as the idempotency key; `discard_plan`, `list_plans`.
  - Write tools over the mutations that exist: `plan_block_domain` / `plan_allow_domain` (quick rules, optionally for devices or groups, with expiry), `plan_rename_client` / `plan_assign_client`, `plan_set_local_name` / `plan_remove_local_name`, `plan_forward_domain`. Immediate, audited: `flush_cache`, `pause_blocking`, `resume_blocking`.
  - Approval (optional, `[agents] require_approval`): a plan needs an operator's approval before `apply_plan` succeeds. UI: "Pending agent changes" (approve, reject, see the diff and impact); REST: `GET /plans`, `POST /plans/{id}/approve|reject`.
  - Deferred until their write APIs exist: `plan_add_list`, `plan_update_group`, `plan_update_upstreams`, `plan_set_schedule` (lists, groups, and upstreams come from files or Git today).
  - *AC:* an agent with `config:write:rules` plans and applies a block, and the query is blocked on every node; a plan made before another change fails as stale; with approval required, `apply_plan` is refused until an operator approves in the UI; every apply is audited with the agent, its owner, and the reason; MCP tool annotations mark side effects.
  - *Done 2026-10-06:* plans in `telltale-api/src/plans.rs` (ADR-070, Proposed), `GET /api/v1/plans`, `POST /api/v1/plans/{id}/approve|reject`; MCP tools `plan_block_domain`, `plan_allow_domain`, `plan_rename_client`, `plan_assign_client`, `plan_set_local_name`, `plan_remove_local_name`, `plan_forward_domain`, and (after T7.5) `plan_add_list`, `plan_update_group`, `plan_update_upstreams`; `apply_plan`, `discard_plan`, `list_plans`; immediate `flush_cache`, `pause_blocking`, `resume_blocking`; `[agents] require_approval`; the Agent changes page and header pill. The AC runs in `deploy/mcp-e2e.sh` with the official MCP SDK (plan, refused before approval, approve, apply, blocked in DNS; stale; rejected; audit). *Deferred:* `plan_set_schedule` (needs FLT-010); alert destinations for pending plans (OBS-010).
- [x] **T7.5 Edit upstreams, lists, and groups from the UI and API (owner report 2026-10-06).** A standalone node installed with `install.sh` can't change its upstreams or lists without editing the file and reloading. *(API-002, API-010, FLT-001, UPS-001, AGT-002, AGT-003)*
  - Managed entries in `state.db` like devices and local names (ADR-040): `PUT/DELETE /api/v1/upstreams/{name}`, `/api/v1/upstream-groups/{name}`, `/api/v1/lists/{name}`, `/api/v1/groups/{name}`, with dry run, `If-Match`, idempotency, audit. Unlike devices and local names, an API entry may override or hide a file entry of the same name (ADR-069, Proposed); the files are never rewritten. `GET /api/v1/config/entries` lists each entry with its source.

- [x] **T7.6 An interactive `install.sh` (owner request 2026-10-06).** A novice accepts every default in one keypress; an advanced user steps through the options, each with its default shown and Enter (or `skip`) to keep it. *(OPS-001, DOC-001)*

  - First choice: "accept all defaults" or "walk me through it".
  - Steps: upstreams (the built-in presets: encrypted or plain, which providers); starting blocklists (none, recommended, or pick from common ones); the listen address; freeing port 53 from systemd-resolved; the first admin (create now, or a setup token); query-log retention and privacy level; release or edge builds.

CI: `deploy/systemd/guided-e2e.sh` (15 answer sets through `config check`, Enter = the starter, no questions without a TTY) and a guided install with an admin on real systemd in `deploy/systemd/e2e.sh`.

## M8 — v2 (stretch)
Agent analytics DSL `vqlog` (AGT-012), WASM upstream plugins, io_uring, beaconing detection, cache-warm hints (CLU-011), mDNS client naming, router integrations (UniFi/OPNsense lease import).

Beaconing detection shipped in T3.13 (the anomaly engine). io_uring was measured against the batched `recvmmsg`/`sendmmsg` path and not adopted (ADR-083, Proposed: no measurable gain). WASM upstream plugins were measured and deferred (ADR-084, Proposed: wasmtime would add 3.3 MiB compressed, wasmi 0.6 MiB; the process plugins from T7.16 cover the use case). **M8 is complete.**

- [x] **T8.6 The newer features in the UI.** *(AGT-012, API-010, FLT-014, FLT-015, DNS-016, DNS-018)* An Analyze page for vqlog; devices found by DHCP, routers, and mDNS on Clients, with their names suggested when naming a device (the suggestion T3.10 deferred); answer filtering, rewrites, and DNS64 on group cards and in the group editor; zones on "Names on my network".

- [x] **T8.2 Router integrations.** *(API-010 naming sources)* `[[router]]`: UniFi (API key or local user) and OPNsense (API key and secret) DHCP clients name devices.
  - *AC:* a UniFi OS login (cookie) or API key reads the client list (alias over host name); OPNsense's leases from whichever DHCP server it runs; unnamed devices take those names (the query log, the API); the leases API shows them with `source = router`; a router that's down keeps its last list and is retried; config errors for missing credentials.
  - *Done 2026-10-06:* `crates/telltale/src/routers.rs` (ADR-080, Proposed), `Client::insecure` and `Client::with_ca_file` in the fetch client, the router lease view and naming fallback, `source` on leases; config and validation; unit tests; `deploy/routers-e2e.sh` in CI (fake UniFi OS and OPNsense APIs); docs.
  - *Upstream sync review fixes (2026-11-17; ADR-166, ADR-942):* an offline UniFi client (remembered, not connected) is not "seen", gives no address binding to discovery, publishes no DNS name, isn't counted in a network's devices, and isn't in `/dhcp/leases`; the configured clients (`rest/user`) name offline devices for `offline_days` > 0 as the history does; the offline pass follows `[[router]]` order. The e2e saves and reopens the window. Tests: `t8_2_offline_lease_is_not_an_observation`, `t8_2_offline_clients_publish_no_name`, `t8_2_offline_name_follows_configuration_order`, `t8_2_offline_*` (routers), and the `t8_2 offline clients days` Playwright test.
  - *Done 2026-11-17 (owner decision "Show, marked offline", ADR-942):* `GET /api/v1/dhcp/leases` lists the offline clients with `"offline": true` (connected and reserved rows `false`; the field is always present); they still name no DNS name (`dnsName` absent), give no discovery binding and aren't counted in a network's devices. The Clients page's Discovered list and the Networks drawer's device list show an "offline" badge; help topic `offline-leases`; Playwright `t8_2 offline leases show the marker` (mocked leases); `deploy/routers-e2e.sh` asserts the offline row is listed marked offline with no DNS name. No MCP tool lists leases, and `telltale ctl` prints none, so neither changed. *Open:* no last-seen time is shown: a `DhcpLease` carries only the lease end (`expiresUnixSeconds`, 0 for an offline client), and the router's history timestamp isn't kept on the lease.
  - *Image gate (measured 2026-11-17, review MED 5):* `docker buildx build --platform <p> --output type=oci` then `deploy/image/size.py --oci` on this commit: linux/amd64 13.74 MiB, linux/arm64 13.02 MiB, linux/arm/v7 13.50 MiB, all within the 25 MiB gate (ADR-916). The reference line is the last measured amd64 figure on the base line (13.02 MiB, T28.4, a VM build); that is not a same-commit measurement, so the exact delta is approximate, roughly +0.7 MiB on amd64. The Dockerfile's CA bundle drop is safe: no fork TLS client reads the system store (`webpki-roots` only, checked by grep in this sync).
  - *Open (LOW, deferred):* `telltale-snmp` isn't among ADR-170's `opt-level = "s"` crates (a benchmarked follow-up, as ADR-170 says).

  - *AC:* the documented forms parse and answer exactly (checked against known rows); mistakes are 400s with hints (unknown stages, fields, keys, groups, statuses); the cost estimate bounds the matches and a dry run doesn't scan; queries over the limit are refused, and long scans stop and say so; it needs `querylog:read` and group-restricted agents can't use it; the MCP tool works from the official SDK client.

## M10 — The v1.0 gate (owner request 2026-10-07)
Every `00 §5` gate and the other v1.0 gate items, measured and closed. Status: `docs/v1-gate.md`.

- [x] **T10.1 Gate report.** *(NFR-001, OBS-002)* `bench/gate.py` (server and dnsperf on separate cores; idle RSS, cache-hit max and p99 at 50 %, telemetry loss at 75 %, blocked p99 and RSS with 1M names + 100k cache entries, cold start with the snapshot, 2M compile and recompile, image size), `docs/v1-gate.md` (every gate with its latest number, how it was measured, and the evidence from CI and earlier tasks). *Done 2026-10-07:* pass: cache-hit throughput (170.6k qps on two physical cores), image size, telemetry loss, availability with the primary down, failover, query-log search. Fail: idle RSS 25.2 MiB, RSS with 1M names 130.4 MiB, cold start 565 ms, Pi recompile 11.6 s. Unproven: p99 latency (same-host client), Pi throughput, cross-site propagation. The live Pi uses 82 MiB with 2.7M names: the "about 60 MB" claim (README summary, site, v0.1.0 notes) and the image sizes ("~9 MiB", "about 3 MiB") were out of date and are corrected.
- [x] **T10.2 Memory.** *(NFR-002)* Find where idle RSS (25.2 MiB) and RSS with 1M names + 100k entries (130.4 MiB) go (per-worker rings, the cache's preallocation, the snapshot and its index, allocator retention after a compile) and bring both under their gates (20 and 64 MiB).
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* measured with the shipped musl build (`bench/memprobe.py --bin`, a binary from the Dockerfile's `bin` stage), since the bench build carries 11 MiB more resident code. After the earlier steps (one allocation per cache entry, the index replacing the FST pages) two things were left: mimalloc kept freed pages for its 10 ms purge delay (a compile's churn stayed resident), and about 15 MiB of the executable's code stayed resident after running once (start-up, the API router and OpenAPI document, a compile). Now mimalloc purges at once and the executable's read-only, unmodified pages are released 15 s after start-up and after each filter index build (ADR-144). Idle 13.7 MiB (16.1 after 3 min), 1M names + 100k entries 60.0 MiB (60.9 after 3 min), compile peak 97.5 MiB (was 170). The heap that remains: the cache (about 23 MiB per 100k entries: answers 9, hash tables 10, FIFOs 4), the lookup index (10 MiB per 1M names), telemetry rings (2 MiB).
  - *Findings 2026-10-07 (`bench/memprobe.py`: RSS by mapping next to the server's counters):* idle is **18.8 MiB with the shipped binary** (v0.1.0, musl, fat LTO): 14.2 MiB of it is the binary's own code pages, 4.4 MiB heap. The gate run's 25.2 MiB was the bench build (more code). With 1M names + 100k entries the shipped binary used 111.7 MiB: code ~15, heap ~96, of which the lookup index 10.2, the FSTs read into memory ~9, the cache ~30 (312 B per entry, where the byte budget counted 208), the query log's open dictionary ~10 (capped at 65,536 names per part), and ~20 still unattributed.
  - *Done so far:* cache entries in one allocation, an epoch-relative timestamp, and a byte budget that counts real memory (`tests/footprint.rs`: 312 → 260 B per entry, budget within 5 %; 4-thread hits 12.7 % faster, single-thread unchanged). The domain FSTs are memory-mapped (`telltale_net::MappedFile`, the only new `unsafe`, in `sys`) and their pages dropped after the hash check and again once the lookup index answers (−9.2 MiB heap at 1M names; ~25 MiB on a Pi with 2.7M).
  - *Heap profile (`bench/heapprof.py`, a `dhat-heap` build):* the "unattributed" memory was a 64 KiB response buffer each query held while waiting upstream (12.5 MiB with 200 in flight), left resident in the allocator after a burst. It's now allocated once the answer is in, sized to it (100k-fill stage 101.4 → 85.0 MiB). The query log's dictionary cap went from 65,536 to 16,384 names per part (its table was 4 MiB at the old cap).
  - *Status 2026-10-07 (bench build):* 1M names + 100k entries 130.4 → **84.2 MiB**; the Pi with 2.7M names 82.8 → **49.6 MiB** (edge 185). Gate: 64 MiB.
  - *Next:* the cache's hash tables and queues (~14 MiB at 100k entries: a 32-byte key in the map and both queues, power-of-two growth), and the binary's code (~15 MiB resident).
- [x] **T10.3 Recompile on a Pi 4 within 8 s.** *(FLT-003)* 2M names take 11.6 s on the single recompile thread; measure 2 threads at `SCHED_IDLE` under load (p99 regression ≤ 10 %), or make the single-thread compile faster.
  - *Progress 2026-10-17:* 2 threads cost more p99 during a recompile on 4 cores (`bench.py swap --server-cpus 0-3 --client-cpus 4-7`: 40.5 → 54 µs vs 44 → 49.5 µs on 1 thread), so the owner kept 1 thread and asked for a faster compile. A parser fast path for the usual line shapes (`name`, `0.0.0.0 name`, `||name^`; the same result as the general parser, tested on the bench lists) cut the parser by a third. On the live Pi (owner's OK, once, in RAM at background priority; DNS answered throughout), the gate's 2M names: 14.2 → 11.8 s on 1 thread, 9.7 s on 2. Building the FST alone takes 8.1 s on one Pi core (the `fst` crate's builder, about 4 µs a name), so a one-thread compile can't meet 8 s while the new filter waits for the FST. The owner chose the first: since ADR-145 the compile's first merge pass fills the lookup index and the new rules serve from it (a pending snapshot), and a second pass writes the FSTs; the finished snapshot reuses the index, and a compile that fails after going live puts the previous filter back (`flt_003_the_live_matcher_decides_like_the_finished_snapshot`, `flt_003_a_recompile_goes_live_before_its_snapshot_is_finished`, `flt_003_sources_rewind_for_a_second_pass`). VM, one thread, 2.74M real names: rules answer at 1.98 s, the compile finishes at 4.55 s (peak 170 MiB, was 163); p99 during a recompile on 4 cores 44.5 → 53 µs (within this harness's noise of the 1-thread baseline). On the live Pi (the owner's OK for a second run; in RAM at background priority, DNS answered throughout), the gate's 2M names on one thread: the new rules answer at 4.77 and 4.71 s; the compile finishes at 12.8 s in the background. **Met** (the gate is measured to when the recompiled filter answers, ADR-145).
- [x] **T10.4 Cold start under 500 ms with the snapshot on disk.** *(NFR-001)* 565 ms until blocking works with 1M names: find what runs between ready (53 ms) and the index being built, and serve from the snapshot sooner.
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* the 565 ms was the measurement. `gate.py` waited for the lookup index (`telltale_filter_lookup_index_bytes > 0`), polled every 0.5 s, but the node blocks before the index exists: the snapshot is served through the FST walk at once, and the index replaces it when built. Measured as the gate states it (until a blocked name is answered blocked, over DNS every 5 ms, a new name each time so the cache can't hide it), the shipped build blocks 52 ms after starting with 1M names (median of 5; ready 51.5 ms; the index at about 0.5 s). `bench/coldstart.py` measures it and checks the detector against names not on the lists; `gate.py` reports blocking and index times apart.
- [ ] **T10.5 Measurements on separate hardware.** *(NFR-001)* p99 at 50 % load (cache hit, blocked) with the load generator on another machine; Pi 4 throughput on a Pi that isn't serving the home network; cross-site config propagation p95. Needs hardware or the owner's OK for the live cluster.
  - *Progress 2026-10-18 (owner's setup: load from the build VM against a temporary pod on the homelab node, not the live service, no load on the Pi):* a pod in its own namespace (`telltale-bench`, host network on spare ports, 4 CPUs, 1M names, a loopback stub upstream, the API off, only the VM allowed, rate limiting off), removed afterwards. `bench/remote.py` finds the saturating rate, runs at half, and reads the server's latency histogram around the run: cache hit 99.999 % and blocked 99.999 % of queries within 250 µs (p99 ≤ 50 µs), 0 lost, so **both latency gates are met**; dnsperf's end-to-end p99 (4.9 ms / 455 µs) is the network between the machines. The live service, probed from the homelab host throughout: 1,085 of 1,085 answered, p99 as before the load. **Open:** Pi 4 throughput (owner: no spare Pi, stays unproven); cross-site propagation p95 (read-only from both nodes' logs: 17–32 ms steady, 2.2 s during a primary restart, too few versions yet).
- [x] **T10.6 Requirement check.** Every P0 requirement in `spec/01` against its test or evidence.
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* `spec/trace.py` (Rust tests named after the requirement, Playwright titles, `REQ:` marks in end-to-end scripts and CI, `spec/trace-evidence.toml` for what a test can't show) writes `docs/v1-requirements.md`; `--check` runs in CI's `site` job and the VM gate, failing on a stale report or a P0 without evidence. Result: 73 P0s, 66 with named tests, all with evidence. Gaps found and closed: DNS-017 (reverse-zone routing over the private-PTR rule: `dns_017_reverse_zone_goes_to_its_route`), OPS-006 (`ops_006_health_endpoints` through the router), NFR-003 (`nfr_003_unsafe_is_forbidden_outside_telltale_net`), DNS-013's EDE 22 (`dns_013_no_upstream_answer_is_servfail_with_ede_22`), NFR-007's store write latency and segment count (new `telltale_qlog_block_write_seconds`, `telltale_qlog_segments`; `nfr_007_store_write_latency_and_segment_count`). **Open:** NFR-005 (T10.10). T10.8 decided by the owner: our own numbers and method only.
- [x] **T10.10 Supply chain.** *(NFR-005)* `cargo-auditable` release builds, a CycloneDX SBOM per release, cosign-signed images (keyless, GitHub OIDC), and a reproducibility check (two builds of the same commit compared).
  - *Done 2026-10-18 (ADR-146):* the Dockerfile builds with `cargo auditable zigbuild` (pinned, checksummed), and the image workflow checks every binary has the `.dep-v0` section; `syft` (pinned) writes a CycloneDX SBOM per binary from that data, checked by `deploy/release/sbom_check.py` (303 components for amd64) and covered by the signed `SHA256SUMS`; every pushed image (edge, release tags) is signed by digest with keyless cosign and the signature verified in the same step (`.github/scripts/cosign-sign.sh`; `verify.sh` checks it for stable releases); the nightly `reproducible` job builds the amd64 release binary twice without cache and compares. Two clean builds first differed (mimalloc's `__TIME__`): the build date is now the commit's and `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` is passed to the build. On the VM, two uncached builds then came out identical. User docs: "Checking what you run" in `docs/running.md`.
- [x] **T10.7 Security review.** Sign-in (local and OIDC), the cluster channel (mTLS, join, forwarding, failover), and the parsers (DNS wire, lists, config, the query log).
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* `docs/security-review.md`. Fixed, each with a test: unknown cluster members trusted at their word (C1, high), RPC answers accepted from any stream (C2, high), votes for ineligible candidates, users and tokens sent to resolver pods, peer simulations naming local files, unbounded shipped uploads and joins (ADR-143); account changes with any credential (owner: signed-in sessions only), self-service password checks outside the lockout, replica sessions outliving a password change, half-made tokens (ADR-142); the dotted-label filter bypass, query-log dictionary inflation, damaged anomaly state, backup restore paths, zip totals, short cache-dump questions. **Deferred:** fuzz targets for the cluster frame decoder, the backup and import archive readers, and the cache dump loader. **Before rollout:** check (read-only) that each live node's registry lists every node.
- [x] **T10.9 DNSSEC: insecure delegations reached through CNAMEs (live trial 2026-10-07).** *(DNS-011, ADR-098)* hickory 0.26 judges answers in unsigned zones reached through CNAMEs as bogus (`www.netflix.com`: EDE 10) or loops on NS/DS lookups (`prod.ftl.netflix.com`). Before accepting a bogus verdict, check whether an ancestor of each name in the chain is provably unsigned (a secure denial of its DS at the parent); if so, the answer is insecure. Tests on real chains; a bug report for hickory. *Done 2026-10-07:* the depth limit is indeterminate, bogus names are logged in permissive mode, hickory's validator log is off, and replicas no longer repeat config warnings on every applied change (cad423f). Then `Validator::proven_insecure`: walking down from the domain under the TLD, the first apex (own SOA) whose DS denial validates (secure, or insecure for NSEC3 opt-out) makes the zone unsigned; a bogus verdict, an error, or a timeout becomes insecure if every owner in the answer is in such a zone; proven zones cached 15 minutes (4,096 at most) and not validated again. Tests: `dns_011_zone_candidates`, `dns_011_offline_unsigned_zone_through_cname_is_insecure` (the test root delegates an unsigned `u.` with a CNAME chain). Live, Quad9, `validate` mode: Netflix, Amazon, BBC, Apple names resolve (insecure); `isc.org`, `example.com` secure with AD; `dnssec-failed.org` SERVFAIL. A bug report for hickory is drafted (`hickory` judges such answers bogus although it validates the parent's DS denial). Also: hickory's `DnsError::Nsec` (a negative answer with a non-secure proof) is read for the DS denial (NSEC3 opt-out is `Insecure`); the stream pool retries a query once on a fresh connection when the server closes the connection before answering (`tests/pool_retry.rs`; before, only a closed-before-sending connection was retried); each pooled connection logs why it ended (debug); `TELLTALE_LOG_DNSSEC_LIBRARY=1` turns hickory's validator log back on. **Still open:** at a cold start Quad9 sometimes closes fresh DoT connections with queries unanswered, which can make the first validations fail (signed zones bogus for a while); `permissive` stays the recommendation until that's understood (next: failure reasons in `telltale_upstream_requests_total`).
- [x] **T10.8 Comparative report.** Owner decision: publish our own numbers and method only, or change the gate. *Decided 2026-10-17 (owner):* our own numbers and the exact method only, no side-by-side with other resolvers; `docs/v1-gate.md` and `site/performance.html` are that report.

## M11 — Observability pack (owner request 2026-10-08)
From the project review of 2026-10-08: the owner picked six observability capabilities to add, on the condition that none of them costs DNS performance. Everything runs on the telemetry thread, a background task, or the asynchronous upstream path; the cache-hit and blocked-answer paths don't change.

- [x] **T11.1 Service-level objectives.** *(OBS-016, OBS-015, OBS-010, ADR-105)* Availability and latency objectives with error budgets and multi-window burn rates: `[slo]`, `GET /api/v1/stats/slo`, MCP `slo_status`, a dashboard card, a `slo_burn` alert rule and health reason, `/metrics` gauges, Helm recording rules and burn-rate alerts, a standalone rules file, a Grafana row.
  - *AC:* the burn math and the window pairs (fast, slow, ticket) with the quiet-network minimums; slow answers counted per bucket and stored in the rollups so older builds still read them; the route reads 6 hours of minutes and the window's hours; config validation (targets below 100, the threshold a bucket bound); `promtool test rules` shows the chart's SLO alerts firing on a burn and quiet on a quiet network; Playwright: the card from the server, then a stubbed fast burn.
  - *Done 2026-10-08:* `telltale-api` `slo.rs` (`status`, `burning`), `Counts::slow` and `Aggregates::slo_latency_us`, the rollups' `S` section, `TimeBucket::slow`, `SloConfig` and `AlertWhen::SloBurn`, `health::slo_reasons` (federated health adds it once), `render_slo`; `prometheusRule.slo` in the chart and `deploy/helm/tests/alerts.test.yml` cases; `deploy/prometheus/build.sh` and `telltale-slo-rules.yaml` (checked in CI); Grafana **Service level** row with a `$slo_le` variable; `SloCard.svelte`, help topic `slo`. Tests: `obs_016_*` (API unit and route, store, config), Playwright `obs_016 service level card`. Docs: `running.md` *Service-level objectives*, alerts, health; site monitoring and agents pages.
- [x] **T11.2 Cache sizing advice.** *(OBS-021, ADR-106)* A sampled ghost list of evicted keys: the extra hits a 1.25×, 1.5×, and 2× cache would have served, and whether the cache could shrink; Cache page, API, MCP, metrics.
  - *AC:* sampled re-insertions of evicted keys count at the smallest covering size, expired ones never; nothing when everything fits; counts scaled and cumulative; grow/shrink/ok/learning thresholds; the lookup path unchanged (bench-smoke) and the per-entry footprint test still within budget.
  - *Done 2026-10-09:* `S3Fifo::with_sizing`/`insert_at`/`sizing_hits` (`sizing_evicted`, `sizing_reinserted`), `entry::expires`, `CacheStats::ghost_hits`/`peak_bytes`; `crates/telltale/src/cache_sizing.rs` (`advise`); `CacheNodeStats::sizing` (`CacheSizing`, `CacheSizingStep`); `telltale_cache_ghost_hits_total{size}`, `telltale_cache_peak_bytes`; MCP `cache_advice`; the Cache page's **Size** line. Tests: `obs_021_sizing_counts_what_a_bigger_cache_would_hit` (s3fifo), `obs_021_cache_sizing_estimates` (cache), `obs_021_*` (advice), Playwright (fresh server: learning). Docs: `running.md` *Is the cache the right size?*, help topic, site monitoring and agents pages, `spec/03` §4.1.
- [x] **T11.3 Synthetic probes and certificate expiry.** *(OBS-020, OBS-015, OBS-010, ADR-107)* Each listener asked through its own protocol on a schedule (plus optional external targets); success, latency, and TLS certificate expiry in metrics, health, alerts, and the UI.
  - *AC:* the targets follow the listeners (loopback for wildcard binds, DoH paths, PROXY-protocol listeners skipped, extra targets by IP); a real probe gets an answer and a closed port fails; failures in a row and certificate expiry tracked; health and alert rules; probe queries never reach the analytics or the query log; config validation; Playwright: the e2e listener answers its probe.
  - *Done 2026-10-09:* `crates/telltale/src/probes.rs` (`targets`, `ask`, `cert_expiry`, `Probes`, `spawn`), `[probe]` and `probe_target`, `AlertWhen::ProbeFailing`/`CertExpiring`, `UpstreamOptions::observe`, `event::PROBE_NAME` and `Record::is_probe` (dropped on the telemetry thread), `health::probe_reasons`, `Read::Probes` and `Backend::probes`, `GET /api/v1/system/probes`, MCP `probe_status`, `render_probes`; Settings → System → **Listener checks**, help topic `probes`. Tests: `obs_020_*` (probes, health, telemetry, config), Playwright `obs_020 listener checks`. Docs: `running.md` *Listener checks*, alerts, health; configuration.md; site monitoring and agents pages; `spec/06` §9.
- [x] **T11.4 Upstream truth checks.** *(OBS-019, ADR-108)* Sampled second opinions from another upstream, compared off the query path; per-upstream DNSSEC verdicts and EDE codes.
  - *AC:* the comparison (same, different addresses, different response code, filtered incl. sinkholes, unanswered); EDE read from the OPT and counted per upstream; DNSSEC verdicts per upstream; sampling one in N, off by default; through the pipeline, a filtering upstream's answer is counted and compared with the other member's, both answers kept; config validation and the traffic warning; Playwright: the card says second opinions are off, then shows stubbed results.
  - *Done 2026-10-09:* `crates/telltale/src/upstream_checks.rs` (`Gist`, `compare`, `first_ede`, `ede_name`, `Quality`, `Checker`), `Pipeline::after_upstream`/`set_upstream_check`, `[upstream_check]`, `Backend::upstream_checks` and `Read::UpstreamChecks`, `GET /api/v1/analytics/upstream-checks`, MCP `upstream_checks`, `render_upstream_quality`; the Upstreams page's **Answer quality** card, help topic `upstream-checks`. Tests: `obs_019_*` (comparison, counts, sampling, pipeline, config), Playwright `obs_019 upstream answer quality`. Docs: `running.md` *Upstream truth checks*, configuration.md, site monitoring and agents pages, `spec/04` §9.
- [x] **T11.5 Shadow lists and over-blocking suspects.** *(OBS-018, ADR-109)* `mode = "shadow"` lists counted, not enforced; likely over-blocking from retries and quick allows.
  - *AC:* a shadow list never blocks (its name is answered) while an enforced list next to it does; the telemetry-thread sink counts its would-be blocks per list (queries, devices, top names) and lists configured shadow lists with zero; retry bursts once per burst, an answer soon after a block counts, a late one or another device doesn't; privacy levels; cluster merges; config validation (block lists only); Playwright: the shadow badge and counts from real queries, then stubbed suspects.
  - *Done 2026-10-09:* `ListMode` and `[[list]] mode`, `Policy::shadow_lists`, `FilterState::shadow_ids`/`shadow_group_masks`/`list_names`, `crates/telltale/src/shadow.rs` (`Shadow`, `ShadowSink`), `federation::merge_shadow`/`merge_overblocking`, `Backend::shadow_lists`/`overblocking`, `Read::ShadowLists`/`Overblocking`, `GET /api/v1/analytics/shadow-lists` and `/overblocking`, MCP `shadow_lists` and `overblocking_suspects`, `ListInfo::mode`, `ExplainRule::shadow`, `telltale_list_shadow_hits_total`; the Lists page's shadow badge, mode field, **Would have blocked** and **Likely over-blocking** cards, help topics. Tests: `obs_018_*` (pipeline, sink, merges, config), Playwright `obs_018 shadow list and over-blocking`. Docs: `running.md` *Trying a list first (shadow mode)*, configuration.md, site monitoring and agents pages, `spec/06` §7.2.
- [x] **T11.6 Exemplars and traces.** *(OBS-017, ADR-110)* OpenMetrics exemplars on the latency histograms; sampled and slow queries as OTLP traces; trace IDs the query log finds.
  - *AC:* trace IDs carry the start time and a keyed hash of the query-log fields (round-trip, keyed, field boundaries); one recent exemplar per bucket, refreshed at most once a second; `/metrics` negotiates OpenMetrics (counter families, escaped HELP, exemplars, `# EOF`) and falls back to text, or always with `exemplars = false`; traces sampled 1-in-N and slow, bounded queue, privacy levels; spans (server + upstream client) under the same ID; config validation (traces need the endpoint); `GET /queries?trace=` finds the row; verified against Prometheus 3.5 (exemplar storage), the strict `prometheus_client` OpenMetrics parser, and an OpenTelemetry Collector; Playwright: an exemplar's trace ID opens its query in the log. *Deferred:* exemplars on the per-upstream histogram (its events carry no query).
- [x] **T11.7 Traffic map.** *(OBS-026, API-005, ADR-123)* A treemap of what the network is reaching this hour: services, sites, or names; allowed and blocked in their own colours; live, with the fastest-growing tiles marked.
  - *AC:* built only on `/stats/top` and `/services` (no server change, so no bench-smoke needed); grouping by service (catalog suffix), site (base domain), or name; the hour picker; growth only within the same hour; a tile opens its names in the query log; works in both themes, at phone width, and without motion when reduced motion is asked for; Playwright: real blocked and allowed names, then stubbed lists that group into one service and grow.
  - *Done 2026-10-11:* `ui/src/pages/Traffic.svelte`, `ui/src/lib/treemap.ts` (`squarify`), `api.top(..., hour)`, `--tm-ink`, icon `traffic`, help topic `traffic-map`. Tests: Playwright `obs_026 traffic map`. Docs: `running.md` *Traffic map*, site monitoring page, screenshots from the demo network.

## M12 — Ideas from the community trackers (owner request 2026-10-09)
From a survey of the most-requested open issues on Pi-hole's and Technitium's trackers (Pi-hole's feature-request forum included); the owner picked these.

- [x] **T12.1 Exclusions.** *(OBS-022, ADR-111)* Names and devices kept out of the query log, live view, analytics, and exports (still answered and counted in metrics), with a toggle; Settings → System and `[exclusions]` (GitOps).
  - *AC:* names cover their subdomains (label boundaries), clients by address or network (IPv4 and IPv6); excluded events never reach the aggregates or sinks and are counted; the toggle keeps the lists; set, checked, reverted, and kept-in-Git like the rate limit; validation (domain names, at most 1,000 each); the startup replay skips them; Playwright: an excluded name is answered but never logged.
- [x] **T12.2 New-device alerts.** *(OBS-010)* Already there: the `new_device` alert rule (T9.5), cluster-wide. Nothing to build.
- [x] **T12.3 Stale lists.** *(OBS-023, ADR-112)* A URL list unchanged for `stale_after_days` (default 30, per list too): `staleDays`, health `list_stale`, alert rule `list_stale`, a line on the Lists page.
  - *AC:* whole days from `last_changed`; per-list override and 0 = never; files, inline, disabled, and never-downloaded lists aren't stale; health reason and alert; config defaults.
- [x] **T12.4 NSID.** *(DNS-021, ADR-113)* `[dns] nsid`: answers to queries that ask carry the node's name.
  - *AC:* only when asked; off by default; the OPT's length stays consistent; reloadable.
- [x] **T12.5 AAAA filtering per group.** *(FLT-016, ADR-114)* `[[group]] filter_aaaa`: AAAA answered with no data.
  - *AC:* the group gets NOERROR/no records even with an IPv6 address cached; A still answers; other groups unaffected; local records still answer; blocks still win; explain says so; not with `dns64`; the Groups page shows and edits it.

## M9 — Follow-ups to v1 (owner request 2026-10-06)
The deferrals collected after M8, worked through one by one. DHCP is out of scope (owner decision 2026-10-06; the server itself was removed in T9.15, ADR-091: DHCP stays out of the platform's direction).

- [x] **T9.1 Users and tokens across the cluster.** *(CLU-003, API-003; ADR-045)* Users, recovery codes, and API/agent tokens made on the primary work on every node; replicas refuse identity changes with a pointer to the primary.
  - *AC:* the primary's admin signs in on a replica and its new token works there within seconds; a user made on the replica is refused (409); users only on a replica keep working; a name on both takes the primary's account; codes used on a replica stay used; a bad document changes nothing; a promoted replica manages identities.
  - *Done 2026-10-06:* `telltale-store` `state/identity.rs` (export/import, migration 6 `origin`), `identities` in the cluster manifest, publish and apply in `replication.rs`, `Auth::identity_writable` on every identity-changing route; unit tests `clu_003_identities_replicate`, `clu_003_bad_identities_change_nothing`; `deploy/cluster/e2e.sh` (sign-in and a token on the replica, a refused user); docs; ADR-045 implementation notes.

- [x] **T9.2 Exact cluster latency and a cluster-wide live tail.** *(CLU-002, OBS-008)* Latency percentiles merge from the nodes' histograms; the live tail follows every node (scoped like other reads).
  - *AC:* merged percentiles equal those of one histogram with every value; older peers still count (approximate blend, not missing); a query on the replica appears in the primary's live tail within seconds, labelled with its node; `scope=node:local` keeps the tail local; idle remote sessions close.
  - *Done 2026-10-06:* `Aggregates::latency_buckets`, `merged_percentiles` (telemetry); `LatencyHist` and `Backend::latency_hists`; peer reads `LatencyHist`, `TailOpen`/`TailPoll`/`TailClose` with 15 s idle expiry and 16 sessions per node; the federated tail splits the rate cap; unit test `clu_002_latency_histograms_merge_exactly`; cluster e2e (labelled tail rows, latency counts both nodes; the ship-mode step now waits for its own row); docs.

- [x] **T9.3 Dashboard history from ephemeral nodes.** *(CLU-007, ADR-055)* Ship-mode nodes also send their per-minute counts; the cluster's dashboard keeps a replaced pod's traffic.
  - *AC:* minutes arrive on the target per node; a node answering live isn't counted twice; damaged bodies and bad node IDs are refused; retention as for minutes (7 days); the whole-cluster view only (a scoped view shows live nodes).
  - *Done 2026-10-06:* `Rollups::put_shipped`/`shipped_range` (`shipped_minute`), `ship::run_rollups`/`receive_rollups` (RPC `rollup.put`), `Backend::shipped_timeseries` merged into the federated time series (and so the summary); unit test `clu_007_rollup_minutes_ship`; cluster e2e (the replica's minutes reach the primary); docs.

- [x] **T9.4 Alert email.** *(OBS-010)* `type = "email"` destinations: STARTTLS or TLS, AUTH, any provider (no relay of one's own).
  - *AC:* a scripted SMTP server receives the alert over STARTTLS (private CA) and implicit TLS with the right envelope, subject, and body; a wrong password is reported (535) and nothing is delivered; a password over `smtp+insecure://` fails the configuration check; checked by hand against Mailpit.
  - *Done 2026-10-06:* `crates/telltale/src/smtp.rs` (ADR-085, Proposed), email destination fields and validation, `deliver_email`; unit tests `obs_010_smtp_urls`, `obs_010_smtp_message`; `deploy/email-e2e.sh` in CI; docs (Gmail app passwords, Mailpit).

- [x] **T9.5 More alert rules.** *(OBS-010)* `sync_lag`, `new_device`, `disk_full`, `plan_pending`.
  - *AC:* a replica behind the primary, a device seen for the first time on any node (not during the first day after a fresh start), a data disk over `threshold`, and an agent plan waiting for approval each raise an alert (the last three once per subject); the email e2e sees `disk_full` fire.
  - *Done 2026-10-06:* `anomaly::Engine::new_clients` (learning guard), `Backend::new_devices` (federated, earliest sighting wins), the conditions in `alerts.rs` (plans read from this node's plan store); unit test `obs_010_new_devices_after_the_first_day`; `deploy/email-e2e.sh` checks `disk_full` (and now waits for its mail server); docs.

The Pi-hole and Technitium importers still turn DHCP reservations into named devices. A configuration with `[dhcp]` is refused at load (unknown field), so remove the section before upgrading.
- [x] **T9.16 Aggressive NSEC (RFC 8198).** *(DNS-011)* Secure NSEC denials are kept per upstream group (ranges, the zone's SOA, signatures; bounded; TTL = min(NSEC, negative TTL)); a later miss inside a proven range gets a signed NXDOMAIN or NODATA without an upstream query (`[dnssec] aggressive_nsec`, default on; `telltale_dnssec_synthesized_total`). Unit tests for the proofs (wildcard, delegation, signer, insecure); `deploy/dnssec-e2e.sh` checks it against the live root zone (ADR-092). NSEC3 deferred.
- [x] **T9.17 Stamp certificate hashes.** *(UPS-003)* DoH, DoT, and DoQ stamps' hashes are parsed (32-byte SHA-256 of a chain certificate's `tbsCertificate`) and enforced: a certificate the server presents must match one, after the usual verification (and with it off). Unit tests (parse, malformed), an integration test against a DoT server (match, any-of, mismatch, insecure) (ADR-093). Completes T7.24's deferral.
- [x] **T9.18 Zone apex SOA and NS.** *(DNS-018)* `[[zone]]` answers SOA and NS at its apex (the zone file's records, else made up: `localhost.`, `hostmaster.<zone>`, `negative_ttl` as MINIMUM), and negative answers carry that SOA owned by the apex (RFC 2308) instead of a placeholder owned by the question. Test in `dns_018_local_zones` (ADR-094). Zone transfers stay out of scope.
- [x] **T9.19 dnstap forwarder messages; per-sink drops.** *(OBS-007, OBS-010)* `[telemetry.dnstap] forwarder = true` adds `FORWARDER_QUERY`/`FORWARDER_RESPONSE` for every upstream exchange (the query as sent, the answer, the upstream's address, port, and protocol; sampled like client messages; one `OnceLock` load per exchange when off; the recursive resolver's internal queries excluded), through `telltale_upstream::set_exchange_observer`. `telltale_sink_dropped_total{sink}` counts each event sink's drops (T7.13's deferral). Tests: the encoding, and the observer on a real exchange.
- [x] **T9.20 `$dnsrewrite` in lists.** *(FLT-001, FLT-014)* AdGuard's `$dnsrewrite` applies: addresses (A/AAAA by family, several combine), a CNAME (resolved like a safe-search target), an rcode (`NXDOMAIN`, `REFUSED`, `SERVFAIL`, or `NOERROR;;`), in shorthand or `RCODE;TYPE;VALUE`; other types are unsupported lines. A matching rewrite wins over blocking; `@@…$dnsrewrite` turns it off; `$client`/`$dnstype`/`$denyallow` narrow it. One bool check per query when no list has rewrites. Attributed to its list. Tests: values, combining, and the pipeline (ADR-095). Closes T7.20's deferral.
- [x] **T9.21 Uninstall (owner request 2026-10-06).** *(OPS-004)* `install.sh --uninstall`: stops and disables the service, removes the unit, its drop-ins, and the binary; offers to restore systemd-resolved when the install changed it (the default, also without a terminal; `--keep-resolver` declines): `/etc/resolv.conf` goes back to the link or file the install recorded (installs now record it in their resolved drop-in) and systemd-resolved restarts; keeps `/etc/telltale` and `/var/lib/telltale` unless asked or `--purge` (which also removes the user). `deploy/systemd/e2e.sh` checks it on CI's machine: everything gone, the recorded resolv.conf back, names resolve, the config kept; then `--purge`.

Playwright API test.

No threshold, no badge (owner agreed). README: CI and license badges; a release badge comes with the first stable tag.

The pipeline now identifies the device first; an unparseable query counts for `default`. Test `flt_006_local_answers_keep_the_device_group`.

Docs: *Upstream servers per group* and a VLAN example in `docs/running.md`; a new site page, **Groups** (`site/groups.html`), and a row in the home page's list.   - *AC:* the hints see only the priming query and the primed root is used after; a slow server is hedged (answer from the next server well before the slow one would have) and isn't without hedging; expired, not-yet-valid, and missing signatures give EDE 7, 8, 10; the built-in anchors are KSK 20326 and 38696; a good anchors file replaces them and a bad one doesn't.

- [x] **T9.29 Health level and icon (owner request 2026-10-08).** *(OBS-015, ADR-104)* `GET /api/v1/system/health` (federated `Read::Health`, plus the cluster's own conditions) and the MCP tool `health`: `healthy`, `degraded`, or `severe`, with reasons (code, node, summary, UI link). Severe: an upstream group with none answering, no node serving, SERVFAIL ≥ 25%. Degraded: one upstream down (owner), a node unreachable/not serving/behind, a list failing, any rate-limited query in 5 minutes (owner), SERVFAIL ≥ 5%, a data disk > 90%. Anomalies don't count (owner). UI: an icon left of the project links (circle ✓, triangle !, octagon ×; same 18 px line style), a panel with the reasons, and a dot on the phone menu button. *AC:* each level's rules hold (`obs_015_*` unit tests); Playwright `obs_015 health icon, its reasons, and the phone dot`.

## M13 — Operations pack (owner request 2026-10-09)
From the capability review of 2026-10-09: the owner picked four of the proposals. Same condition as M11: nothing on the cache-hit or blocked-answer path; everything runs on the telemetry thread, a background task, the cluster channel, or the API. Each task has an opinionated design doc in `docs/design/` that the implementing agent follows; deviations go into the task's ADR as amendments. Suggested order: T13.4 (smallest, exercises heartbeat fields and health plumbing) → T13.1 → T13.3 → T13.2 (largest; builds on T13.1's `impact` for the pin diff and on T13.4's heartbeat changes).

- [x] **T13.1 Change simulation.** *(OBS-024, AGT-002, ADR-115; design `docs/design/change-simulation.md`)* `simulate=<window>` on every dry run adds `impact` (newly blocked / newly allowed / changed route / changed answer, by device and name, rows, partial); `POST /api/v1/simulate` for a whole candidate shared configuration and `telltale config simulate`; MCP `simulate_change`, `plan_*` tools take `simulate` and carry `impact` only when asked or when `plans_by_default` is on (owner 2026-10-09); the UI's `ChangePreview` gains a "What would this have done?" button; `[simulate]` as a shared one-of section with Settings → System → *Change simulation* (`PUT /api/v1/simulate-settings`, ADR-111 pattern); metrics.
  - *AC:* (1) a block rule for a name a device asked 40 times yesterday reports 40 newly blocked queries, 1 device, that name, and the rule as the deciding list; an allow rule for a name blocked yesterday reports it under newly allowed with the list that had blocked it; a group's upstream change reports the changed route counts; a change that affects nothing reports zeros. (2) Adding a list compiles **only** that list (test: the serving snapshot's version is unchanged and the side snapshot holds the new list's names only) and the merged decision equals a full compile's `decide` for a corpus of 10k names × 3 clients (property test over both lookup modes). (3) Bounds: a second simulation on the same node gets `409 simulation_busy`; `max_rows = 1000` on a 5,000-row log answers `partial: true` with exactly 1,000 rows read, newest first; a 5-s `max_secs` is honored within 1 s. (4) Privacy level 1 answers `unavailable` with the reason; a device-only change (assign a device to a group whose lists are the same) still works at level 1. (5) Federated: in the cluster e2e, a simulation on the replica includes the primary's rows and the controller's copy of a ship-mode node's rows, with `missingNodes` for a stopped node. (6) Determinism: two runs over the same log produce byte-identical `impact`. (7) `make bench-smoke` unchanged (the engine never touches the pipeline's state). (8) Playwright: the preview button on a quick block rule shows the count from real queries made in the test; Settings → System turns `plans_by_default` on (checked, applied, reverted); the MCP e2e shows a plan without `simulate` has no `impact`, one with `simulate = "24h"` has it, and with the toggle on every plan has it. (9) Docs (`running.md` *Trying a change against yesterday's traffic*), help topic, configuration reference, site monitoring and agents pages, `spec/06` §7.3.
  - *Done 2026-10-09:* `crates/telltale/src/simulate.rs`: `Decider` (both configurations through the pipeline's own steps: `explain::answered_early`, quick rules and schedules at each row's time, `FilterState::list_rewrite`, the filter, AAAA filter, group rewrites and safe search, `Router::select`), the serving snapshot plus a side snapshot of only the changed lists (`changed_lists`, `compile_side` under `<data_dir>/snapshots/sim-*`, removed after) merged with `Matcher::decide_ranked` / `Ranked::{remap, merge}` in the candidate's list-ID space; `scan` over the query log newest first with `max_rows`/`max_secs`; `Tally` (bounded, mergeable, deterministic `finish`); one at a time per node (`409 simulation_busy`); `mode()` (disabled, privacy level, not applicable); `run_devices_only` at privacy level 1; RPC `sim.run` to every reachable peer with both shared parts, `missingNodes`; runs on its own background-priority thread. `server::build_candidate`. `simulate=`/`simulateUntil=` on every dry run (`telltale_api::SimulateOpts`, carried through write forwarding with a longer deadline) adds `simulation`; `POST /api/v1/simulate`, `telltale config simulate` / `telltale ctl simulate`; MCP `simulate_change` and `simulate` on every `plan_*` tool (default `auto`; a busy node gives the plan with a `note`); `[simulate]` as a managed one-of (`ManagedKind::Simulate`, `/api/v1/simulate-settings/default`, scope `config:write:simulate`, Settings → System → Change simulation; no MCP tool); agents need `querylog:read`; metrics `telltale_simulations_total{outcome}`, `telltale_simulation_duration_seconds`, `telltale_simulation_rows_total`. UI: `SimulationCard` on the quick-rule form, the device drawer, the config editor's preview (lists, groups, upstreams, schedules), and agents' plan cards. Help topic `simulate`; `running.md` *Trying a change against yesterday's traffic*; configuration reference; site monitoring and agents pages; `spec/06` §7.3. Tests: `obs_024_merged_decisions_equal_a_full_compile` (10k names × 3 clients × both lookups), `obs_024_a_block_rule_reports_what_it_would_have_blocked`, `…_an_allow_rule…`, `…_route_changes_and_no_op_changes`, `…_only_changed_lists_are_compiled`, `obs_024_bounds`, `obs_024_privacy_level_1`, `obs_024_deterministic_and_mergeable`, `obs_024_simulation_requests_over_http`, `obs_024_simulate_parameters`, `obs_024_simulation_routes_need_their_scope`; cluster e2e (the replica's simulation counts the primary's rows and its copy of the replica's shipped rows; the primary's, with the replica stopped, lists it in `missingNodes`); MCP e2e (no simulation without `simulate`, one with `"24h"`, refused without `querylog:read`, `simulate_change`, every plan with `plans_by_default`, agents can't change it); Playwright `obs_024 simulate a quick rule, and the simulation settings`. Bench-smoke unchanged (VM: cache-hot 571k q/s, miss-heavy p50 231 µs / p99 359 µs; the engine never touches the pipeline's state). ADR-115 amended (still Proposed): re-decide the configuration in effect rather than compare with the recorded status; `simulation`, not `impact` (taken); refused rows replayed; no `has_qlog` heartbeat bit; `auto`; level-1 device-only mode with `undetermined`; exact bounded counts instead of Space-Saving; scopes. Not done: pauses and CNAME-target blocks aren't replayed (by design, documented); a peer that can't fetch a changed list's file or URL is listed as missing.
- [x] **T13.2 Staged rollouts and pins.** *(CLU-013, CLU-003, CLU-008, OBS-010, ADR-116; design `docs/design/staged-rollouts.md`)* Two heads with targeted delivery; `[cluster.rollout]` (canaries — **empty by default, off until set** (owner 2026-10-09) —, bake_secs, guard thresholds, require_traffic, history) as a shared one-of section with Settings → Cluster → *Staged rollouts* (a canary picker over the cluster's nodes, sites, and "resolver pods"; `PUT /api/v1/cluster/rollout-settings`; GitOps shows the TOML); the guard from heartbeat serving stats, probes, readiness, and apply errors; promote/abort; pins as new versions with the diff; `versions.json` history with protected blobs; API, CLI, Cluster page (stage per node, bake countdown, guard readings, pin banner with diff, version list with Pin), MCP, alerts, health, metrics, events; Helm `cluster.rollout` values.
  - *AC:* (1) `deploy/cluster/rollout-e2e.sh` (CI): primary + canary replica + plain replica under two-server load; a record change reaches the canary within 5 s and the plain replica only after `bake_secs = 5`; the plain replica's applied version never equals the canary version before promotion. (2) A change that makes the canary SERVFAIL (an upstream group routed to a black hole for a test domain the load generator asks) is pinned back before the bake ends: the plain replica never applies it, the canary is back on the stable version within 5 s of the pin, 0 lost queries on either replica, `rollout_failed` fires once, health is `degraded` with `cluster_pinned`, and `PUT` of a record answers `409 cluster_pinned` with a non-empty diff. (3) `DELETE /api/v1/cluster/pin` resumes: the same change rolls out again and, with the black hole removed, promotes; `GET /api/v1/cluster/versions` lists both attempts with outcomes. (4) An operator `pin` of an older kept version applies on every node (the primary included) and the primary's own `explain` reflects it. (5) Failover during a pin: kill the primary, promote the eligible replica, it publishes still pinned (`pinned` in its first manifest). (6) N−1: `deploy/cluster/upgrade-e2e.sh` extended: the published edge binary as a replica receives only stable versions during a rollout. (7) Helm scaled e2e: with nothing set, a change reaches every pod at once (no rollout); after `PUT /cluster/rollout-settings { canaries: ["ephemeral"] }` the pods are canaries and `rollout_status` names them; an outside node that joins is non-canary. Playwright: Settings → Cluster picks a node as canary (checked, applied, reverted). (8) Unit: guard math (before/after shares, the 50-answer minimum, `require_traffic`), head selection per peer, `versions.json` rotation and blob protection, manifest `rollout`/`pinned` serialization defaults. (9) Promtool tests for the two alert rules in the chart. (10) Docs (`running.md` *Rolling a change out in stages*, *Pinning the cluster to a version*), help topics, configuration reference, site clusters page, `spec/12` §4.1.
  - *Done 2026-10-10:* `crates/telltale/src/rollout.rs` (the version history `versions.json` with protected blobs, `rollout.json`, the guard over per-node heartbeat history: apply errors, not ready 15 s, listener checks, gone a minute after applying, and the SERVFAIL share against `max(before + 2, servfail_pct)` judged from 50 answers during the bake; canary selection; the JSON-Patch diff; the command queue the API feeds), `rollout/surface.rs` (status, versions, commands with dry runs and `If-Match`, `409 cluster_pinned` with the diff, the RPC `rollout.call` that brings a replica's request to the primary, audited on both). `replication.rs`: `publish_loop` rebuilt around a `Primary` (canary publish to online canary peers that take part in rollouts, a 60 s grace for a canary to connect, promotion as a new `seq` without `rollout`, pins as new versions with the pinned content served on the primary from `pinned.json` and `snapshots/pinned-<v>`, unpin, failover keeps the pin, meta-only changes wait for the bake). `telltale-cluster`: manifest `rollout`/`pinned`, the canary head and `head_for`, heartbeat `rollouts`, `sync_error`, `probe_failing`, `canary_of` (lag counts stable versions), `BlobStore::link`; and a fix found by the scaled e2e: blob fetches use the URL of the node's own stream, so a node outside Kubernetes can apply the controller's configuration. `[cluster.rollout]` as a managed one-of (`/api/v1/cluster/rollout-settings/default`, admin, agents `cluster:admin`); `GET /api/v1/cluster/rollout`, `/cluster/versions`, `POST …/rollout/promote|abort`, `…/versions/{epoch}.{seq}/pin`, `DELETE /cluster/pin`; problems `cluster_pinned` (with `diff`), `no_rollout`, `version_unknown`, `version_blobs_missing`; `telltale ctl rollout status|promote|abort`, `versions`, `pin`, `unpin`; MCP `rollout_status`, `plan_pin_version`, `plan_unpin` (only with `require_approval`); alert rules `rollout_failed`, `cluster_pinned`; health `cluster_pinned`, `rollout_stuck`; metrics `telltale_cluster_rollout_stage`, `…_rollout_info`, `…_rollouts_total{outcome}`, `…_pinned`, `…_pinned_to`; events. Helm `cluster.rollout` (rendered only when canaries are set), `TelltaleDNSRolloutFailed`, `TelltaleDNSClusterPinned` with promtool tests. UI: `RolloutPanel` (pin banner with what it holds back and Unpin; the bake with canaries, time left, readings, Promote and Abort; the versions table with Pin and a dry-run preview), canary badges and dashed canaries in the topology, Settings → Cluster → Staged rollouts (a canary picker with node, site, and resolver-pod labels and a sentence on who gets changes first). Help topics `rollouts`, `pins`; `running.md` *Rolling a change out in stages*, *Pinning the cluster to a version*; configuration reference; site how-it-works, monitoring, agents; `spec/12` §4.1. Tests: `clu_013_guard_compares_the_share_before_and_during_the_bake`, `…_too_little_traffic_passes_unless_traffic_is_required`, `…_immediate_failures`, `…_canary_selection`, `…_version_history_rotates_and_protects_its_blobs`, `…_the_diff_between_two_versions`, `…_the_canary_head_reaches_only_its_peers`, `…_rollout_and_pin_fields_default`, `…_blobs_link_from_snapshot_files`, `…_lag_counts_stable_versions_only`, `…_rollout_settings_are_shared`, `…_pinned_and_stuck_rollouts_degrade`, `…_rollout_failed_and_cluster_pinned`, `…_versions_parse`; `deploy/cluster/rollout-e2e.sh` (CI; ordering, the guard pinning a SERVFAIL-ing version 5 s into a 20 s bake with 0 lost queries and the plain replica never running it, 409 with the diff, unpin and fix, a manual pin, failover keeping the pin); `upgrade-e2e.sh` step 5 (the edge build as a replica named a canary still gets stable versions only); `helm/scaled-e2e.sh` step 6 on kind; MCP e2e step 7; Playwright `clu_013 staged rollouts: pick a canary in Settings`, `clu_013 the cluster page shows the pin, the versions, and the bake`. Gate on the VM: 728 tests, 48 Playwright, cluster/failover/git/rotate e2e pass; bench-smoke cache-hot 520k q/s, miss-heavy p50 247 µs / p99 391 µs (no query-path change). Stripped binary +0.69 MB (30.36 → 31.05 MB). ADR-116 amended (still Proposed). Not done: SLO burn in the guard; per-change provenance (user, agent) in the version history; rewriting the source back to a pinned version (the diff says what to change).

(2) Removing the MAC from a fixture device lowers its confidence without changing the product; removing its domains leaves `unknown` with vendor. (3) `[[client]] kind` wins over any inference and shows as `source = "override"`. (4) A group with `device_classes = ["camera"]` makes the camera's identity carry `suggestedGroup`; a device already in that group gets none. (5) OUI: `presets/build-oui.py` is reproducible (same CSV → same bytes), the table resolves 10 known prefixes incl. one MA-M, and its compressed size is reported in the PR against the image gate. (6) Per-device state ≤ 1 KiB, the 10-minute pass over 1,024 devices takes < 50 ms in a release build with the aggregator's top lists, and the query-log fallback runs at idle priority with at most 8 devices per pass. (7) Privacy level 1 disables it and the API says so. (8) Federation: two nodes seeing the same device agree after merge (highest confidence). (9) Playwright: a device that queried roku.com names in the test shows "Looks like a Roku player" on Clients, and the naming form suggests it. MA-M), `obs_025_oui_builder_is_reproducible`, `obs_025_the_catalog_ships_40_signatures`, `obs_025_identities_are_byte_identical_to_the_golden_file` (`testdata/identify-golden.json`), `obs_025_fixture_products_and_ablations`, `obs_025_vendor_penalty_and_ties`, `obs_025_overrides_and_group_suggestions`, `obs_025_local_signatures_replace_and_remove`, `obs_025_per_device_state_is_small`, `obs_025_merge_keeps_the_highest_confidence`, `obs_025_off_at_privacy_level_1_and_kept_across_restarts`, `obs_025_a_pass_over_1024_devices_is_fast`, `obs_025_identity_routes`, `obs_025_identity_routes_need_analytics`; Playwright `obs_025 a device that talks to roku.com looks like a Roku player`.
