- [x] **T13.4 Node maintenance mode.** *(OPS-010, CLU-008, OBS-010, OBS-015, ADR-118; design `docs/design/maintenance-mode.md`)* `maintenance.json` state with expiry; readiness; the heartbeat field; alert and health exclusion; candidacy; a primary's handover under automatic failover (`handover`, default on; owner 2026-10-09: "follow your lead"); `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/nodes/{id}/maintenance` (RPC to the target node); `telltale ctl maintenance`; Cluster page action and banner; MCP `start_maintenance`/`end_maintenance`; alert rule `maintenance`; audit; metrics.
  - *AC:* (1) Entering maintenance flips `/readyz` to 503 within 1 s while `dig` through every listener keeps answering (unit + the cluster e2e under load: 0 lost queries across enter, 30 s, exit). (2) Restarting the node inside the window comes back with `/readyz` 503 and the same `until`; after expiry it's ready without intervention. (3) In the cluster e2e, a replica in maintenance that is then SIGSTOPped raises no `node_down` alert and the health level stays `healthy` with the node listed under `maintenance`; SIGCONT and ending maintenance restores normal evaluation (a later stop raises `node_down`). (4) A node in maintenance isn't elected: with auto failover and a witness, killing the primary while the only eligible replica is in maintenance elects nobody until maintenance ends (sim or e2e step). (4b) Handover: in the failover e2e, starting maintenance on the primary (auto mode, an eligible replica up) elects the replica within the lease window with 100 % of DNS answered and the old primary following; with `handover = false`, or in manual mode, the primary stays primary and the response carries `note: "promote another node first…"`. (5) `forSecs` > 86,400 and a missing reason are 422; an agent token asking for 3 h gets 422 with the 2 h cap in the hint. (6) The request on any node reaches the target node (e2e: start on the primary for the replica, the replica's file and readiness change); a target that's down answers 503. (7) Audit entries on the target node name the user, the reason, and the window; the once-only `maintenance` alert rule fires on entry and resolves on exit (promtool for the chart's copy if one is added; unit test for the built-in rule). (8) Playwright: start maintenance from the Cluster page, see the banner and the health note, end it. (9) Docs (`running.md` *Taking a node down for maintenance*, Kubernetes note on the single readiness probe), help topic, site clusters page, `spec/08` §9.
  - *Done 2026-10-09:* `crates/telltale/src/readiness.rs` (`Readiness`: bound, synced, stopping, maintenance; `serving()` for heartbeats, probes, and `not_serving`, `ready()` for `/readyz`, `started()` for `/readyz?startup=1`), `crates/telltale/src/maintenance.rs` (`Window` in `maintenance.json`, `Maintenance::open/start/end/expire`, `spawn_expiry` with an `expired` audit, `handover_plan`, `apply_here`, the `maint.set`/`maint.clear` RPC with a 30-s staleness cut and one retry), `/readyz` answers JSON with the reason; heartbeat tags 20–23 and `Member::maintenance`/`MaintenanceWindow`, `Cluster::set_maintenance`/`in_maintenance`/`stepping_down`/`give_up_handover` (60 s), `Elector::standing`/`stepping_down`; `telltale_api::maintenance_api` (`POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/nodes/{id}/maintenance`, `check_reason`, `check_secs`, agents ≤ 7,200 s), `MaintenanceWrite`, `Backend::maintenance` (local, and federated to the target node; `node_unknown`, `node_unreachable`), problem codes `maintenance_too_long`/`maintenance_invalid`/`node_unreachable`/`node_unknown`, scope `ops:maintenance`, MCP `start_maintenance`/`end_maintenance`; `NodeMaintenance` on `ClusterNode`, `SystemInfo`, `Health.maintenance`, `AlertsStatus.maintenance`; `health::exclude_maintenance`, `alerts::Paused` (per-node rules, the SERVFAIL share through `Federated::excluding`, "(maintenance)" resolutions, `AlertWhen::Maintenance`), the Cluster page's checks skip the node; `[node] maintenance_default_secs`; `telltale ctl maintenance start|end|status`; `telltale_node_maintenance`, `_until_seconds`, `_total`, `telltale_cluster_peer_maintenance_until_seconds`; UI `MaintenanceForm.svelte`, the Cluster page's per-node action, badge, banner, and standalone card, the hatched topology node, the app banner, the health panel's note, the Alerts page's line, help topic `maintenance`; chart: startup probes on `/readyz?startup=1`, node rules `unless` an open window (`last_over_time`), `NOTES.txt`; Grafana **Maintenance** timeline. Tests: `ops_010_*` (readiness, window file and expiry, forwarded requests, `/readyz` and metrics, requests and scope, HTTP with an agent token, wire, election (never stands, hands over), config, alerts, health, cluster checks, ctl), the simulator's maintenance properties (both mutation-checked: a renewing stepping-down primary and a standing maintenance node each fail it; 10,000 schedules pass), promtool cases (a node stopped inside its window stays quiet, alerts after), Playwright `ops_010 maintenance from the Cluster page`, `deploy/cluster/e2e.sh` (3,628 of 3,628 queries answered across enter, 30 s, exit; same `until` after a restart; no `node_down` while stopped, health unchanged with the node listed; a later stop raises `node_down`; a timed-out request isn't applied later; the primary in manual mode stays primary with the note; 422s and 404) and `deploy/cluster/failover-e2e.sh` (nobody elected for 40 s with the only eligible replica in maintenance, then it is; the handover elects the replica in 16 s with 1,945 of 1,945 queries to the old primary answered; `handover: false` keeps the lease). Bench-smoke on the VM (nothing on the query path changed): before 682,791 q/s cache-hot, miss-heavy p50 223 µs / p99 383 µs; after 646,576 q/s, 279 µs / 407 µs, within the VM's usual spread. **Found and fixed (CLU-005):** a key share refused from an old primary that came back ended the read side of its stream, so its answers to RPCs were lost (seen in the failover e2e); the key is now ignored and the stream kept (`clu_005_only_voters_can_fence_the_primary` covers it). ADR-118 amended (startup probe, readiness composition, `declined`, `maintenance_invalid`, handover give-up, RPC shape, chart expressions). CI's `native` job timeout raised to 45 minutes. *Not done:* the cluster e2e can't show the level as `healthy` (its upstream is deliberately unreachable, so the level is `severe` throughout); it checks the level doesn't change and the node's reasons are gone, and `ops_010_health_leaves_out_nodes_in_maintenance` checks `healthy`.

## M14 follow-ups (owner request 2026-10-17)
The proposals from M14's reports the owner chose to build (2026-10-17: "keep going with #2"): a display token for the wall (owner's choice over longer viewer sessions), an informational "chatty loop" finding (owner's choice), identity changes on the device timeline, the stale-SERVFAIL fix, and the demo screenshots. Same rules as M14.

- [x] **T14.10 Never serve an expired SERVFAIL stale.** *(DNS-006, DNS-007, ADR-137)* An expired SERVFAIL entry is a miss and `get_stale` skips it.
  - *AC:* a SERVFAIL for a PTR name is a hit for 5 s, then a miss with nothing to serve stale, with `stale_max_age` 3600; an expired A answer is still `Expired` (servable stale).
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* `Entry::is_servfail` (the stored answer's RCODE); `Cache::get` returns `Miss` for an expired SERVFAIL (and drops it), `Cache::get_stale` skips it. Tests: `dns_007_an_expired_servfail_is_never_served_stale`; `dns_006_negative_and_servfail_caching` now expects `Miss` after 5 s. Gate on the VM: 793 tests, 60 Playwright, clippy; bench-smoke cache-hot 446k q/s (the hit path is unchanged; today's runs 445k–538k), miss-heavy p99 463 µs.
- [x] **T14.11 The "chatty loop" finding.** *(OBS-036, ADR-138)* Informational: a tracked pair queried at one steady rhythm faster than every 30 s for an hour; not in the badge or the `anomaly` alert; `when = "chatty_loop"`.
  - *AC:* a 2.5 s loop for 2 hours is one finding (window 03:00, typical gap 2.3–2.7 s, strip kept); after an hour and a half quiet and an hour back, a second one; a browser's gaps never are; the golden fixture is unchanged; `anomaly` alerts skip it and `chatty_loop` takes only it; Playwright `obs_036 chatty loop` (informational card, strip, badge not counting it).
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* `Kind::ChattyLoop` (`chatty_loop`, `Kind::informational`), `Tracked::chatty_bin` / `chatty_reported`, `CHATTY_MIN_GAPS` 120, `CHATTY_SHARE_PCT` 80, `CHATTY_BINS` 1–8, checked in `close_pairs` before the rhythm folds; its strip kept like a beacon's; `AnomalyFinding.informational`; `AlertWhen::ChattyLoop` (the `anomaly` rule skips informational findings); the sidebar badge skips them; the Anomalies page marks them "for your information" with the strip. Tests: `obs_036_a_chatty_loop_is_reported_once_per_streak`, `obs_036_chatty_loops_alert_only_by_kind`, Playwright `obs_036 chatty loop`; the golden fixture unchanged. Gate on the VM (with T14.12, T14.13): 800 tests, 63 Playwright, clippy; bench-smoke cache-hot 532k q/s, miss-heavy p99 463 µs.

Tests: `obs_034_identity_changes`, `obs_034_identity_change_event`, Playwright `obs_034 device timeline` (now with an identity event); `obs_025_per_device_state_is_small` holds with the two fields at their longest.
- [x] **T14.13 Display token for the wall.** *(OBS-035, ADR-140)* A revocable display link (`#/display?code=…`) exchanged for a wall-only session, optionally limited to some networks; Settings → Users → Wall displays. Also (owner request 2026-10-17) the Wall entry moved to an icon button in the header between pause and the theme.

- [x] **T14.15 Wall layouts for the screen.** *(OBS-035, ADR-141; owner request 2026-10-17)* Screen classes, templates, `chooseLayout`, a `kpi` panel, `?layout=`, one animation loop, panels sized to their slot.
  - *Done 2026-11-18 (upstream #69):* the wall's slot effect depended on `layout`, which `chooseLayout` rebuilt on every measure or resize of the area, resetting the rotation index; it is now keyed on `slotKey` and `paramsKey`, so a remeasure with the same panels keeps its place. The regression guard is Playwright `obs_035 wall keeps its place when remeasured` (fails 3 of 3 without the fix); the original `obs_035 wall` loop passed without the fix too, so it is not evidence, and now waits for the first measure and asserts `data-panel` after each key. The link from this mechanism to the reported flake is plausible, not proven. No new setting, route or tool, so no new UI control or help topic.
  - *Open (2026-11-18, upstream #69 review): the `display-revoke` / `display-row` e2e check (one row left after revoke, 8 s) failed once under heavy load and did not reproduce in 40 runs; not investigated. A remeasure that crosses a screen-class boundary in `auto` mode still resets the rotation, because the slots really change.*
  - *AC:* the class of 390 × 844, 1080 × 1920, 1920 × 1080, 2560 × 1080, 2560 × 1440 is compact, portrait, standard, ultrawide, large; across sizes every slot fits its panel, none overlap, the area is filled, every panel is placed; a TV puts the map in the hero and `kpi`, `slo` beside it; portrait's lower slot gets a big panel; a large screen shows six with none rotating and the tail beside them; Playwright `obs_035 wall layouts` at five sizes (class, slots, no scrollbar).
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* `ui/src/lib/wall.ts`: `SCREEN_CLASSES`, `screenClass`, `screenClassStable` (4 % hysteresis), `PANEL_MIN`, `TEMPLATES`, `parseLayout`, `chooseLayout`, `FrameHub` / `frameHub`; `Wall.svelte` places slots (class from the whole wall, slots from the panel area), per-slot rotation, captions in multi-slot layouts, the tail as a column on large screens; `WallKpi.svelte`; `WallMap` and `WallFlow` on the shared loop; the flow's labels hidden under 600 px; the SLO rings sized to their slot; display sessions may read `/system/health`. Tests: `obs_035 wall helpers` (classes, hysteresis, six sizes for fit, overlap, fill and placement, the TV and portrait choices, the large screen's six, the hub), `obs_035 wall` (rotation checks under `layout=single`), `obs_035 wall layouts` (five browser sizes), `obs_035_display_sessions_read_only_the_wall` (health allowed). Gate on the VM: 800 tests, 64 Playwright, clippy; UI 203.0 KiB gzipped. *Not done:* screenshots of each layout for the site (the next demo run).
- [x] **T14.14 Demo screenshots.** *(DOC-006)* The pack's views and the wall, from the demo network only.

Reviewing them found three UI fixes, made here: the device timeline opens scrolled to now and gains a 1 h window; the wall's SLO needle (it read as a minus sign beside the number) is replaced by the burn rate in words, amber from 1× and red from 6×. *Not used:* the timeline (10 minutes of demo data on an hour's scale) and the SLO wall shot (taken before the needle fix).

## M15 — Kubernetes workloads and failover drills (owner request 2026-10-18)
Two tasks from the design session of 2026-10-18, built in order T15.1 → T15.2 (independent; T15.2 gains from T15.1). Shared rules: nothing unpaid on the query path (A/B `make bench-smoke` and `bench.py sustain` on the build VM), DNS never depends on the Kubernetes API, a peer, or a report file (CLU-004), every new read federated with `missingNodes`, names at read time, privacy levels and exclusions, agent-ready APIs with MCP tools, no new `ui/` runtime dependency (both tasks under 25 KB gzipped), chart defaults unchanged for existing installs. Never touch the live nodes without the owner's go.

- [x] **T15.1 Kubernetes workloads as devices.** *(FLT-017, OBS-025, API-010, ADR-150; design `docs/design/kubernetes-workloads.md`)* A read-only pod/namespace watcher on the controller (no client crate), the `Workloads` table distributed to peers (`workloads.table`), `IdSource::Workload` in `ClientTable::identify`, groups from labels and `[[kubernetes.map]]`, a synthetic analytics key per workload, time-aware names at read time, `GET /api/v1/kubernetes/workloads` and `/status`, MCP `list_workloads` / `get_workload`, the Clients page's **Workloads** tab and chip glyph, chart values (`kubernetes.*`, `service.dns.clusterIP`), metrics, `kubernetes_stale` health, `TelltaleDNSKubernetesWatchStale`.
  - *AC:* (1) **Parser:** the seven fixture pods map to `media/deploy/jellyfin`, `db/sts/postgres`, `kube-system/ds/cilium`, `jobs/cronjob/backup`, `tools/job/once`, `tools/pod/debug`; the host-network and terminating pods are skipped; a pod with two IPs maps both. (2) **Identification:** with a loaded table, a query from a pod address gets `IdSource::Workload`, the group by pod label > namespace label > map rule > `default_group` > network group, and explain names the workload, the pod, and the group's origin; an empty table takes the existing path unchanged (T2.5's precedence tests pass); a `[[client]]` keyed by a pod address loses to the workload. (3) **Analytics:** 1,000 events from three pod addresses of one Deployment give one device in top clients, one anomaly baseline, one identity (`class: workload`, `certain`), one new-device entry; log rows carry pod addresses and are named by the workload at read time; after a simulated rollout that reuses the addresses for another workload, old rows name the old workload and new rows the new one (`device_name_at`). (4) **Distribution** (`deploy/cluster/e2e.sh`): a fixture table loaded on the primary through a test hook reaches the replica within 5 s; a replica started afterwards pulls it within 60 s; a peer without the call is reported missing, not an error. (5) **Watcher** against an in-test fake API server: initial list, ADDED and DELETED update the table, `410 Gone` re-lists, a token file change is picked up, errors increment the metric, and a DNS query is answered under a failing watcher in the same test. (6) **Chart:** `ct lint`; with `kubernetes.enabled: true` only the controller pod mounts the token; the ClusterRole has exactly `pods` and `namespaces` with `get/list/watch`; `enabled: true` without `clusterDns` fails schema validation; `deploy/helm/e2e.sh` on kind: a busybox pod with `dnsConfig` pointing at the Service resolves an external name, its log row names `default/pod/busybox`, and `kubernetes.default.svc.cluster.local` resolves through the forward. (7) **UI:** Playwright `flt_017 workloads`: the tab lists the fixture workloads with group origin; "Add to group…" writes a map rule (dry run first) and the next query is in that group; the chip shows the glyph and the pod name on hover. (8) **Bench:** empty-table A/B within noise; 1,000 pod addresses loaded, cache-hit path within 2 %; `bench.py sustain` at 50k q/s with 0 drops and CPU reported; RSS before/after (table + history under 300 KB for 1,000 pods). (9) **Docs:** `running.md` section with both wirings and the k3s snippet; site page and diagram; help topic `kubernetes-workloads`; glossary "workload"; OpenAPI and MCP regenerated; ADR-150 and FLT-017 written; this box ticked.
  - *Done (2026-10-19, local commit, not pushed):* (1)–(3), (5) by the `flt_017_*` unit and integration tests, AC 3 as `flt_017_a_deployment_is_one_device`. (2) **Lifecycle:** `forSecs` 301 → 422; a start in maintenance → 409 `drill_refused`; a second start → 409 `drill_active`; `DELETE` ends it and the report still runs; a restart cancels it (readiness back, nothing dropped, `telltale_drills_total{result="cancelled"}`); expiry ends it; `drill.start` and `drill.end` audited with actor and reason; an agent without `ops:drill` → 403. ## M16 — Community requests (2026-11-14)

- [x] **T16.1 Stub zones.** *(DNS-022, UPS-006, UPS-007, ADR-153)* A `recursive://` upstream with `stub_zone` + `stub_servers` starts names at or under the zone at those servers (RD=0) and follows their delegations; health checks ask a stub upstream its zone's SOA; forwards take `stub: true` (API, MCP `plan_forward_domain`, the UI's checkbox).
  - *AC:* (1) with the public example.com cut cached, names under a stub `corp.example.com` and its delegated `sub.corp.example.com` resolve from the stub's servers only, and its NXDOMAIN is theirs; (2) stub names don't prime the root, and a CNAME out of the zone is chased from the root without the zone's names reaching it; (3) a stub upstream's health question is the zone's SOA, a plain one's `. NS`, and its probe counts a REFUSED reply as up and silence as down; (4) a stub zone without servers, servers without a zone, or stub keys on a non-recursive upstream are refused at their paths; (5) a stub forward merges into one `recursive://` upstream with the stub fields, a non-address server is refused, and the stored entry keeps the flag.
  - *Done 2026-11-14:* (1) `dns_022_stub_zone_follows_its_own_delegations`; (2) `dns_022_stub_zone_cname_leaves_the_zone`; (3) `dns_022_stub_upstream_health_question`, `dns_022_stub_probe_counts_any_answer`; (4) `dns_022_stub_zone_validates`; (5) `dns_022_stub_forward_merges`, and the stub case of `api_002_keep_in_git_toml` (the stored entry, the change it shows, and the Git TOML keep the flag; a non-address server is refused). Docs: `docs/running.md` "Stub zones", the Encrypted DNS and upstreams guide; OpenAPI, MCP tools, config schema, and UI types regenerated.
  - *Found along the way, fixed:* a `recursive://` upstream asked for bootstrap servers (its placeholder host looked like a name to resolve), so it failed to build wherever `/etc/resolv.conf` lists only loopback resolvers and TelltaleDNS listens on `0.0.0.0:53` (systemd-resolved, Docker's embedded DNS, or TelltaleDNS as the host's own resolver).
  - *Deferred:* NS priming from the stub servers (BIND `type stub`), ports other than 53, the Technitium importer's stub zones, and an apex-SOA health check for plain forwards.

## M17 — Supportability (owner request 2026-11-15)

- [x] **T17.1 Support package.** *(OPS-011, API-003, AGT-001; GitHub issue #50)* A redacted diagnostics archive from the CLI, the API, and the System page: build and platform facts, the effective configuration without secrets, the cluster view and timeline, health checks, listener and upstream state, list counts, a metrics snapshot, aggregate statistics, recent logs (a `tracing` layer keeps a bounded in-memory ring and two rotating files of about 1 MiB under `<data_dir>/logs/`, since the process can't read its stderr back on any platform), and an opt-in query-log sample; three obfuscation levels (`strict` by default, then `standard` and `none`) with stable per-package pseudonyms; a manifest with per-category redaction counts. Design: ADR-156 (Accepted 2026-11-16).
  - *AC:* (1) a fixture with every kind of secret planted (password hashes, API, session, and join tokens, TOTP secret, OIDC secret, node and CA private keys, bootstrap secret, SMTP, webhook, and Git credentials) produces a package, in each level, that contains none of them (a scan for each planted value and for the PEM and token shapes); (2) at `strict` (the default), every planted IP, MAC, host, device, client, group, node, pod, namespace, e-mail, and home path is replaced, the same input always by the same pseudonym, two addresses of one /24 stay in one /24, private stays private, loopback, link-local, and the well-known public resolvers are unchanged, no query name appears anywhere, and only WARN and ERROR log lines are present; at `standard` INFO lines and the opt-in query-log sample appear with their names pseudonymized; at `none` real values are kept (the secrets are still gone), and the query-log sample is refused at `strict`; (3) `--reveal NAME` keeps that name and only it, `--keep-map` writes the map outside the package, and the API's map download works once, only for the package just made, only for an admin, and not after 10 minutes; (4) the manifest lists every file with its redaction counts and the level, and the counts match a recount of the fixture; (5) the CLI and the API produce the same package, `--list` writes nothing, a size over the cap is truncated and says so, and a slow or failing collector marks its file as missing instead of failing the package; (6) the redactor never panics and never leaves a planted value on arbitrary text (a fuzz target); (7) the log layer keeps the last 2,000 lines in memory and two rotating files of about 1 MiB under `<data_dir>/logs/`, lines from before a restart reach the package, a full channel drops and counts instead of blocking, and a read-only data directory falls back to memory; (8) the bug-report issue form asks for it, and `docs/running.md`, the site, and OpenAPI are done (it is not an MCP tool).
  - *Decided (owner, 2026-11-15):* default level `strict`, with fuller logs and the query-log sample opt-in; the query-log sample is offered; no MCP tool for now; the UI offers the pseudonym map as a short-lived second download (in memory, gone after the first download or 10 minutes); the log files default to on (`[support] persist_logs`).
  - *Progress 2026-11-16:* phase 1 done: `support_log` (a `tracing` layer: a ring of 2,000 INFO+ lines from the first line on, two rotating files of about 1 MiB under `<data_dir>/logs/` once the data directory is locked, lines of the last run read back at start, a bounded channel that drops and counts, a read-only directory leaving the ring, flushed at shutdown) and `[support] persist_logs`, node-local and restart-only; the console keeps following `TELLTALE_LOG`. AC (7) is covered. Next: the archive writer, redaction, pseudonyms, and the classification table; then the collectors and the three surfaces.
  - *Progress 2026-11-16 (phase 2):* the pure core is the new `telltale-support` crate: a ustar and gzip writer (`archive`), keyed pseudonyms with Crypto-PAn style address permutation that keeps subnets, private ranges, public-ness, loopback, link-local and the well-known resolvers (`pseudo`), the two-pass redactor (`redact`: one multi-pattern search for known names and inline secrets, then shape scanners for addresses, MACs, e-mail, URLs, host names, home paths, PEM blocks and token-looking runs; nothing is scanned twice), configuration classification that fails closed (`classify`: a class table for every string field of the generated schema, numbers, flags and fixed words plain by type, an unclassified field dropped and listed, credential-looking key names dropped as a second net, and tests that fail when a schema field has no class or a table entry matches no field), the manifest and the package builder with the size cap (`manifest`, `package`), and the fuzz target `support_redact` in the nightly workflow. AC (1), (2), (4), part of (5) and (6) are covered at the library level. Next: the collectors, the CLI, the API and the System page.
  - *Done 2026-11-16 (phase 3):* the server side is `support.rs` (collectors on their own threads with a 5 s deadline each and 30 s overall, one package at a time, the pseudonym map held in memory for one download within 10 minutes, an offline builder) behind `POST /api/v1/system/support-package` (admin; `?dryRun=true` returns the manifest) and `GET /api/v1/system/support-package/{id}/map`, audited as `support.package` and `support.package.map`, with no agent scope and no MCP tool; the command is `telltale support-package` (`--level`, `--reveal`, `--query-sample`, `--keep-map`, `--list`, `-o`, `--offline`; a running node by API token, else the config files and log files; the file is owner-only and never replaces one); the System tab has a Support package card (preview, download, the map once); the bug report issue form asks for it; `docs/running.md`, the help topic, the site's FAQ and architecture page, OpenAPI and the UI types are done. A real-binary check on the build VM found one leak the unit tests didn't (a DNS provider's profile ID in an endpoint URL's path, which only token-shaped path pieces were scrubbed from) and fixed it: at strict and standard a URL path keeps lowercase words and turns anything with a digit or a capital into `<id>`.
  - *Open:* a `--cluster` collection (ask each node over the cluster channel, redacting on the node: ADR-156's later phase; GitHub issue #55).

## M31 — CMDB: configuration items from many sources (owner request 2026-11-18) [fork #21]

One configuration item (CI) per real thing on the network, built from every source that reports on it: UniFi, Home Assistant, SNMP, imported scans, Pi-hole imports, DHCP leases, query attribution and Kubernetes. Each source's record is kept and linked to its CI, the owner declares the definitive source per attribute, and changes go through a MACD lifecycle (move, add, change, delete). It builds on M25's inventory (T25.1, keyed by MAC, ADR-910) and keeps M25's rules: read-only towards devices, off the DNS path, bounded storage, privacy levels respected, one management plane in a cluster. The standing rule applies: every setting and action ships with a UI control, a help topic and a Playwright test.

- [ ] **T31.1 Design.** *(new DSC-9xx requirements; ADR)* Design only. The CI types and relations (device, interface, network/VLAN, TelltaleDNS node, service, upstream; on network, attached to port, member of group); the source record (as reported, first and last seen, source and its identity); reconciliation (match keys in order: MAC, serial, router client ID, hostname plus network; manual link and unlink; how M25's MAC merge becomes the first rule); the definitive-source model (a precedence per CI type and attribute, a per-CI override, manual values as a source, a conflict view where a source disagrees with the definitive value, what happens when the definitive source goes silent); MACD (the change record and its states requested, approved, done and rejected; who may approve; changes detected from sources, such as a new device, a moved port or a disappeared device, raised as a MACD to confirm; history and audit); storage and retention, the API and MCP tools (read for agents, writes plan-only), federation in a cluster, and the test plan. Batch the owner decisions it needs into one AskUserQuestion at the milestone start. Then refine T31.2 to T31.6 into numbered steps with acceptance criteria.
- [ ] **T31.2 CIs, source records and reconciliation.** After T31.1. The store, the ingestion from each existing source (M22 routers, M25 inventory and imports, leases, the Pi-hole importer), matching, and the manual link and unlink in the UI.
- [ ] **T31.3 Definitive sources and conflicts.** After T31.2. The precedence settings (UI), the per-CI override, the merged view with each value's source, and the conflict view.
- [ ] **T31.4 MACD lifecycle.** After T31.2. Change records, states, approval, history and audit; changes detected from sources raised as MACDs; alerts for unconfirmed changes.
- [ ] **T31.5 API, MCP and the CI pages.** After T31.3 and T31.4. The CI list and detail pages (sources side by side, and the device's effective policy from T24.30: its filters, profile and rules), the API and MCP read tools, plan-only writes.
- [ ] **T31.6 Closing.** Docs, help review, site page, the cluster check in `deploy/cluster3/e2e.sh`, and the README fork block.

## M30 — Query-log negation and a cluster-wide support package (GitHub issues #58 and #55; upstream M24)
  - *Upstream numbering (sync, 2026-11-18, ADR-986):* upstream's M24, T24.1 and T24.2 are M30, T30.1 and T30.2 here (the fork's M24 is the Pi-hole milestone), and upstream's T22.2 (Bootstrap Icons) is T29.2; upstream's ADR-170 to 172 keep their numbers.

- [x] **T30.1 Search the query log for what does not match.** *(OBS-003, OBS-008, API-005, AGT-012; ADR-171; GitHub issue #58)* A "not" per filter (name in each of its five matches, client, status, type, response code): the store, `GET /api/v1/queries`, the Live stream, `telltale qlog search`, and the Query log page.
  - *AC:* (1) a negated search returns exactly the rows a full scan finds that do not satisfy the filter, for every kind of name match and for client, status, type, and response code, and a filter and its negation add up to the whole window; (2) a block is skipped for a negated status or response code only when everything in it is excluded, and a negated name or client uses no bloom filter; (3) a flag without its filter is refused with a message that names it, on the API and the command line; (4) the Live view honours the flags; (5) an older peer is listed as not reached instead of answering un-negated rows (new reads `QueriesNegated`, `TailOpenNegated`); (6) the Query log page reads and writes the flags in its address; (7) OpenAPI, the UI types, the MCP schema, `docs/running.md`, and a Playwright test are updated.
  - *Done 2026-11-17:* (1) the store: `obs_003_negated_filters_match_exactly_what_a_scan_would` (every kind of name match) and `obs_003_negated_client_status_type_and_rcode_match_exactly_what_a_scan_would` (client, status, type, response code, a combination, and a negation with nothing to negate) compare each negated search with a scan of all 40,000 rows; (2) `obs_003_negated_status_skips_only_blocks_it_excludes_entirely` (an all-blocked block is skipped from its header: one block read, none when every status is listed) and a negated exact name reads blocks where a positive one reads none; (3) `check_negations` on `GET /queries` and the stream (each flag refused without its filter, checked in Playwright for all five) and `obs_003_cli_negation_needs_its_filter` for the command line; (4) `obs_008_negated_filters` for the Live view (every name match but regex, which it never had); (5) the reads `QueriesNegated` and `TailOpenNegated`, which an older peer doesn't know; (6) the Query log page (the Match menu, the not boxes, the not chip) and the Playwright test `obs_003 the query log can keep what a filter does not match`: 82 Playwright tests in 1-ui.spec.ts (the 81 of before and this one) and the 13 in the other specs passed on the VM; (7) OpenAPI, the UI types, the MCP `search_queries` schema, `docs/running.md`, spec/06 and spec/13.
  - *Not run:* a mixed-version cluster (an older node asked for a negated search): the unknown-read behaviour is the existing one for newer reads, and `deploy/cluster/e2e.sh` was not run.
  - *Accepted:* ADR-171, by the owner on 2026-11-17.
- [x] **T30.2 A support package for the whole cluster.** *(OPS-011, CLU-002; ADR-172; GitHub issue #55)* `--cluster` / `cluster: true` / a System page checkbox: every reachable node makes and redacts its own part under one key made by the node that was asked; one package, with each node, and one label per device across nodes.
  - *AC:* (1) a device, group, or host has the same label in every node's files, in any order of meeting, and another label under another key; (2) no real name or address leaves a node (each part is redacted on its node; the key is sent over the cluster stream and never stored); (3) a node that doesn't answer, is older, is busy, or is not connected is listed with a fixed reason, and the rest of the package is complete; (4) the 20 MiB cap is shared in equal parts and a part is cut to fit one cluster answer, the manifest saying what was cut; (5) an ephemeral member can be asked but cannot ask; (6) `keepMap` with `cluster` is refused; (7) OpenAPI, `docs/running.md`, and the command line (`--cluster`, `--list`) are updated.
  - *Done 2026-11-17:* (1) `ops_011_keyed_labels_are_the_same_whatever_the_order` (one device, one label, in any order and under one key; another key, another label; counted labels unchanged) and `ops_011_a_device_has_one_label_on_every_node_and_no_real_value_leaves_a_node`; (2) each node redacts its own part (the same redactor and classification as a single-node package) and the key travels only in the cluster read and is hidden from `Debug`; (3) `ops_011_a_cluster_package_lists_every_node_and_names_the_ones_that_are_missing` (present, missing, and over-share nodes, fixed reasons), and the manifest's `nodes` table on the System page; (4) the cap is shared in equal parts (`ops_011_a_part_is_cut_to_fit_the_wire`, `ops_011_a_part_survives_the_wire_and_a_bad_one_is_refused`, an unpack limit); (5) an ephemeral member can be asked and cannot ask (the handler's refusal); (6) `ops_011_a_cluster_package_has_no_map_to_keep` (400, and `--keep-map` with `--cluster` an error); (7) paths from another node are checked (`ops_011_a_node_cannot_write_outside_its_folder`: `..`, absolute, hidden parts, backslashes, drive colons), OpenAPI, `docs/running.md`, the command line (`--cluster`, `--list`), and the Playwright test `ops_011 support package for the whole cluster`. Verified on the VM on 2026-11-17 from a clean sync: the whole UI suite (83 tests, including the new cluster checkbox test), the icons, markers, and markup specs (13), and the two-node cluster script with its new step, which reported that both nodes made their parts, one package, and pseudonymous node names. An earlier run of both was discarded because a stale script overwrote the VM tree during it.
  - *Not run:* a cluster with a node older than this change (it answers an error to the unknown read, so it is listed as missing), more than two nodes, and resolver pods; a part that really exceeds the cluster channel's size (the cut is unit-tested).
  - *Accepted:* ADR-172, by the owner on 2026-11-17.

## M28 — A leaner image (owner request 2026-11-17)
  - *Upstream numbering (sync, 2026-11-17):* upstream's T23.1–3 are T28.1–3 here, and upstream's T22.1 is T29.1; upstream's ADR-166–170 keep their numbers.

- [x] **T28.1 Three size cuts: the OpenAPI document compiled in, a gzip-only embedded UI, no CA bundle.** *(NFR-001, API-001, API-005, OPS-001; ADR-168)* At 13bb27d the images were 14.84, 14.16, and 14.82 MiB (amd64, arm64, armv7) of the 15 MiB gate, so the next feature the size of the domain icons would have failed it. A measured review (Opus agent, 2026-11-17) ranked what to cut; the owner chose these three, none on the DNS path.
  - *AC:* (1) `GET /api/v1/openapi.json` is byte for byte `docs/api/openapi.json`, and the drift test still builds the document from the code; (2) the UI is embedded as `.gz` files only: a browser gets the stored file with `Content-Encoding: gzip`, any other client the inflated file, and a damaged file is refused; (3) the image carries no CA bundle and no code reads one; (4) the image size gate passes with at least 0.9 MiB more headroom on amd64 than before; (5) clippy, the Rust tests, and the Playwright suite pass.
  - *Done 2026-11-17:* (1) `openapi_document()` includes the committed file, the generator is a test-only function, and the test `api_001_the_served_openapi_is_the_committed_file`; (2) `ui/scripts/postbuild.mjs` (replaces each text file with its `.gz`), `ui.rs` (`gunzip`, the test `nfr_001_gunzip_reads_what_postbuild_writes`), and the e2e test `api_005 the UI is served gzipped, and plain to a client that does not accept gzip`; (3) the Dockerfile; (5) clippy clean, 1,030 Rust tests, 80 Playwright on the VM (including the new gzip-and-plain test), generated files current. (4) is read from the size gate of the CI image job on this commit (the numbers are in its log).
  - *Measured:* the size gate of the CI image job on 3027e71 printed 13.83 (amd64), 13.08 (arm64), and 13.54 MiB (armv7), against 14.84, 14.16, and 14.82 before: 1.0, 1.1, and 1.3 MiB gained, so (4) holds.
  - *Open:* the off-path crates at `opt-level = "s"` are T28.3; a slimmer zoneinfo and SQLite without its unused modules are T28.2; zstd layers (2.1 MiB) are declined for now (Docker before 23.0 can't pull them).

- [x] **T28.2 Two more size cuts: a compact zoneinfo and SQLite without its unused modules.** *(NFR-001, OPS-001, FLT-010; ADR-169)* The owner chose items 3 and 4 of the 2026-11-17 review once T28.1 had landed ("do items 3 and 4 too"). Neither is on the DNS path.
  - *AC:* (1) the image's zoneinfo is built once from `tzdata.zi` by `zic -b fat` (hard links, no `posix/` or `right/` trees) and the build fails if a zone name is lost; against the host's tzdata jiff reads every file, every name is present, and every zone's offset, abbreviation, and daylight-saving flag agree with the host's to 2075; (2) SQLite is built without FTS3, FTS5, the R-tree index, and dbstat, by the repository's cargo config so the tests run against the shipped build, and foreign keys on by default, the JSON functions, STAT4, and column metadata stay; (3) the image size gate passes with more headroom on every architecture than T28.1's 13.83, 13.08, and 13.54 MiB (expected: about 0.4 MiB on amd64); (4) clippy, the Rust tests, and the Playwright suite pass.
  - *Done 2026-11-17:* (1) the Dockerfile's `zic -b fat` step (built from the package's `tzdata.zi`, copied with `cp -a`, and the build fails if a zone name is lost) and `crates/telltale-config/tests/zoneinfo.rs`, which found that jiff refuses a slim `America/Ojinaga` (so the build is fat, not slim: ADR-169); (2) `.cargo/config.toml` (`LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS`) and `crates/telltale-store/tests/sqlite_build.rs`; (4) clippy clean, 1,034 Rust tests (the zoneinfo and SQLite ones included), 80 Playwright on the VM, generated files current. (3) a real amd64 image build on the VM the way CI does it printed 13.39 MiB, against 13.83 for T28.1 in CI; the CI size gate on this commit has the numbers for all three architectures.

  1. **Registry.** `telltale-config/src/guarded.rs`: one row per guarded setting (`id`, config paths, `mode` of `window`, `immediate`, `staged` or `action`, `weakens(old, new)`, a proof hook for `window` rows, dry-run checks), the table of ADR-956 §1. The rows for T27.14 to T27.17 are added by those tasks. `[auth.oidc] disable_local_login` and `allowed_admin_networks` are deliberately absent (file-only, "No UI" reasons, ADR-956 §1; `api_902_lockout_settings_are_file_only`). `node.data_dir` is present as a `staged` row (T27.17; `api_902_data_dir_is_guarded_and_staged`). `restart-now` is an `action` row with no config path (step 12).

  3. **Routes** (`/api/v1/guarded`): `GET` (set by file, pending, `pendingRestart`; `config:read`; never `previewId`, `confirmToken` or pending values), `POST /{id}/preview`, `POST /{id}`, `POST /pending/{id}/confirm`, `POST /pending/{id}/cancel`. Role and token checks as ADR-956 §3: admin session only; agent tokens 403 `agent_not_allowed`; other tokens 403 `reauth_required` with a hint; weakening needs a sign-in within 10 minutes; 5 failed confirmations in 10 minutes block the session (429).
  4. **Preview and apply.** The preview returns `previewId`, `phrase`, the effect, `weakening`, `restartRequired`, `wouldDisconnect` and warnings, and is bound to admin, node, setting, value hash and config version (5 minutes; 409 `preview_stale`). Apply checks `previewId` and `confirm` on the server and answers 200 `applied`, 202 `staged` or 202 `pending`.
  5. **Window state machine** on the target node: one pending change per node (409 `guarded_change_pending`); `[api] confirm_window_secs` (default 120, 30 to 600, file-only, added to the schema); the `deadlineSecs` measured on the monotonic clock; the single-use `confirmToken`; confirmation proved by a per-row hook that returns `new_socket`, `new_cert` or `proxy_list` (a TLS-only change gets a temporary parallel listener on a port named in the preview); a pending proxy list applies only to the confirm route until confirmed; rollback on expiry, cancel, failed commit or failed bind, with reasons; a restart inside the window starts with the previous values.
  6. **Staging and probation.** `staged` entries are written at once and listed as `pendingRestart` (the `data-dir` pointer is read first from the file-named directory's `state.db`, and the sidecar stays there); start-up marks staged and restored entries on probation, with the failed-start counter in the sidecar `<data_dir>/guarded-probation` (fsync before the guarded values apply); healthy means the state opened, the API listener bound, the node's own DNS probe answered and (for `secrets-key`) the store is unlocked, within `max(confirm_window_secs, 180)`; two failed starts drop them, but never back to the old key alone once any row was re-encrypted; a bind failure for a confirmed guarded listener falls back for that start (`guarded_bind_fallback`).
  7. **Cluster.** Only the primary forwards a request for another node over the cluster channel, with an assertion (admin, role, sign-in time, expiry) signed by the primary that the target verifies (a replica's is refused); the pending change and confirmation stay on the target; the entry is written through the primary (409 `primary_unreachable` when it can't be reached); no "all nodes" apply.
  8. **Audit and alerts.** `settings.guarded`, `settings.guarded_rolled_back` (reasons `expired`, `cancelled`, `commit_failed`, `bind_failed`, `failed_start`) and `settings.guarded_refused`; the alert condition `guarded_setting_changed` on apply and on outcome; credentials redacted in before and after.
  9. **Agents.** No tool, plan or scope; the plan store refuses guarded paths; the spec/13 §3 list gains the routes and the new problem codes.
  10. **Recovery** (ADR-957): `telltale guarded list` and `clear` (offline, audited with `cli:<os user>`), `TELLTALE_IGNORE_GUARDED=1` with the health reason `guarded_ignored`, the local suppression of a cleared entry until the primary's copy changes (and the removal request on reconnect), and `docs/recovery.md` (the symptom and fix table).
  11. **UI.** `GuardedChange.svelte` (preview, red "weakens security" mark, the phrase, the sign-in prompt, the countdown from `deadlineSecs`, Confirm and Cancel, the confirm step opened from the URL fragment on the new address, "pending restart" with the restart steps, the rollback message and reason, and the "Restart now" flow of step 12) and a `guarded.svelte.ts` store; "set by file" locks the control. Help topics `guarded-settings` and `guarded-recovery`. No setting is wired to it until T27.14; the Playwright tests here use a test-only registry row (an `immediate` and a `window` one) compiled only under the cargo feature `e2e` (never behind an environment variable alone; the e2e server is built with it, the release binary is not). The exit hook of step 12 is gated the same way: it exists only under `e2e`, so no environment variable in a release build can turn a restart into a no-op.
  12. **Restart now** (ADR-956 §4a; the `restart-now` `action` row). `supervisor` is computed once at start from the declaration only: `TELLTALE_SUPERVISOR` (`systemd`, `docker`, `kubernetes`, `none`), set by the shipped unit, Compose bundles and chart; nothing is detected from `INVOCATION_ID`, `JOURNAL_STREAM` or Kubernetes variables (inherited by terminals and CI runners). `GET /api/v1/guarded` lists it per node. Preview and apply as step 4 with its own phrase `<node-id> restart`, a sign-in within 10 minutes, an interactive admin session only (agents, MCP and tokens refused), the guarded rate limit; 409 `no_supervisor` with no supervisor and 409 `guarded_change_pending` while a window change is pending; acceptance takes the per-node guarded-operation lock atomically with the pending check and the audit write (every guarded route answers 409 `restarting` while it is held; a restart is refused while a window change, `cluster-join` or other guarded operation holds it); the audit row `settings.guarded_restart` is written before the exit (no audit, no restart), then a drain (5-second cap) and exit code 75; the alert outcome `restarting`. Cluster: node-local, forwarded only by the primary with the signed, single-use assertion naming the action (a nonce bound to the `previewId` and node; the target keeps used nonces; a replica's is refused), warnings for a primary or a site's only healthy resolver, no "restart all". UI: a "Restart now" button on every "pending restart" item only when the node reports a supervisor, a confirm dialog with the phrase and the note that the node cannot check that the supervisor restarts it, then "restarting", polling the health endpoint, and the outcome (`applied` or `rolled back` with the reason) or the recovery link after the timeout. The shipped systemd unit, Compose bundles and Helm chart set `TELLTALE_SUPERVISOR` and a restart policy; `docs/recovery.md` and the help topic `guarded-recovery` cover a node that does not return.
  - *AC:* (1) `api_902_registry_covers_every_guarded_path` and `api_902_weakens_table` (every case of ADR-914); (2) `api_902_guarded_change_needs_admin_and_refuses_agents` and `api_902_tokens_refused_for_guarded_changes`, table-driven over the registry for agent, other token, viewer and operator; - [ ] **T27.14 Listeners, API address and TLS, trusted proxies.** *(API-902, CLU-006, CLU-004, DNS-001; ADR-914, ADR-956)* After T27.13. The four `window` settings on Settings → System, per node, each a registry row.
  1. **Listeners.** A form for `[[listen]]` (address, port, protocol, TLS). `Listeners` gains stage, commit and abort: the new sockets are bound next to the old ones for the whole window, the old ones close only on commit, a failed bind changes nothing and reports the address and the error. The confirmation proof (`new_socket`) is that the node's own probe of the new listener answers (an encrypted listener is probed over its protocol).
  2. **API address and TLS.** Forms for `api.listen` and `api.tls`. The API server gains the same stage, commit and abort for its sockets; the proof is that the confirming request arrived on the new socket (`new_socket`), or, for a TLS change on the same address and port, on the temporary parallel listener the preview names, with the new certificate (`new_cert`).
  3. **Trusted proxies.** A form for `api.trusted_proxies`; the proof (`proxy_list`) is that the confirming request passes under the new list, and the confirm step shows the client address the server now sees; until confirmed the new list is used by the confirm route only.
  4. **Checks.** 409 `would_disconnect_you` for a change that leaves no DNS or no API listener once confirmed, or that removes the API listener the apply request came in on; the preview warns when the node runs in Docker or Kubernetes (published ports and Service ports are not changed) and when the new address is not on a local interface. Weakening: TLS removed or downgraded, `api.listen` moved from loopback-only to a wider address, a proxy added.
  5. **UI.** Forms on `GuardedChange` with the address shown as a link to the new origin; help topics `listeners`, `api-address`, `trusted-proxies`.
  - *AC:* (1) `api_902_listener_change_binds_before_release` and `api_902_old_listener_kept_until_confirmed`; (2) `api_902_bad_listener_rolls_back_and_dns_keeps_answering` (a UDP query loop across the whole change); (3) `api_902_api_listen_change_confirmed_from_new_address` and `api_902_api_tls_change_confirmed_with_new_certificate`; (4) `api_902_would_disconnect_refused` (no DNS listener, no API listener, the request's own listener); (5) `api_902_trusted_proxy_add_is_weakening` and `api_902_trusted_proxy_confirm_shows_seen_address`; (6) `api_902_listener_change_on_another_node_confirmed_at_that_node` (two-node test); (7) Playwright `api_902 move the api to a new port and confirm` (the e2e server on two ports), `api_902 listener change rolls back without confirmation`, `api_902 trusted proxy add asks for sign-in`; (8) help topics `listeners`, `api-address`, `trusted-proxies`; `make bench-smoke` (listener code is `telltale-net`), and the result in the Done note.

  1. **Key status card.** Source (environment, `secrets_key_file` or `secrets.key`), `key_id`, previous key, rows under it, locked, ephemeral; an environment key shows "set by environment" and has no rotate control. Read-only for operators.
  2. **Key rotation** (`secrets-key`, `staged`). The admin enters the path of a new key file already on the node (the steps and the offline `telltale secrets rotate-key` are shown). The preview checks that it is a regular file (not a symlink) of the right mode and owner, holds a valid key and is not a path the T23.2 rules deny, without returning content; apply stages `secrets_previous_key_file` = the current key's path and `secrets_key_file` = the new path for the next start, where re-encryption runs (`ops_014_previous_key_reencrypts_at_start`, crash-safe); a start that cannot open the store, or opens it locked, falls back by probation (T27.13 step 6; once any row is re-encrypted the fallback keeps both key paths). When no rows remain under the previous key, the UI offers to clear it (a second staged change).
  3. **`secret_file_dirs`** (`immediate`). A widening is weakening; a narrowing lists every `*_file` reference it would refuse.
  4. **`allow_secrets_over_http`, `allow_insecure_basic`** (`immediate`, weakening when turned on).
  5. No route takes key material in any body or query.

  1. **Make this node a primary** (`cluster-init`): name, advertise URLs, site, authority; the preview says the node generates and keeps the cluster CA and whether it needs a restart (staged if so).
  2. **Join a cluster** (`cluster-join`): `cluster.join_url` and the bootstrap secret, which is entered write-only over HTTPS or loopback, used for this join, and never stored, logged, audited or put in a preview. The preview says the node's configuration will be replaced by the primary's. Transactional: a failed join leaves the node as it was.
  3. **Leave the cluster** (`cluster-leave`): the preview lists what the node keeps and drops, and warns when it is the primary.
  4. **`[cluster.git]`** (`cluster-git`): repository, branch, path, authority; credentials stay T23.12's `SecretField`; the preview says when authority moves to Git. The one cluster-wide setting (ADR-956 §5).

  1. **`node.id`** (`staged`), "pending restart" until the next start; refused while the node is a cluster member; a value the file sets shows "set by file".

A form with the new path, the optional **Copy state** checkbox (off by default) and the preview: the checks (absolute, not a symlink, not a T23.2-denied path, not nested with the current directory, writable; re-run at apply and at start), the persistence check (a target on the container's writable layer or an `emptyDir` is a warning; `TELLTALE_DATA_EPHEMERAL` is a refusal, 409 `data_dir_not_persistent`), what the new directory already holds, and the plain statement of what does not carry over, generated from the single const `DATA_DIR_FILES` (the same list feeds the preview, the help topic and `docs/recovery.md`: `state.db`, `secrets.key`, `rollups.db`, `maintenance.json`, `cluster/` with `applied.json`, `watchdog/restarts`, `runs.json`, `anomaly.json`, `devices.json`, `annotations.jsonl`, `sinks/*.spill`, `snapshots/`, `logs/`, `setup-token`), with the commands to copy by hand and the advice to make the move permanent in the file. With Copy state on, the copy is made at the start that applies the pointer, before any state opens (so it holds every write up to the restart), and copies only `state.db` and the default `secrets.key` (0600, `O_EXCL`), refuses a non-empty target (409 `target_not_empty`), never copies `cluster/`, removes partial files and drops the pointer on failure, and records file names only. When the new directory will have no admin accounts (nothing copied, no `state.db`, no `TELLTALE_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_*` admin; with the variables set the preview names the admin to be created and it is not weakening) the form needs the second acknowledgement (409 `acknowledgement_required`) and counts as weakening (sign-in within 10 minutes). Refused outright while clustered (409 `clustered_node`; leave, move, re-join is the path), while another staged or window change is pending (409 `guarded_change_pending`) and while `secrets_rotation_incomplete` holds (409 `rotation_incomplete`). The pointer is local, never replicated, written to and read only from the file-named directory's `state.db`, followed one hop; the node locks both directories; once active the target must exist and hold the recorded state id, otherwise the node starts in the file-named directory with health reason `data_dir_missing` and never creates the target after the first start; probation needs at least one admin account when the preview promised one. The control shows "pending restart" and the Restart now button (T27.13 step 12). A value the file sets shows "set by file".
  2. **Watchdog** (`immediate`): turning it off is weakening.
  3. **File paths** (`immediate`): `trust_anchors_file`, `signatures_file`, `hosts_files` and `[[zone]] file`, confined by T23.2's path rules (the denied prefixes, the key files, every credential file the node uses, `secret_file_dirs`). The preview parses the file and gives counts and line numbers, never content; a path that fails to load keeps the previous configuration (CLU-004).
  4. `config_version` is shown read-only, the one setting without a control (ADR-914).

- [ ] **T27.19 Per-node guarded assertion keys.** *(API-902, CLU-001; ADR-977)* **Owner decision 2026-11-18.** Close ADR-977 point 5's residual: each node registers its own assertion key at join (stored with the member record, rotated on rejoin), the primary signs guarded assertions with it, and a target accepts an assertion only under the key registered for the primary's node ID. A replica that holds `ca.key` can then no longer forge one by minting a certificate. Write the ADR first (key type, registration and rotation, what happens to pending changes on failover).
  - *AC:* a test where a CA-holding replica mints the primary's certificate and sends an assertion that is refused; failover hands the role over without stranding a pending change; `deploy/cluster3/e2e.sh` passes; no UI beyond the Cluster page showing each member's key fingerprint (help topic, Playwright).
- [ ] **T27.18 Closing.** *(API-901, DOC-006)* A check that walks the configuration schema, the OpenAPI document and the MCP catalog and fails on any field, route or tool that has neither a UI control (by its help topic or a `data-setting` mark) nor a listed "No UI" reason; `docs/running.md` (which tier each setting is in), `site/guide-settings.html`; the README fork block.
  - *AC:* (1) `api_901_every_setting_has_a_control` passes in CI; (2) the bundle under 400 KiB; (3) every M27 box ticked, with *Open* items listed.

## M26 — Live network map (owner request 2026-11-16) [fork #14]

An animated, live map of the network and of where its lookups go, coloured by health: green healthy, yellow warnings, red errors. It has two views: the **network map** (gateway → networks/VLANs → devices, the TelltaleDNS nodes and their upstreams, with query flows animated along the links) and the **world view** (arcs to where answers point, using an optional IP-to-country database downloaded at runtime, never in the image). Flows are DNS lookups, not packets. It is built only on existing streams and counters, so nothing is added to the query path. Every control is in the UI. Topology gets richer once M25's SNMP/LLDP data exists, but the first version runs on M22's inventory.

- [x] **T26.1 Design ADR.** *(OBS-901, OBS-902)* No UI: a design record. A Proposed ADR: the data sources (query tail, time buckets, upstream and cluster health, inventory), server-side sampling with a cap per second, the health classification (green/yellow/red from the SLO and health settings), the map API (snapshot + stream) and MCP read tool, the GeoIP source, licence, download, verification and updates, the rendering approach (SVG/Canvas, no heavy library, within the 400 KiB UI budget), and cluster behaviour. *Done 2026-11-17: ADR-958 (the map: data model, the sampler on the aggregator thread, classification and `[map]` settings, API, cluster, privacy, SVG rendering, animation and accessibility, test plan) and ADR-959 (GeoIP: DB-IP Country Lite, CC BY 4.0, admin opt-in download, verification, on-disk lookup), both Proposed. Found: `QueryEvent` has no answer address, so T26.2 step 1 adds one behind a benchmark gate, with a fallback to upstream markers. T26.2 to T26.6 below are refined into steps.*
- [ ] **T26.2 Map data API and health classification.** *(OBS-901, AGT-001..005, CLU-002; ADR-958)* Snapshot and stream routes, OpenAPI, an MCP `network_health` read tool, the federated cluster read. UI: the map pages (T26.3, T26.5); T26.2 isn't ticked before their Playwright tests pass against it.
  - Steps: (1) the answer address: `answer: Option<Addr>` copied from the cache entry into `QueryEvent` (computed at insert, never parsed on a hit); kept only if the event fits its slot and `bench-smoke` is within noise, else recorded as Open and `/map/world` returns `address_not_recorded`; (2) `[map]` in the schema (defaults and ranges of ADR-958 §4), the one-of kind `map-settings/default` (GET/PUT/DELETE, scope `config:write:map`, MCP `plan_set_map`, audit, GitOps refusal), config schema regenerated; (3) the sampler subscriber on the post-privacy stage beside the analytics (not the tail): counter-based 1-in-N with weights, counted drops folded into N, 12 x 5 s window, 1,024 keys per slot and a 1 MiB cap, the busiest-edge sample ring; (4) the pure classification function (`min_sample` on unweighted events; the gateway, network and answer nodes included); (5) the snapshot builder (networks from `/api/v1/networks`, names from identities, privacy collapse, caps and `truncated`); (6) `GET /api/v1/map` with the server filters, `GET /api/v1/map/stream` (shared ticker, 8 streams, stops at zero), `GET /api/v1/map/world`; (7) the federated gather and merge through `blocking()` (ADR-946), `partial` and `missingNodes`; (8) MCP `network_health`; (9) OpenAPI and MCP snapshots regenerated.

## M25 — Network inventory: SNMP, gateway reads and imported scans (owner request 2026-11-16) [fork #15]

This milestone gathers what the network already knows, and what the owner's own scans found. It follows on from M22 ("Network inventory and home integrations"):
- a read-only SNMP poller of listed gateways and switches, plus identity reads of hosts that routers or L2 tables already see;
- more read-only UniFi and Home Assistant reads;
- uploads of the owner's nmap or CSV results;
- one inventory keyed by MAC across every source.

TelltaleDNS itself never probes (ADR-907): no sweeps, no ICMP, TCP, ARP or mDNS solicitation, no scheduled scans. Everything is off by default and runs on one designated node. SNMP runs in a separate worker process, and import parsing in another, so neither can take DNS down (CLU-004). The inventory never feeds policy or DNS names (ADR-910).

Each task:
- ships its own UI controls, help topics and Playwright tests (the standing rule at the top of this file); T25.14 is the walk-through and help review;
- updates `docs/` and `site/guide-devices.html` for whatever it makes visible (DOC-006);
- regenerates OpenAPI, `ui/src/api-types.ts` and `docs/config-schema.json` when its schema changes;
- records its image delta with `deploy/image/size.py --oci` on amd64, arm64 and arm/v7.

The deltas are summed per architecture against a milestone stop line of 0.08 MiB. T25.0 records the measured per-architecture image size from the image job, so the headroom after M23 is a measured number, not an estimate.

Order:
1. T25.0 first.
2. T25.1, T25.2 and T25.3 can start at once.
3. `SecretValue` and `Credential::resolve` land in T23.3, so T25.4 needs T23.1–T23.3, or defines `SecretValue` itself.
4. `*_ref` and the UI fields need T23.3–T23.6. Replicated refs need T23.5.

  - `spec/12`: a note on the `[discovery]` designated node.
  - `spec/13`: the §3.1/§3.2 rows and the new scopes.

  - *Review fixes (fix: review fixes for m25-b, 2026-11-17):* the worker writes `oom_score_adj` before it becomes not dumpable, so a non-root worker starts (`dsc_002_worker_starts_as_non_root`; the test runs as root and skips otherwise), which the systemd unit, the image and the chart need. `telltale_net::worker::harden` now also clears the ambient, permitted, effective and inheritable capabilities, sets `no_new_privs`, and installs a seccomp filter (x86_64 and aarch64) that refuses `bind`, `listen`, `ptrace` and `process_vm_readv`/`writev` with EPERM, so a worker can't share the DNS port or read the parent (ADR-908; `dsc_002_worker_privileges_are_dropped`). The first-write gate uses `DISCOVERY_SINCE` = 0.8.0, and this node's own `dev` build counts as the crate's version (`dsc_001_current_build_passes_the_gate`, through the real route; ADR-928). A worker's stderr is read through a 400-byte cap with control characters escaped (`dsc_002_stderr_line_is_bounded`, `dsc_002_stderr_is_escaped`). The supervisor refuses a worker whose reported limits differ from the request (`dsc_002_limits_must_match`, `dsc_002_worker_reporting_other_limits_is_refused`). The async frame reader moved into `frames.rs`, and the `worker_frames` fuzz target runs it beside the sync reader and requires them to agree (`dsc_002_async_reader_agrees_with_sync`; ADR-929). Reconciles apply in reload order (`dsc_001_reconciles_apply_in_order`), and the start-up audit reads the files' own copy (ADR-930). A job's timeout is per op (`supervisor::timeout_for`: a ping 10 s, other ops 30 s). The worker calls `background_thread()` itself. `#![allow(dead_code)]` is gone: the readers waiting for T25.11 and T25.12 are allowed per module, with the task named. The admin write's audit record is checked (`dsc_001_write_is_admin_only_and_audited`). The help links point at the real anchor (`network-inventory-devices-that-never-ask-dns`). The review's item 16 is unchanged: see the Open items below. Verified (2026-11-17): `cargo test` passes in full for `telltale-net` and for `telltale` (573 tests, including `dsc_002_worker_starts_as_non_root`, which ran as root); clippy with `-D warnings` and rustfmt are clean for both crates; `cargo check` of the fuzz crate passes; `npm run check`, `check:help` and `build` pass, and the full `1-ui.spec.ts` passes (139 of 139, `CI=1`); `spec/trace.py --check` and `site/build.py --check` pass. No dependency was added to a shipped crate (the image delta is unchanged; `libc` was already a dependency of `telltale-net`, and `tokio` is added only to the fuzz workspace). `make bench-smoke` does not pass its loss gate on this host: cache-hot 33.9% loss and miss-heavy 85.9% against 1%. T25.3 recorded the same gate failing before these changes (38.7% and 68.6%), and the bench itself warns that a UDP `bind()` costs about 10 ms on this host. This change does not touch the DNS listeners, handlers or cache, so the loss is the host's; the bench is Open until it runs on a host where the gate can pass.
  - *Review round 2 (fix: review fixes for m25-b, 2026-11-17; review of 276315b):* N1, the worker killed under the shipped unit: `systemd-analyze syscall-filter @privileged` (systemd 255, in the wrendns-dev container) lists `capset`, and the unit's `~@privileged` kills a call to it with SIGSYS. Each worker spawn now clears the child's ambient set before exec (`telltale_net::worker::prepare_spawn`), and `drop_worker_capabilities` calls `capset` only when a permitted or effective capability is held, so a worker under the unit never calls it (`dsc_002_spawn_clears_ambient_capabilities`, `dsc_002_worker_without_capabilities_never_calls_capset`, which also checks the filter kills a `capset` call). N2, the seccomp filter: it refuses every x32 number (`nr | 0x40000000`) and `io_uring_setup`, `io_uring_enter` and `io_uring_register`, each with EPERM (`dsc_002_worker_refuses_x32_and_io_uring`); and the parent becomes not dumpable while it spawns workers, so a worker can't open `/proc/<parent>/mem` (`dsc_002_worker_cannot_read_its_parent`; the dumpable control runs only under Yama scope 0, which this container doesn't run). ADR-934. N3, armv7: the filter covers 32-bit ARM (`AUDIT_ARCH_ARM`, the EABI numbers from `libc`), so the worker runs on the shipped `linux/arm/v7` image; the armv7 build is type-checked and clippy-clean for `armv7-unknown-linux-gnueabihf` (ADR-933). A `telltale-net` test helper that did not compile on ARM is fixed. The fmt diff at `inventory.rs:324` is fixed (N4). The worker's documentation in `docs/running.md` no longer claims what the filter did not yet refuse.
  - *Review round 3 (fix: review fixes for m25-b, 2026-11-17; review of 845f114):* N6 (MEDIUM): a non-dumpable parent broke the host write-rate metric on every non-root deployment, because `/proc/self/io` is root-owned 0400 once the process is not dumpable (the SD-card wear signal on a Pi). `prepare_spawn` now opens that file first, and `telltale_net::self_io_text` reads the descriptor with `pread` (`dsc_002_write_rate_survives_a_worker_spawn`, run as uid 65534; with the old fresh open the test fails). The other `/proc/self` readers were checked as uid 65534 after the change: `maps`, `smaps`, `exe`, `status`, `limits`, `fd`, `task` and the owner of `/proc/self` (the secrets key check) stay readable by the process itself. The trade-off is in ADR-934 and `docs/running.md`. N7 (LOW): `dsc_002_worker_cannot_read_its_parent` makes the shield's middle process dumpable first and checks that `prepare_spawn` clears the flag, which holds under any Yama scope; the comment is corrected. N8 (LOW): `X32_SYSCALL_BIT` is gated to the architectures that use it.

That is more than a small change here, so the seam stays Open until T25.6.

  - *Done in part (fix: review fixes for m25-snmp, 2026-11-17):* authNoPriv and authPriv over the connected agent. Engine discovery accepts only a noAuthNoPriv Report whose varbind is `usmStatsUnknownEngineIDs`, and a failed exchange drops the cached engine. The time window follows RFC 3414 §3.2.7b (higher boots or a later time resyncs; lower boots, or equal boots more than 150 s behind, is stale; boots at 2^31-1 is stale). An authenticated notInTimeWindow Report takes the agent's clock and the request is sent once more, at most once per agent per minute. The reply must match the request's security level, engine ID and user. Ku and the privacy key are zeroized; the MAC and the community are compared in constant time. Tests: `dsc_003_rfc3414_a3_key_vectors`, `dsc_003_rfc7860_vectors`, `dsc_003_rfc3826_aes_cfb_vectors` (NIST SP 800-38A F.3.13, which RFC 3826 uses), `dsc_003_refused_protocols_explained`, `dsc_003_spoofed_report_resync_limited`, `dsc_003_usm_encode_offset_round_trips`, `dsc_003_usm_privacy_round_trip`, `dsc_003_time_window_follows_rfc3414`, and the loopback v3 tests (`dsc_003_v3_*`, `dsc_003_authenticated_time_report_resends_once`). The fuzz target `snmp_usm` is written and type-checks. *Round 2 fixes:* an authPriv request accepts the agent's time-window `Report` at authNoPriv, with request ID 0 or the request's own, when its MAC verifies (`dsc_003_authenticated_time_report_resends_once_authpriv`; ADR-931 (Accepted 2026-11-17). The authNoPriv case is `dsc_003_authenticated_time_report_resends_once`. The loopback fake now sends its time Report at authNoPriv, as an agent does. The resync rule is ADR-931 (Accepted 2026-11-17), and the ADR-908 text points to it.
  - *Done (T25.5, track snmp-v3, 2026-11-18; ADR-980 for the HMAC key note; shipped crate unchanged in the first commit, fixed in d453399 and in the review round 2 commit):*
    - Interop with net-snmp at authNoPriv and authPriv for SHA and SHA-256, run: `crates/telltale-snmp/tests/v3-e2e.sh` starts its own throwaway `snmpd` container on its own Docker bridge (`tt-snmpv3-e2e-net`, 172.30.247.0/24, no host networking, nothing published, not `tt-snmp-net`), generates four USM users (SHA and SHA-256, each authNoPriv and authPriv with AES-128) and their passwords at run time into a private work directory (never printed, never committed, removed on exit), and tears the container, network and files down with a `trap` on exit, error or interrupt (checked: nothing is left after a run). It runs in CI (`snmp` job) with `cargo` on PATH, or from a dev container with `TT_V3_DEV=<container>`. Result: 7 of 7 interop runs pass (`dsc_003_net_snmp_auth_no_priv` for SHA and SHA-256, `dsc_003_net_snmp_auth_priv` for SHA and SHA-256, plus the new `dsc_003_net_snmp_v3_walk` (a bounded authPriv walk, SHA-256), `dsc_003_net_snmp_wrong_password_fails` and `dsc_003_net_snmp_unknown_user_fails`). The script refuses any address outside its own range; no SNMP is sent to 192.168.0.0/16 or any real device.
    - The fuzz target `snmp_usm` cannot run here (libFuzzer needs a nightly toolchain, and cargo-fuzz is not installed); its cases are in `tests/usm_hostile.rs` (5 tests under `cargo test`): every truncation and a trailing byte refused, every single-byte change to a valid authenticated message (SHA-1/96, SHA-256, SHA-512; with and without AES) never verifies, 20,000 seeded random mutations and noise never verify and never panic, deep nesting and over-long lengths are refused, and Debug output of the user and the round keys holds no password or Ku (`dsc_003_usm_debug_output_holds_no_secret`).
    - Tests: `telltale-snmp` 33 unit + 11 BER hostile + 5 USM hostile + 10 loopback + 1 read-only pass (6 interop ignored without an agent, run by the script). clippy `-D warnings` and fmt clean. No new dependency: `cargo tree -d -p telltale-snmp` prints nothing; `cargo deny` is not installed in the dev container, and the dependency graph is unchanged since the last clean run. Image delta on all three architectures: 0 bytes (tests, a script and a CI step only).

The CI `test` matrix runs the workspace on the hosted arm64 runner, and the `snmp` job now also runs the vectors on the software backend and on linux/arm/v7 under QEMU (`docker/setup-qemu-action`, as `image.yml` does). The host here has no emulator (`exec format error`), so the QEMU step has run only in CI, not yet here.
  - *Review fixes (T25.5):* an authenticated notInTimeWindow Report resyncs only when it moves the clock forward (`dsc_003_authenticated_time_report_never_moves_the_clock_back`); the `snmp_usm` fuzz target derives its keys once; the script's range guard checks the address the tests get, the tests assert it too, and the network and container names carry the PID and the network is removed only when this run created it; the wrong-password and unknown-user tests assert a timeout.
  - *Review fixes round 3 (ADR-980, Proposed):* passwords under 8 octets are refused (`Refused::ShortPassword`); rediscovery cannot move an engine's clock behind its last authenticated value; SHA-256 and SHA-512 key-localisation vectors (computed with hashlib over the A.2 stream, not from net-snmp, which is not installed here); the CI arm/v7 line is one plain line; the script clears stale `.v3e2e.*` directories and drops its constant shell guard (the Rust assertion is the guard); the HMAC key copy in ring is accepted (ADR-980).   - *Review fixes round 4 (ADR-980 item 4):* an agent whose clock fell more than 150 s behind the client's estimate was locked out for good (every reply classified stale, rediscovery restored the same estimate). The engine now remembers the last authenticated boots and time; a later authenticated time resyncs even when it is behind the estimate (RFC 3414 §3.2.7b), an older one stays refused. Tests: `dsc_003_agent_clock_behind_the_estimate_recovers`, `dsc_003_agent_clock_never_goes_back_past_the_last_authenticated_value`, `dsc_003_slow_agent_clock_resyncs_but_never_below_the_latest_received`.
