- [ ] **T25.18 UniFi device and group sync.** [owner request 2026-11-18] *(DSC-016, DSC-017, API-016, AGT-001..005; ADR-998, ADR-999 (both Proposed), amends ADR-912's "reads only")* Depends on T25.10, the router integration (M22, M23) and **T24.30** (the preview's before-and-after effective policy uses its resolver; if T24.30 is not done when step 5 is built, the preview ships without the policy column and that is recorded Open here). **Design is this entry and the two ADRs; nothing is built until the owner accepts ADR-998 and ADR-999.** The sync keeps the membership of a TelltaleDNS group and one UniFi group object the same, in either direction chosen per link. It syncs **membership only**; categories and policy are not mapped (ADR-998 §11). The owner's premise that UniFi's content filtering is traffic-level, not DNS-level, is unconfirmed and public sources say the opposite (ADR-998) and suggest UniFi Content Filter on a synced group may redirect DNS to the gateway and so bypass TelltaleDNS for it; the product text claims neither stacking nor IP-level enforcement until the owner confirms (Q4).
  - **Decide the order of work.** Steps 1 and 2 are small and safe and come first; the read-only direction (steps 3 to 7) is useful without ADR-999; the write steps (8, 9) need both ADRs accepted.
  - **1. Capability check (no code on the DNS path, no request to the owner's gateway by anyone but the owner).** Find out which UniFi object holds device-group members and how to read and write it, from the gateway's own OpenAPI document (`/proxy/network/api-docs/integration.json`), which **the owner downloads** (it may be committed as a fixture only after it is checked for site IDs and any other identifier), or from the owner's browser's network log of creating a device group (**a HAR or network log carries the session cookie, `x-csrf-token` and real MACs: it stays outside the repository and is never committed or pushed; only a hand-written, redacted summary goes into `docs/unifi-sync-capabilities.md`**; a secret-scan or redaction check (grep for cookie, token, key and MAC patterns) runs on every file the step commits); record the path, the member form (MAC or client ID), whether offline clients are members, the write method, the CSRF need, the role a key or user needs, and whether UniFi's admin log shows the write. Output: a short `docs/unifi-sync-capabilities.md` and an update to ADR-998 §2 (the first backend kind), or a note that no API exists, which stops the task at this step and is reported to the owner. Decision Q1 covers how it is done.
  - **2. The pure merge.** `crates/telltale/src/unifi_sync/merge.rs` (no I/O): inputs B, U, T (sets of MACs with flags `private`, `syncable`), the mode, the guard limits; output a `Plan` of changes by side and device, the held and conflict items, and the reasons for each skip. Property tests: convergence, idempotence (applying the plan then merging again gives an empty plan), no removal without a baseline, no oscillation, a change on both sides in the same way is a no-op, and unsyncable devices are never touched.
  - **3. The read client.** `GroupBackend::list_objects` and `::members` for the first kind over the router's existing session or key (the inventory thread's shared session, ADR-912; the read credential only). Row and byte caps as T25.10 (4 MiB, 8,192 rows). Device strings are untrusted (DSC-015).
  - **4. Links and settings.** `[[group_link]]` as a shared managed kind `group_links` (`/api/v1/group-links[/{id}]`; create, change of mode and unlink are admin-only; an operator may pause) and `[unifi_sync]` (`enabled`, `writes_enabled`, `interval_secs`, `max_changes_per_round`, `mass_change_percent`, `baseline_max_age`, `stale_alert_rounds`) as an admin-only singleton `unifi-sync` (an operator or agent may only turn `enabled` off). Validation: the group and router exist, one link per group per router, one link per UniFi object, `kind` known. Regenerate OpenAPI, config schema, UI types and the MCP snapshot.
  - **5. The preview and the apply (read direction).** `POST /api/v1/group-links/{id}/preview` (read, off the query path, bounded; returns the plan with a `preview_id`, each device's before and after effective policy from the T24.30 resolver, the skipped and private and unmatched lists, the held items) and `POST /api/v1/group-links/{id}/apply` with the `preview_id` (409 `preview_stale` if either side changed). The TelltaleDNS-side changes are config writes through the normal path with `origin = sync:<id>`, one audit record per change, and created devices `[[client]] name, match = [mac], groups = [group]`.
  - **6. The sync thread, on the primary.** Its own thread and runtime like the inventory (ADR-912, ADR-930's gate: not an ephemeral pod, not a witness, only the primary), a timer at `interval_secs`, one round per link at a time, baselines in `state.db` (sent best-effort to peers, a missing one makes the link held and additive-only), stale and flapping states, `telltale_unifi_sync_*` metrics (rounds, changes by side and kind, held, conflicts, stale links, write failures), alerts `unifi_sync_stale` and `unifi_sync_flapping`.
  - **7. The conflict view.** `GET /api/v1/group-links/{id}/conflicts` and `POST .../resolve` (per row: take UniFi, take TelltaleDNS, keep both, skip), shaped like T31.3's conflict rows so M31 can absorb it.
  - **8. The write client (ADR-999; after the owner accepts it).** `unifi_write::member_op` as the only function that holds the write credential and builds a write; `write_api_key_ref` and `write_username`/`write_password_ref` on `[[router]]` through the M23 store (`SecretField`, `discovery`-style names `router.<name>.write_api_key`); audit before the request; one write a second, 50 changes a round; no redirects; the pinned TLS settings; verify by re-read before the baseline advances; the Check button (one GET of the target object with the write credential, the single stated exception, and the account's role where reported); the **verify re-read uses the read credential**; a session-only local user as the fallback if keys need a super admin (password through M23, CSRF handled); write credential replication limited to nodes that can become primary.
  - **9. Write modes.** `telltale_definitive` and `two_way` in the merge's apply, behind `writes_enabled` and the admin's applied preview; **entering a write mode, or `writes_enabled` off to on, holds every affected link until a fresh preview is applied; applied state lives in `state.db`, and links from file, Git or a restore start preview-only and held;** a synced add appends the group last in a client's `groups` and a removal removes only that entry; an agent-originated membership change on a write-mode linked group is refused with 403 `agent_not_allowed`; a parseable empty read when B was not is held as a preview; *Undo last round*; the flapping pause; the mass-change guard in both directions.
  - **10. API, MCP and agents.** Every operation in OpenAPI with dry run, `Idempotency-Key` and reason (AGT-001..006); MCP read tools `list_group_links`, `get_group_link_preview`, `get_unifi_sync_status` and plan tools `plan_link_group`, `plan_set_group_link_mode`, `plan_unlink_group`; no agent scope applies a preview or a plan that writes to UniFi (403 `agent_not_allowed`, as ADR-903 §5), and `apply_plan` refuses an `agent:*` change that alters the membership of a write-mode linked group; spec/13 rows; a plan response says `propagates_to_unifi` for a group in a write mode.
  - **11. UI (standing rule).**
    - Groups page, per group, a **Linked to UniFi** card: the link state chip (in sync, held, stale, flapping, broken, paused); **Link to UniFi…** (admin) opens a dialog: router, the UniFi objects read from the gateway (searchable, a same-name one only suggested), mode as three radio choices with one sentence each (UniFi is definitive, TelltaleDNS is definitive, both ways), and the switches *Create missing devices* and *Include private MACs*; **Preview** (a table by side and device with the badges *private MAC*, *no MAC, skipped*, *unmatched*, each device's change in effective policy, and a held banner with the reason), **Apply** (admin, only the previewed plan), Pause, **Unlink** (says it deletes nothing). A banner on a linked group's membership editor, for any role, says a change reaches UniFi in the write modes. The link dialog and the card show the warning **"UniFi Content Filter on this group may bypass TelltaleDNS"**; changing the mode to a write mode, or turning writes on, shows the link as *held* with the text that a new preview is needed.
    - Settings or the Network inventory page: the **UniFi sync** card (admin): the `enabled` and `writes_enabled` switches (the second disabled with the reason until a write credential is set), the intervals, the write credential through `SecretField` on the Routers page's UniFi entry, the Check button with its result.
    - A **Conflicts** tab on the link with the per-row choices.
    - Help topics `unifi-group-sync`, `unifi-write-access` (how to make the dedicated UniFi user, what it can do) and `unifi-sync-conflicts`; `check:help` passes.
    - Playwright `dsc_016 link, preview, apply (UniFi definitive)`, `dsc_016 first preview is additive only`, `dsc_016 held round needs the admin`, `dsc_016 conflicts resolved per row`, `dsc_017 write switch disabled without a credential`, `dsc_017 operator cannot link or switch writes`, `dsc_016 viewer sees the state only`, `dsc_017 undo last round`, `dsc_016 pause and unlink`, `dsc_016 operator can turn enabled off`, `dsc_017 check button result`, `dsc_016 banner on linked group membership editor`, `dsc_017 mode change to write needs new preview`, `dsc_016 content filter warning shown`, run against `deploy/routers-e2e.sh`'s fake or `page.route`.
  - **12. Docs and site.** `docs/unifi-sync.md` (what syncs and what does not, the private-MAC note, the first-sync walk-through, the security note on who can change a linked group, outages, the kill switches, how to remove it, and the warning that UniFi Content Filter on a synced group may bypass TelltaleDNS), `docs/security.md` (the first write to the gateway), the Routers page text, `docs/configuration.md`, `site/guide-devices.html`.
  - **13. Open from the start (recorded, not built).** Showing read-only which UniFi policies and content-filter profiles use a linked group; creating the UniFi group from a TelltaleDNS group; syncing a display name (the only place last-writer-wins would apply); other object kinds (`usergroup`, `firewallgroup`); writing any UniFi policy; whether UniFi's own admin log records the write.
  - **Test plan (fake only).** The fake UniFi in `routers_tests.rs` and the Python fake in `deploy/routers-e2e.sh` gain one stateful group object (list, members, add and remove, the CSRF and key checks, a read-only role answering 403 on writes, injectable 500, timeout and 429, a request log with counters, an unparseable and a truncated member list). A guard in the test support refuses a non-loopback base URL, and no test or CI step names the gateway (`192.168.1.1`), a Pi-hole, the VIP or Home Assistant.
  - *AC (read direction, needs only the owner's capability answer):*
    - `dsc_016_merge_converges_and_is_idempotent` (property test);
    - `dsc_016_no_removal_without_baseline`;

  - *Owner decisions the build needs (asked as one batch at the start of the task; recommendations in brackets):*
    - **Q1** How is the capability check (step 1) done: the owner downloads the gateway's OpenAPI JSON and, if needed, a browser network log of creating a device group and places them in the repo, or the owner allows one read-only session from wrendns-dev against the gateway? [the owner supplies the files; nothing else touches the gateway.]
    - **Q2** Accept ADR-998 (links, MAC identity, three-way merge, membership only)? [accept]
    - **Q3** Accept ADR-999 (the first write to the gateway, one operation, a separate credential)? [accept, with the read direction shipping first]
    - **Q4** Confirm the premise: is UniFi's content filter, on the owner's gateway, traffic-level or DNS-level? Public sources say DNS-level. It decides the product wording. **Also test: with UniFi Content Filter on for a device group, do that device's queries still arrive at TelltaleDNS (check the query log)? Likely they do not (DNS redirect).** [owner checks and tells us]
    - **Q5** Default for a new link: `unifi_definitive` and preview-only until applied, and `create_missing_devices` on, `include_private_macs` off? [yes]
    - **Q6** The write credential: a dedicated local UniFi user with the narrowest role that works (the build finds which), or the owner's existing key? [dedicated user]
    - **Q7** Mass-change guard: hold a round that removes more than 5 members or 20% of a link? [yes]
    - **Q8** Should an operator be allowed to change the membership of a linked group in a write mode (it reaches UniFi), or only admins? [operators may, with the banner and the audit; links and modes stay admin-only]
    - **Q9** Which UniFi site and which gateway: the first `[[router]]` of type `unifi`, one site per link? [yes]
    - **Q11** UniFi has no object-scoped role: the write credential can probably change the whole Network application, and a key may need a super admin (fallback: a session-only local user with no MFA). Does the owner accept that exposure for the write modes? [accept only with the read direction first; otherwise refuse ADR-999]
    - **Q10** Where does the task sit in the order: right after T25.10, before the rest of M25, or after T25.17? [after T25.10; T25.17 then closes after it]

## Worker fixes (track worker-rlimit)
- [x] **T-wl Sandboxed workers in the musl release build and the image.** *(API-007, DSC-002; ADR-988, ADR-989)* The Pi-hole import worker answered `"<path>: <path>"` in every musl build and the image had no `/tmp` for it. mimalloc's 1 GiB arena counted against `RLIMIT_DATA`; workers now start with `MIMALLOC_ARENA_RESERVE=0` (set by `prepare_spawn`), `harden` refuses a limit the process already exceeds and says why, and the import's scratch files live in `<data_dir>/import-tmp/<unique>` (0700, removed afterwards).
  - *Done (track worker-rlimit, 2026-11-18):* `telltale-net` `worker.rs` and `sys.rs` (`WORKER_ALLOCATOR_ENV`, the `harden` preflight, `vm_data_bytes`); `pihole_import.rs` (`Upload` scratch directory, `--scratch`), `pihole.rs` (`load_in`); tests `crates/telltale/tests/pihole_import_worker.rs` (3, spawn the built binary under the real limit), `dsc_002_prepare_spawn_sets_allocator_env`, `dsc_002_vm_data_is_readable`, `api_007_upload_scratch_*` (2), and `discovery_workers.rs` started through `prepare_spawn`; `deploy/worker-image-e2e.sh` and the CI job `worker-image` (musl image, read-only root, no `/tmp`: discovery handshake and a gravity.db and a Teleporter zip through the API). No setting, route or tool was added, so no UI control, help topic or Playwright test applies. Reproduced first with a `bench-fast` musl build (`docker buildx build --target bin`) and `strace`. Also found: the discovery worker's limit had bounded nothing (its allocations stayed inside the pre-mapped arena); it is now a real bound.
  - *Review fixes:* the temp-dir fallback no longer chmods or sweeps the system temp directory (ADR-989 amended), entry cap 256 MiB, ADR-988 reworded. Open: a release-build test that the discovery worker's limit aborts an allocation (the `alloc` fault op is debug-only by design, `dsc_002_release_has_no_fault_ops`); the 256 MiB archive cap means no archive can now provoke the abort, so the image check covers only the handshake and the refusal when the variable is missing.
  - *Open:* the CLI `telltale import pihole` of an archive still uses the system's temporary directory (it runs in an operator's shell); the arm64 and armv7 images were not run here (CI builds amd64; the same zigbuild path and allocator apply).

## Test fixes (fork #20)
- [x] **T-f20 `ups_005` parallel race test.** [fork #20] *(UPS-005; ADR-974)* `ups_005_parallel_races_and_first_answer_wins` no longer checks a 150 ms bound, which measured the UDP bind cost of wrendns-dev (about 10 ms each, queued across the process; `bench/README.md`) and not the race. It checks that the first good answer is the fast member's, that the group returns before the slow member's attempt cap (1 s), and that it hands the fast answer back within 150 ms of the fast member sending it (a gap with no bind in it). The slow member's own timeout is above that cap, so only a group that waits for it would take longer. `ups_006_single_member_waits_for_a_slow_answer` now bounds its budget check at 500 ms, below the 600 ms slow answer. The product is unchanged. A fan-out delay below 1 s is not separated from bind cost by a timing check here (ADR-974, open). *AC:* 20 consecutive parallel runs of `cargo test -p telltale-upstream --test upstream` pass in wrendns-dev (done: 20 of 20, 14 of 14 each, 67 to 106 s); after the review fixes, three further full runs of the binary pass 14 of 14 (66 to 69 s); clippy and fmt clean; a group that waits for every member fails at 1.00 s (checked by mutation). *Done 2026-11-17: `crates/telltale-upstream/tests/upstream.rs`. Open (ADR-974, Proposed): the per-query UDP bind in Docker Desktop, which caps cache misses near 100 a second there; removing it from the query path is the owner's decision and needs a benchmark on quiet hardware (T24.22).*

## M24 — Replacing Pi-hole (owner request 2026-11-16)

What a home running a Pi-hole pair behind a keepalived VIP needs to switch to TelltaleDNS and keep running it: Pi-hole's special names, "Update Gravity", the importer's gaps, and the failover pieces. The UniFi integration (M22) is the edge Pi-hole never had. Benchmarks from Docker Desktop don't count for the 5% rule (every UDP `bind()` there takes about 10 ms; `bench/README.md`), so each hot-path task reruns `make bench-smoke` on the homelab before release. Open items are also filed as GitHub issues on the fork (`nbacheller/telltaledns`), written "fork #N" here to tell them from upstream's issue numbers. Fork-wide items without a task: #10 (tests that fail on a CRLF checkout), #11 (Windows `nslookup` on port 1053), #12 (Home Assistant source untested against a real instance). Upstream issues to take when fixed, checked at each sync: upstream #7 (the process stops answering on every port while the service stays up) and upstream #43 (a replica attached to another replica never moves back to the primary).

Pi-hole's "disable forever" is the setting `maxPauseMinutes` (default 60, 1 to 1,440), a longer timer is cut to it, and a non-numeric `timer` (Home Assistant sends `true`) is "no timer". - [x] **T24.11 Shared missing-set race.** [fork #8] *(CLU-003, OBS-009)* `Federated::missing` is one shared mutex, so two `GET /lists` requests at the same time can overwrite each other's `missingNodes`. Give each request its own missing set (or compute it per call) so a replica's answer names only the nodes its own reads missed. Also: `GET /lists` on a replica makes two serial reads (`Read::Lists` then `Read::ListHits`), up to 2 s each, so one request can take about 4 s; run both in one round. No UI: an internal fix.
  - *Done 2026-11-17 (track missingrace):* a request's missing set is its own. `Backend::for_request` gives each request a handle with its own `missing` cell (`crate::request_backend` in `telltale-api`, called by every handler that returns `missingNodes`), so one request's missing nodes never show in another's answer (`Federated::for_request`). The federated gather is now a round that returns its missing set (`Federated::round`); nothing is written to a shared cell. `GET /lists` asks the primary's lists and every node's hits in one round (`Backend::lists_and_hits`, `ListsAndHits { lists, hits, missing }`), so it takes one deadline instead of two, and its handler no longer resets the set between two reads. API shapes are unchanged; no DNS hot-path change, no UI (an internal fix). Test: `clu_003_concurrent_requests_name_only_their_own_missing_nodes` (a replica of a silent primary: the first request names the primary, a concurrent second request on the same backend names nothing); it fails when per-request handles are disabled. Tests: `cargo test -p telltale` 592 passed, 1 ignored (the 5 integration binaries pass); `cargo test -p telltale-api` 165 passed. No ADR: the task text already names the per-request set. Nothing deferred.
- [x] **T24.12 Special names in the UI.** *(DNS-019, API-002, API-011, AGT-001..005; ADR-904, ADR-914)* A shared singleton managed kind `special` (`PUT/DELETE /api/v1/special-names/default`, files win under ADR-040, `config_entries` kind `special`, agent scope `config:write:special` implying operator, MCP `plan_set_special_names`, spec/13 catalog row), applied on reload like `ratelimit`. `ddr_name` is admin-only on the server (an operator's change is 403 naming the field; agents never). `Explanation` gains `special` (`private_relay`, `single_label`, `resolver_arpa`, `ddr`, `firefox_canary`, `private_ptr`, `chaos`, `localhost`). - [x] **T24.20 Windows nslookup on a non-53 port.** [fork #11] **Owner decision 2026-11-17: investigate.** Windows `nslookup -port=1053 <name> 127.0.0.1` gets no answer from the Docker Desktop bundle while UDP and TCP queries from Windows (Python) and from inside Docker are answered. Find out why with a packet capture (what nslookup sends first, e.g. a PTR for the server address or an ANY, and what comes back), and fix the product if our response is at fault; otherwise document the quirk with a working command.
  - *AC:* the cause is written down with the captured exchange; if it is ours, a test reproduces it and the fix passes it; the bundle docs' example command works as written.
  - *Done 2026-11-17: not ours. Cause (observed on Windows 11 build 10.0.26200; re-checked 2026-11-17 on that one build): `nslookup -port=1053` (command line), interactive `set port=1053` and `-vc` all fail against the bundle, so the client does not reach port 1053. The earlier capture, not repeated, showed every query going to port 53 of the server address. Captured with a logging relay on 127.0.0.1:53 forwarding to the bundle's 1053 (no packet reached a relay on the chosen port in any run). Exchange, decoded: (1) `0001 0100 0001 0000 0000 0000` + `1.0.0.127.in-addr.arpa` PTR, no OPT, from nslookup; the bundle answers `0001 8583 0001 0000 0001 0000` (NXDOMAIN, AA, SOA in authority); (2) `0002 0100` A `example.com.<search suffix>`, answered `0002 8180`, 2 A records; (3) `0003 0100` AAAA, answered `0003 8180`, 2 AAAA records. Responses are well formed and nslookup prints them, so no product change and no test; the same answers come back over TCP with 2-byte length prefixes. Docs: `deploy/docker-desktop/README.md` section 4 and `compose.yaml` now give `docker run --rm alpine nslookup -port=1053 <name> host.docker.internal`, verified to answer; Python works from Windows. ADR: none (documentation only). Re-verification (review fixes 2026-11-17): a Python UDP query to 127.0.0.1:1053 from Windows returns `flags 8180 answers 2` for `example.com`; `definitely-not-here-xyz.example` returns NXDOMAIN (no wildcard on the bundle); the README gives that Python command as the Windows-native check, and says `host.docker.internal` needs Docker Desktop. Open (not blocking): whether nslookup's search suffix produced the `example.com.<search suffix>` answer in the capture is not investigated; the `example.com` answer is public, so the bundle does not own it. Deferred: none.*

The bundle's `test-ha` profile is unchanged.

- [ ] **T24.22 Benchmarks on quiet hardware.** [fork #9] *(NFR-*)* **Owner decision 2026-11-17: this PC when idle.** Run `make bench` and the comparison suite on the development PC at a time with no agent waves or builds running; record the special names, upstream budget and device-name results against the spec budgets in `bench/` and on the issue.
  - *AC:* the numbers are recorded with the machine, commit and conditions; any budget missed becomes its own task.
- [x] **T24.23 OIDC without chrono.** [fork #17] *(API-004; ADR-034)* **Owner decision 2026-11-17: replace the OIDC library.** Move OIDC sign-in off `openidconnect`/`oauth2` (which pull the unmaintained `chrono`, RUSTSEC-2026-0543) to a library without it, or to a minimal authorization-code-with-PKCE flow on crates the tree already has (ID-token signature and claim checks, discovery, JWKS rotation, nonce and state), then drop `telltale-api`'s own `chrono` and the `deny.toml` ignore. Security-sensitive: a design ADR first, Opus review, and the existing OIDC e2e (`3-oidc`) must pass unchanged; record the image delta.
  - *AC:* `cargo deny check` passes with no RUSTSEC-2026-0543 ignore; every existing OIDC test and the `3-oidc` Playwright spec pass; new tests cover a bad signature, a wrong audience or issuer, an expired token, a nonce mismatch and a JWKS key rotation.
  - *Steps (ADR-951 (b-ring) and ADR-952, accepted 2026-11-17 as implemented, after the second review):*
    1. **Spike.** Done in the review. `jsonwebtoken` 9 was rejected (ADR-951 (c-9)): it is a frozen line, and it skips claims of the wrong JSON type. The openidconnect 4.1.0 source confirmed the client-authentication default (Basic with a secret, `client_id` in the body without one) and the zero `exp` leeway. The chosen option is (b-ring), an in-tree verifier on `ring`.
    2. **Owner accepts** ADR-951 and ADR-952. Done 2026-11-17: the owner chose "second review, then merge" (accept as implemented if a second independent security review found nothing HIGH or MEDIUM in the verifier); that review found no HIGH, and its two MEDIUMs (CI had not run the matrix; acceptance recorded by an agent) are resolved: the full matrix and cluster3 passed on 7dd39f7, and the orchestrator recorded the acceptance from the owner's answer.
    3. **Verifier.** Done: `crates/telltale-api/src/auth/oidc/jose.rs` (algorithms, JWK keys, header rules, signatures), `claims.rs` (claim checks) and `metadata.rs` (discovery and transport), with the flow in `oidc.rs`. The surface `routes.rs` and `mod.rs` call is unchanged. `identity`, `role_for`, `username_for` and the agent role cap are not touched.
    4. **Tests.** Done: 38 OIDC tests, `api_004_*` and `agt_008_*`, cover each rejection in ADR-951's negative list, the JWKS rotation, the refetch rate limit (per provider, collapsed when concurrent) and the failed refetch. The existing flow tests keep their bodies; `chrono` in `flow_tests.rs` is `std::time`.
    5. **Dependencies.** Done: `openidconnect`, `oauth2` and `chrono` removed from `crates/telltale-api/Cargo.toml` (`openidconnect` was `:26`; `chrono` was the dev-dependency `:47`; `oauth2` was never a direct dependency). `ring` 0.17.14 is a direct edge (already in the tree, so no new crate). The `Cargo.toml` profile entries for `openidconnect`, `oauth2`, `chrono`, `rsa` and `num-bigint-dig` are gone. Both `deny.toml` ignores are removed (ADR-952). `cargo deny check` passes. `Cargo.lock`: 458 to 389 packages.
    6. **Docs.** Done: `docs/running.md` (the 2048-bit RSA minimum, the curves, client authentication, HTTPS endpoints under an HTTPS issuer, key rotation). No new setting, so no UI control or help topic. `spec/02`, `site/data/architecture.json` and ADR-034 name `ring` or note the supersession.
    7. **Integration.** Done: `3-oidc` passes against the real Keycloak 26.4 and Authentik 2025.10.1 stack (`ui/tests/oidc/compose.yml`, `OIDC_E2E=keycloak,authentik`, `E2E_IDP_HOST=host.docker.internal`), run together with `1-ui.spec.ts`: 171 passed, the six `3-oidc` tests among them.
    8. **Review.** Second adversarial review 2026-11-17: no HIGH, 2 MEDIUM, 6 LOW. Fixed: LOW L1 (no `=` in a token part or JWK field), L2 (`key_ops` must allow `verify`), L3 (discovery reloads are serialized per provider and serve the cached copy for 60 s after a failed reload), L5 (the exact `sub` everywhere), L6 (a clock before 1970 reads every token as expired). L4 (fractional dates refused) is documented in `docs/running.md`, not changed: an interop limit with no security effect, and Keycloak and Authentik pass. M1: CI runs on `feat/**` only, so the same SHA is also pushed to `feat/oidc-without-chrono-ci` and merge waits for green. M2: ADR-951 and ADR-952 are set back to Proposed; the owner's own confirmation is needed before they are Accepted.
  - *Done 2026-11-17 (review fixes, commits a587d0c and the docs commit): the in-tree verifier on `ring` replaces `openidconnect`, `oauth2` and `chrono` (ADR-951 (b-ring)); `cargo deny check` passes with no RUSTSEC-2026-0543 or RUSTSEC-2023-0071 ignore (ADR-952). Image delta: `linux/amd64` 13.86 MiB compressed on the base commit `53c21ac` (the branch's merge parent, built from `git archive` with `Dockerfile`) and 13.53 MiB on the merged branch, so −0.33 MiB against the 25 MiB gate (`deploy/image/size.py --oci`). `3-oidc`: 6 of 6 pass, with `1-ui.spec.ts` (171 tests in that run). Tests: `cargo test -p telltale-api` all pass; `cargo clippy -D warnings` clean for `telltale-api` and `telltale`; `cargo fmt --check` clean; `spec/trace.py --check` and `site/build.py --check` pass. Deferred: none in the code. Open: the ADR-951/952 owner acceptance, and the Opus review (step 8).*
  - *Open:* refresh tokens and userinfo (not in this change); logout keeps using `end_session_endpoint` as today.

## Auth fixes from upstream issues (fork #63, #64)

- [x] **T24.24 Router names rank below CIDR.** [fork #6] *(API-010, FLT-006; ADR-973)* **Owner decision 2026-11-18.** Change `ClientTable::identify` so a router name (a `[[client]]` `hostname:` key or a group's `router_names`) is tried after the most specific CIDR and before `default`, as ADR-973's owner decision sets; quick rules by router name are unchanged. Update `docs/running.md` ("A router name can be claimed"), the `device-name` help topic and the precedence list wherever it is written (spec/04 or the clients docs).
  - *AC:* `flt_006_identification_chain` asserts the new order (a device in a CIDR group is identified by the CIDR even when a client has its router name; a device in no CIDR is identified by the name); the Playwright `api_010` test still passes; no change on a query that uses no router name (`make bench-smoke` if `telltale-policy` changes).
  - *Done 2026-11-18: `crates/telltale-policy/src/clients.rs` (name tried after the CIDR scan; skipped when a group network holds the device, ADR-976 Proposed), tests `flt_006_identification_chain` (extended) and `flt_006_group_network_beats_router_name`; `docs/running.md`, `device-name` help topic. Deferred: none.*
- [ ] **T24.25 Pre-bound UDP sockets for upstream queries.** [fork #20] *(UPS-005, NFR-*; ADR-974)* **Owner decision 2026-11-18: build behind a benchmark.** In Docker Desktop each upstream UDP query waits 10 to 20 ms for its socket bind (ADR-974), which caps cache misses near 100 a second. Build a bounded stock of pre-bound sockets, each used for one query and then closed, refilled off the query path, so the source port stays random per query (RFC 5452) and nothing on the cache-hit path changes. Write the ADR first (stock size, refill, what happens when it is empty: bind inline as today).
  - *AC:* measured on quiet hardware with T24.22: cache-miss throughput and p99 with and without the stock, on Linux and in Docker Desktop; merged only if it is better on both and no worse on the cache-hit path; RSS within budget; source ports pass a randomness check over 10,000 queries.
- [ ] **T24.30 A device's effective policy.** [fork #21, #23] *(FLT-006, API-010, AGT-001)* **Owner request 2026-11-18:** click a device and see every filter and policy that applies to it, and why. `GET /api/v1/clients/{id}/policy` (and an MCP read tool) resolves it off the DNS path, through the same code a query's Explain uses:
  - the group the device lands in, and the key that matched (client ID, MAC, workload, address, network, router name), with the precedence of ADR-973 and ADR-976;
  - the group's lists (and, once T24.29 lands, its categories and filter profile), blocked services, SafeSearch and YouTube level, and rewrites;
  - the schedules and whether each is in effect now;
  - the device-scoped and group-scoped rules, including quick rules and their expiry;
  - the upstream group the device's queries use;
  - in a cluster, the same answer from every node, read from the primary's configuration.

  UI: a **Policy** panel on the device's detail in Clients, with links to the group, list and rule each line comes from, a help topic and a Playwright test. The CMDB's CI page (T31.5) shows the same panel.
  - *AC:* a test where one device is matched by network and another by MAC shows the right group and key for each; a scheduled rule shows in effect and not in effect around its boundary; the panel matches what Explain says for a query from that device; nothing new on the query path.
- [x] **T3.5b TOTP pattern reaches the browser.** [fork #64] *(UI-AUTH, API-003, ADR-943)* The six-digit `pattern` on the enrolment and sign-in code fields is written as an expression, so `[0-9]{6}` isn't read as `[0-9]6`; `ui/scripts/check-quantifiers.mjs` guards it in `npm run check`. *AC:* (1) the DOM `pattern` is `[0-9]{6}` on both fields; (2) enrolment with a real RFC 6238 code turns two-factor on; (3) sign-in asks for a code and accepts the next one; (4) the guard fails on a single- or double-quoted quantifier, on one line or across lines. *Done 2026-11-17: `ui/src/pages/Settings.svelte`, `ui/src/pages/Login.svelte`, `ui/scripts/check-quantifiers.mjs` (whole-file, both quote styles); the e2e TOTP spec records the enrolled secret and code for the sign-in test (the review's two-line bug). Deferred: none.*
- [x] **T3.5c Break-glass defaults to loopback.** [fork #63] *(API-004, ADR-944)* `[auth.oidc] allowed_admin_networks` is optional: absent is loopback only, set is exactly the list. Forms, HTTP Basic and the MCP on-behalf-of path check the same networks; a proxied request from an untrusted peer is never break-glass; startup warns while the list is unset; the Users tab names the default and its help topic `break-glass`. *AC:* (1) an API test per path (form, Basic, MCP on-behalf-of) refuses a LAN address by default; (2) an explicit list, including `[]`, is never overwritten; (3) a loopback peer with forwarding headers from an untrusted peer is refused; (4) the Users note and help topic show in the UI (Playwright). *Done 2026-11-17: `schema.rs` (`break_glass_networks`), `auth/mod.rs`/`routes.rs` (`break_glass_proxied`), `crates/telltale/src/auth_setup.rs` (warning), `docs/running.md`, `spec/08`, `docs/help/topics.json` (`break-glass`), `Settings.svelte`. Recovery: `docker exec` or set the list and restart. Migration note in ADR-944 and `docs/running.md`. Deferred: none.*

## M23 — Secrets and integrations in the UI (owner request 2026-11-16) [fork #13]

Anything set up through a secret file can also be entered in the UI, and the files keep working and win. Values are write-only, encrypted per node and replicated to full members (ADR-903). Nothing changes on the query path, and no crate is added. Every task records its image delta (`deploy/image/size.py --oci`) against a milestone total of at most 0.10 MiB.

Order:
1. T23.1 ships first, with no behavior change.
2. T23.2 ships right away, with T23.1 (owner decision 2026-11-16).
3. T23.3–T23.5 come before any UI task.

- [x] **T23.1 Close the credential leaks in read surfaces.** *(API-014, OPS-011, AGT-010; ADR-903 §4)*
  - **Credential table.** `telltale-config::credentials::PATHS`, checked for completeness against `classify.rs`.
  - **Where it is applied:**
    - `config_entries`, again on a replica after decoding (`api_backend.rs:2873-2960`, `federated.rs:2439-2450`), with `ConfigEntry.redacted`;
    - write responses;
    - both audit sites (`config_api.rs:1902-1913`, `forward.rs:232-237`, `apply_ack`);
    - `keep_in_git`, forward logging, and a redacting `Debug` on `forward::Write`;
    - `config check --print` (`--show-secrets`);
    - `compare.rs`, and the MCP read tools and resources.
  - **Marker rule.** A `<redacted>` marker keeps the value only while the destination is unchanged, otherwise 422 `credential_reentry_required`. Checks never resolve markers.
  - **Idempotency.** The node key loader (`telltale::secrets::NodeKey`: env, `[node] secrets_key_file`, `secrets.key`, subkeys) lands here. The idempotency request becomes a keyed hash of the full body, and the response is stored redacted.
  - **Storage hygiene.** Migration 10 purges old idempotency rows. `secure_delete=ON` and WAL `TRUNCATE` checkpoints.
  - **Spawned processes.** `RESERVED` gains `SECRETS_KEY` and `SECRETS_PREVIOUS_KEY`. Plugin and self-update `Command`s `env_remove` the key, bootstrap-password and token variables.
  - **Git webhook.** A generic 401 with the detail logged, empty secrets refused, and the Gotify token percent-encoded.
  - **Docs.** The comment at `config_api.rs:656-657`, `docs/running.md`, `site/guide-monitoring.html`, and the release note on older audit entries.
  - *AC:*
    1. `api_014_config_entries_redacts_upstream_headers_and_args`: headers, args and URL passwords come back as `<redacted>` and are listed in `redacted`, on the primary and on a replica, including against an N-1 primary.
    2. `api_014_write_response_and_audit_redacted`: covers a write entered on a replica and both audit records.
    3. `api_014_idempotency_row_holds_no_credential`: replay still works, and two PUTs differing only in a header value are not treated as a replay.
    4. `api_014_keep_in_git_never_prints_values`.
    5. `api_014_redacted_marker_keeps_value`, `api_014_redacted_marker_without_value_422`, and `api_014_redacted_marker_with_new_url_422`, including an MCP `plan_update_upstreams` URL change.
    6. `api_014_config_check_print_redacts`.
    7. `api_014_credential_paths_cover_secret_class`.
    8. `api_014_planted_credential_never_returned`: walks every GET in the OpenAPI document (except `/backup` and the support package, for stated reasons), compare, every MCP read tool and resource, and the query tail, with a planted header, arg, URL password and `client_secret`.
    9. `ops_014_plugin_env_has_no_secrets_key`.
    10. `api_014_webhook_401_is_generic` and `api_014_empty_webhook_secret_refused`.
    11. `api_014_idempotency_purge_leaves_no_residue`: the file and WAL hold no planted value after migration 10.
    12. The OpenAPI document and `api-types.ts` are regenerated, clippy is clean, and the size delta is recorded.
  - *Done 2026-11-16:* `telltale_config::credentials` (`PATHS`, `NOT_CREDENTIALS` with reasons, `DESTINATION_FIELDS`, entry and whole-configuration redaction as JSON and typed, URL passwords, `restore_markers`); `crates/telltale/src/redact.rs` applies it to `config_entries` (again on a replica: `federated::entries_from_primary`), write answers (so the response, both audit records and the stored idempotency response), the `keepInGit` TOML (from redacted inputs, and re-redacted from an older primary), `forward::Write`'s `Debug` and forward logging, and the pin and version diffs; `ConfigEntry.redacted`; `config check --print` redacts (`--show-secrets`); the marker rule in `plan_override` (422 `credential_reentry_required`), and checks refuse markers; `telltale::secrets` (`NodeKey`, `KeySlot`: env, `[node] secrets_key_file`, `secrets.key` 0600 with `O_EXCL`, the previous key, subkeys); idempotency records keep a keyed BLAKE3 of the full request (`Backend::idempotency_request`; 503 `secrets_locked` without a key for a body with a credential); migration 10, `secure_delete=ON`, WAL `TRUNCATE` after the purge (`State::checkpoint`); `RESERVED` and `NEVER_INHERITED` (plugins' and self-update's `env_remove`); the webhook's generic 401 and empty-secret refusal; the Gotify token percent-encoded. Docs: `config_api.rs` comment, `docs/running.md` ("Credentials are never shown", plugins, the webhook), `docs/configuration.md`, `site/guide-monitoring.html`, `site/guide-settings.html`, and `deploy/release/notes/v0.8.0.md` (draft, with the older-audit-entries warning; `preflight.sh` refuses a draft). Tests: (1) `api_014_config_entries_redacts_upstream_headers_and_args` (primary, replica, an N−1 primary's unredacted body); (2) `api_014_write_response_and_audit_redacted` and `forward::api_014_forwarded_write_audit_redacted` (the primary's record of a forwarded write, and forward logging); (3) `api_014_idempotency_row_holds_no_credential`; (4) `api_014_keep_in_git_never_prints_values`, `api_014_keep_in_git_text_from_an_older_primary`; (5) `api_014_redacted_marker_keeps_value`, `api_014_redacted_marker_without_value_422` (with the check), `api_014_redacted_marker_with_new_url_422` (URL, TLS name, proxy, and MCP `plan_update_upstreams`); (6) `api_014_config_check_print_redacts`, `api_014_typed_and_json_redaction_agree`; (7) `api_014_credential_paths_cover_secret_class`; (8) `api_014_planted_credential_never_returned` (every GET of the OpenAPI document but `/backup` and the support package's map, every MCP read tool and resource, the query tail; checked to fail with the entries' redaction removed); (9) `ops_014_plugin_env_has_no_secrets_key`, `ops_014_self_update_commands_have_no_secrets_key`; (10) `api_014_webhook_401_is_generic`, `api_014_empty_webhook_secret_refused`; (11) `api_014_idempotency_purge_leaves_no_residue`; also `ops_014_node_key_sources_in_order`, `api_014_idempotency_digest_is_keyed`. OpenAPI, `api-types.ts` (it was also behind on `/networks` and `dnsName`), and `config-schema.json` regenerated; the MCP snapshot is unchanged; clippy and `svelte-check` clean. Implementation notes for the owner are in ADR-903. *Review fixes 2026-11-16* (ADR-903 notes 8–12): every `url`-class field is in `PATHS` (and the completeness test checks them), with `list` as an entry kind and `/checks/list` refusing markers, and the Cluster page's Git `repo`, `GET /lists`' `source` and error, and router errors redacted; a replica refuses to forward markers to a primary older than 0.8.0 (409 `upgrade_required`); plans show their body redacted and replay the agent's; pin and version diffs show a credential-only change as a `replace` with the marker; unparseable `keepInGit` text from an older primary is withheld; markers aren't credentials for `secrets_locked`; a failed key load is retried; `secrets.key` is written whole (temporary file, hard link, directory fsync); the Gotify token is scrubbed in its encoded form; plugins inherit no `TELLTALE_*` variable but their two. Tests added: `api_014_replica_write_through_an_older_primary` (a two-node cluster: the federated route, `forward::managed`, the entry node's audit record and stored idempotency response against a stub 0.7.4 primary answering unredacted, and the 409), `api_014_pin_diff_never_shows_a_credential` (the pin's diff, the 409 `cluster_pinned` body, and a version diff), a plan in `api_014_planted_credential_never_returned` (and a planted list URL), `api_014_plan_body_shown_redacted`, `api_014_url_passwords_in_every_url_field`, `api_014_markers_are_not_credentials`, `api_014_hidden_changes_found_by_pointer`, `api_014_git_repo_password_never_shown`, `api_014_marker_capable_versions`, `ops_014_key_file_made_whole_and_failures_retried`. *Open:* the image size delta (12): no image build was run here (only small code, no crate); measure it with `deploy/image/size.py --oci` on the build VM.

- [x] **T23.2 Who may point a credential somewhere.** *(API-014, CLU-010; ADR-903 §4)* This is a behavior change. The owner chose to ship it right away, with T23.1 (2026-11-16).
  - **Admin-only:** `*_file` and `*_ref` changes, destination changes on any entry holding a credential, and `exec://`/`unix://` on writes and checks.
  - **Paths:** canonicalized, the denied prefixes and key paths, `[node] secret_file_dirs` for `*_file`, and existing out-of-bounds API paths reported in `credential_issues`.
  - **Audit and alerting:** `credential.redirect` and the alert event.
  - **Forwarding.** Forwarded writes carry `role` and `agent` and are re-checked on the primary. Role-less credential-affecting forwards get 409 `upgrade_required`.
  - **The Alerts page** shows path fields read-only to operators.
  - **Docs:** `docs/running.md`, `site/guide-monitoring.html`, and T9.6's amended criterion.
  - *AC:*
    1. `api_014_file_reference_change_needs_admin`: an operator gets 403.
    2. `api_014_operator_cannot_redirect_credential`: an upstream with headers and an alert with `token_file` both give 403 on a URL change.
    3. `api_014_operator_cannot_create_exec_upstream`: covers PUT, `checks/upstream` and a switch to `exec://`.
    4. `api_014_file_path_confined`: covers `/proc/self/environ`, a symlink into `data_dir`, the key file paths, and a path outside `secret_file_dirs`.
    5. `api_014_forwarded_write_rechecked_on_primary`, including a role-less (N-1) forward refused.
    6. `api_014_admin_redirect_audited_and_alerted`.
    7. The Playwright Alerts page shows operator read-only fields.
    8. The size delta is recorded.
  - *Done 2026-11-16:* `telltale_config::credentials` gains `API_PATHS` (the paths an API entry sets, and which hold a credential), `holds_credential`, `destination_change`, `reference_changes` and `program_change`; `crates/telltale/src/credential_rules.rs` holds the rules (`check_write` from `plan_override`, `check_forward_servers`, `check_draft_paths`) and the path rules (`PathRules`: symlinks followed, dangling ones too; `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, the data directory, the key, previous-key, bootstrap and TLS key files; `[node] secret_file_dirs`, default `/run/secrets`, `/var/run/secrets`, `/etc/telltale/secrets`); `ManagedWrite` and the forwarded `Write` carry `role` and `agent` (absent from an N−1 node: 409 `upgrade_required` for a credential-affecting change); `managed::merge` points a refused stored path at `/proc/telltale-refused/<field>` (not read) and `ConfigEntry.credentialIssues` lists it; `POST /checks/upstream` is admin-only for `exec://`/`unix://` or a `tls_client_key` draft, and confines its paths; the `credential.redirect` audit record, the dry run's warning, and the one-off alert condition `credential_redirected`; the new problem code `path_not_allowed`; `ConfigEditor` fields with `admin`, and the Alerts page (files read-only to operators; the address and CA once the destination holds a credential; `credential_redirected` in the conditions). Docs: `docs/running.md` ("Who may point a credential somewhere", the alerts table and API), `docs/configuration.md`, `site/guide-monitoring.html`, the MCP `plan_update_upstreams` description, T9.6's amended criterion, and the release note. Tests: (1) `api_014_file_reference_change_needs_admin` (adding, repointing and dropping a `token_file`, a `tls_client_key`, an agent token with an admin owner and `cluster:admin`); (2) `api_014_operator_cannot_redirect_credential` (the files' upstream with headers on a new URL or TLS name, an alert destination with `token_file`, one with a URL password; edits that keep the destination pass); (3) `api_014_operator_cannot_create_exec_upstream` (PUT, `checks/upstream` for `exec://` and `unix://`, a switch to `unix://`, a plugin's `args`, a forwarded domain's `exec://` server, an agent); (4) `api_014_file_path_confined` (`/proc/self/environ`, symlinks into the data directory, dangling and relative included, `..`, the data directory, the configured secrets key, a file outside `secret_file_dirs`, a list's path, a check's `tls_ca`, and a stored path no longer read and listed in `credentialIssues`); (5) `forward::api_014_forwarded_write_rechecked_on_primary` (operator and agent forwards 403, role-less ones 409 including an N−1 body, a role-less plain change applied, the admin's audited on the primary) and the role in `clu_002_writes_round_trip`; (6) `api_014_admin_redirect_audited_and_alerted` (the audit record without the credential, the alert once, the rule engine); (7) Playwright `api_014 alerts page: operators see credential fields read-only`; also `api_014_credential_rule_helpers`, `api_014_api_paths_cover_path_fields`, `api_014_every_api_path_can_be_refused`, `api_014_relative_and_dotdot_paths_refused`. OpenAPI, `api-types.ts`, `config-schema.json` and the MCP snapshot regenerated; clippy and `svelte-check` clean. Implementation notes for the owner are ADR-903 notes 13–20. *Review fixes 2026-11-16* (ADR-903 notes 21–27): a refused upstream path is cleared instead of pointed at `/proc/telltale-refused` (an unreadable upstream file failed the whole configuration, so the node answered no DNS; CLU-004), the client certificate and key together; `tls_insecure_skip_verify`, `spki_pins` and a destination's `type` are destination fields (admin-only on an entry with a credential, and they need the credential typed again); `tls_ca`, `tls_client_cert` and a list's `path` may not name a credential file the node's configuration uses, a list's `path` may not be under `secret_file_dirs`, and `/var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io` is denied to every API path; a promoted primary judges stored paths against its own files (`managed::merge_on`), not the cluster version it builds on; the `credential_redirected` alert names only the new address's scheme and host; docs and the release note say what a refused upstream path does and ask upstream users to check their entries. Tests added: `api_014_refused_upstream_path_keeps_dns_answering` (the merge and `build_dynamic` succeed; the old pointing fails it), `api_014_promoted_primary_judges_published_paths`, `api_014_non_credential_paths_kept_from_secrets`, skip-verify and pins cases in `api_014_operator_cannot_redirect_credential`, the alert's text in `api_014_admin_redirect_audited_and_alerted`, and destination cases in `api_014_credential_rule_helpers`. *Open:* (7) the Playwright test is written and lists, but wasn't run here (the dev container's Chromium lacks `libglib-2.0`); CI runs it. (8) No image was built here (small code, no crate): measure with `deploy/image/size.py --oci`. The tick with (7) and (8) open needs the owner's acceptance (ADR-903 note 27). Replicas use the primary's judgment of paths and don't judge again (ADR-903 note 25, a deviation for the owner). The Upstreams page doesn't mark admin-only fields yet (the API refuses them); `credential_issues` in heartbeats is T23.5's, and a `_ref` pointed at another kind's secret is audited with T23.3.

- [x] **T23.3 Encrypted secret store and `*_ref` fields.** *(OPS-014, NFR-005; ADR-903 §2–3)*
  - **Storage.** Migration 11 creates the `secrets` table with stamps and tombstones. The `ring` ChaCha20-Poly1305 associated data includes `kind` and the stamp.
  - **Values.** `SecretValue` and `Zeroizing`, with ciphertext cached in memory. `Credential::resolve` replaces the six read sites. Readable checks open without reading.
  - **Config fields.** The `*_ref` schema fields, serialized only when set, with validation.
  - **Key handling.** Key generation only on an empty table; the locked state; the replica self-heal rule.
  - **CLI.** `telltale secrets status | rotate-key`, offline.
  - **Health.** The reasons `secrets_locked` and `secrets_ephemeral`, and the `telltale_secrets` metric. Backups drop the table.
  - **Helm.** The default is the data-directory key. `secretsKey.existingSecret` is mounted read-only on full members only, with `[node] secrets_key_file` set. `secretsKey.generate` (lookup) is documented as unsafe under Argo CD.
  - **Docs.** `docs/secrets.md` (threat model, custody, rotation, recovery), `docs/running.md`, the Helm README, the site settings page, and help topics.

A removed member's purge restores only the local row and drops the cluster copies (`clu_015_removed_member_purge_restores_only_local`). Shadows are keyed by name and origin (migration 13), backed up never, re-encrypted by rotation and at start, and refused by heal while a local one exists (`ops_014_shadow_rows_follow_key_rotation`, `ops_014_shadow_reencrypted_at_start`, `ops_014_backup_excludes_secret_rows`). A listing doesn't undo a deletion (`clu_015_tombstone_of_older_epoch_not_resurrected`). An emergency primary's answer is non-authoritative and its pass changes nothing (`clu_015_emergency_primary_changes_nothing`, `clu_015_emergency_primary_answers_non_authoritatively`). The sync's store, registry and credential work runs on blocking threads (`tick`). Adoption is audited per name and refuses held (including deleted) names and unusable rows (`clu_015_adopt_audited_and_tombstones_conflict`). Pulls are checked against the heartbeat's head and epoch (`clu_015_answer_must_match_heartbeat`). A replica answers the reverse heal for the current primary, since its registry lists only itself: found by `clu_015_reverse_heal_over_mtls`, which now passes. Witness and ephemeral members are refused over the channel (`clu_015_witness_and_ephemeral_refused_over_the_channel`). The pin and bake tests drive `sync::tick` over the channel (`clu_015_replicates_while_pinned`, `clu_015_not_gated_by_canary_bake`), readiness is tested with a failing pull (`clu_015_fetch_never_gates_readiness`), and the simulation's exceptions are explicit (ADR-917 point 10). (b) Adoption isn't gated to one epoch of the joiner's join: a full member can offer names at any time (each audited, conflicts refused), because the registry records no join epoch (ADR-917 point 12; review M3, optional). (c) Stored-secret conflicts (orphans, shadows, kept deletions) aren't shown on the Settings page or in the API: T23.6. (d) The answer and hand-over buffers cross the cluster channel as `Vec<u8>`, and the JSON intermediates hold plaintext strings: they're zeroized only where the code owns the buffer (review L2, partly). (e) closed 2026-11-17: a heal is audited as `secrets.heal` (names and counts, never values). (f) Heartbeats still show an emergency node as primary, because the flag isn't on the wire; replicas take its answer as non-authoritative (ADR-917 point 11). (g) Failover over the real channel isn't tested, and the cluster core has a gap it would show: a heartbeat doesn't carry the primary flag (`telltale-cluster` sets `Member.primary` from the hello only), so after a role change peers keep the old flag until they reconnect, and the sync picks its primary from that flag. That's cluster-core behaviour, outside T23.5; the owner decides. The store-level schedule (16) covers failover's data rules, and the two-node tests cover the fetch, the reverse heal, adoption and the refusals over the channel. (h) The sim (16) is `telltale`'s store-level schedule, not a case in `telltale-cluster/tests/sim.rs` (the election simulator): the replicated state is the store's (ADR-917 point 9). (i) `credential_issues` lists the fields a node can't read (`credential::fields`), not the API-014 path refusals (`credential_rules::issues`).

The primary exports its `managed` rows of the node-managed kinds as `node-managed.json` (references only: `client_secret` and `client_secret_file` are stripped on export and refused on import), re-reading when `config_version` moves; a change is a new version. A replica overlays the blob after `with_shared`, under its own files (`replication::merged_with`); a promoted primary seeds its rows from the inherited blob once (marker `node-managed-seeded`) and stops overlaying it; a Git-sourced primary applies its own rows; an emergency primary passes the blob on; a resolver pod skips entries that name a stored secret. **Kind.** `oidc_provider` (`ManagedKind::OidcProvider`): `PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/oidc-providers/{id}` and the admin-only `GET /api/v1/oidc-providers` (operators 403, agents never; no MCP tool); the body is `[[auth.oidc.provider]]` without the secret fields, `client_secret_ref` names a stored secret (422 `secret_missing`), a file's ID wins (409 `set_by_file`). The UI's providers are merged into `OidcConfig.managed_provider` (not serialized, so the schema and the shared part are unchanged) rather than `provider`, so a node without `public_url` shows them inactive instead of failing the merge. **Auth.** `Auth.oidc` is an `ArcSwapOption`; the flows and the discovery cache (keyed by provider ID and issuer) are `oidc::Shared`, kept across rebuilds; `Auth::reload_oidc` is single-flight with a generation counter (a stale rebuild is discarded before and after its discovery), keeps a provider's previous settings when discovery fails (or leaves a new one out with the reason), and ends the sessions of a provider the configuration no longer names. `auth_setup::run` is the one applier (woken by reloads, secret writes and replicated secrets; they need no identity provider, the real-provider tests still need `OIDC_E2E`). 14: `site/guide-users.html`, `docs/running.md`, the Helm `values.yaml` note, spec/08 §6 and spec/12 §4. 15: no new crate (`arc-swap` is already linked by `telltale`; ## M22 — Network inventory and home integrations (fork owner request 2026-11-16)
In the fork `nbacheller/telltaledns`: the IP and DNS side of a small network, without a DHCP server (ADR-091 stands). The routers and Home Assistant already know the devices; TelltaleDNS names them in DNS, shows the networks they sit in, and stays read-only toward both. Off by default; nothing new on the query path but one branch (and, when on, one lock-free load). A Home Assistant custom integration that reads this API lives outside the binary (`integrations/home-assistant/` in the fork's bundle), so it never touches the DNS path. M25 continues it with SNMP, more gateway reads and imported scans (ADR-907).

- [x] **T22.1 Device names in DNS.** *(DNS-023, DNS-010, API-010, CLU-002; ADR-901)* `[local] device_domain`, `device_ttl` (60), `device_mdns`; `device_label`; the device-name table (`device_dns.rs`, rebuilt every 10 s off the query path, follows reloads); the pipeline answers it after local records and a zone's own records, before a zone's NXDOMAIN; explain; `dnsName` on leases.
  - *AC:* (1) a router's, Home Assistant's, or named device's name answers A and PTR, authoritatively; (2) a `[[record]]` and a zone's own record for the same name win; (3) a device name inside a zone answers, other names in the zone are still NXDOMAIN; (4) off, the table is ignored; (5) two devices with one label: the named device, then router, then mDNS, then the lowest address keeps it; a CIDR client's name never becomes a device name; (6) `local` is refused as the domain; (7) `wpad`, `isatap`, and `localhost` are never device names.
  - *Done 2026-11-16:* `telltale_policy::{device_label, LocalData::from_device_names}`, `ClientTable::{by_exact_ip, by_exact_mac, exact_ips}`, `crates/telltale/src/device_dns.rs`, `Pipeline::device_names`, `Policy::device_dns`, the `local_answer`/`zone_answer` order, `answered_early` (explain; simulation passes none: device names follow the routers, not the simulated configuration). Tests: `dns_023_*` (label, table, settings, names from every source, the pipeline order), config validation; `deploy/routers-e2e.sh` asks real DNS for three device names and a PTR.
  - *Open:* IPv6 (done in T24.8); `bench-smoke` on quiet hardware (T24.3 has Docker Desktop numbers, which can't give a verdict); UI: the settings are T24.13 and `dnsName` on the Clients page is T24.14.
- [x] **T22.2 UniFi reservations and networks.** *(API-010, API-013; ADR-902)* Known clients with a fixed IP (`rest/user`: offline reserved devices keep their name; known clients without one are left out), the networks (`rest/networkconf`: subnet from the gateway's address, VLAN when enabled, DHCP pool when on, domain; no WAN or VPN); both optional reads, reported once when they don't work; `GET /api/v1/networks`; MCP `list_networks`.

Tests: `t22_2_unifi_reservations`, `api_013_unifi_networks`; e2e: the networks API against a fake console.
  - *Open:* OPNsense networks (its interfaces API); UI: the Networks page and "make a group from this network" are T24.15 (the T3.14 deferral "router VLAN import"); routers in the UI are T23.8.
- [x] **T22.3 Home Assistant as a naming source.** *(API-012; ADR-902)* `type = "home_assistant"` (a token file only), the registry template, MACs matched to addresses from the routers' answers, else Home Assistant's trackers; rank-ordered leases (a name given in Home Assistant, then routers in configuration order, then an integration's name); `router` on leases.
