- [x] **T22.4 Home Assistant integration (companion, outside the binary).** *(API-002, AGT-004, AGT-009, OBS-013)* `integrations/home-assistant/custom_components/telltale`: a config flow (address and API token, reauth), sensors (24-hour queries, blocked, blocked share, cache hits, active devices, upstream p90, health, open anomalies, known devices, networks, version), a problem binary sensor, blocking switches for everyone and per group (off = pause for the configured minutes; the pause API's answer updates them at once), actions (pause, resume, quarantine, release, acknowledge), and a `telltale_anomaly` event per new, unmuted finding. Every change sends `X-Telltale-Reason`; a missing scope or an older build disables only that feature.
  - *Done 2026-11-16:* 10 tests (`pytest-homeassistant-custom-component`, Home Assistant 2026.2): against the real debug binary (config flow, entities, pausing a group, the audit reason, quarantine answering a blocked name and release deleting the entry), with mocks (anomaly events once, muted skipped, an upstream build's 403/404, reauth), and the registry template through Home Assistant's real `/api/template` (needs an administrator) plus a TelltaleDNS process reading a Home Assistant test server and answering `porch-light.home.arpa`. CI: `.github/workflows/home-assistant.yml`.
  - *Open:* its own repository for HACS; a diagnostics download; per-device sensors (opt-in). No UI: it lives in Home Assistant; Settings → API tokens gains a "Home Assistant" scope preset (T23.6), and the README's "Settings → Users → API tokens" becomes "Settings → API tokens".
- [x] **T22.5 Docker Desktop bundle.** *(OPS-004)* `deploy/docker-desktop/`: Compose (the fork built with `PROFILE=bench-fast`, DNS on 1053 as the unprivileged user, the API on 8053, secrets read-only, named volumes; an optional test Home Assistant with the integration mounted), a starter configuration for a UniFi gateway and Home Assistant with device names and a quarantine group, and a step-by-step README with what Docker Desktop can't show.
  - *Done 2026-11-16:* `telltale config check` passes; the configuration starts and serves with both sources unreachable; `docker compose config` validates. No UI: a deploy bundle. *Open:* the image build itself wasn't run here (Docker Hub refused anonymous pulls with 429).

M25 ("Network inventory: SNMP, gateway reads and imported scans") extends this inventory with an SNMP poller, more UniFi and Home Assistant reads, uploaded scans and one MAC-keyed inventory across every source. T25.1 is the inventory itself and closes this milestone's neighbour-table *Open* item (inventory only; the neighbour table never feeds policy, ADR-910).

## M21 — Hangs: a watchdog and debug files (GitHub issue #7)
The owner held 0.7.0 for this (2026-11-16): the reply on #7 promised "debug symbols with 0.7.0, and a watchdog so a wedged node restarts itself and leaves a dump".

- [x] **T21.1 Debug files for every build.** *(OPS-013, NFR-005; ADR-164)* The Dockerfile links once with line tables and splits the output into the stripped binary and `telltale.debug`; every release and edge build publishes `telltale-<arch>-linux.debug.zst` under the signed `SHA256SUMS`.
  - *AC:* (1) the shipped binary keeps `.dep-v0` (cargo-auditable), has a `.gnu_debuglink`, and runs as before; (2) the debug file holds the symbol table and line tables, and `addr2line -e telltale.debug` resolves an address of the stripped binary to a function and line; (3) bench-smoke A/B (five alternating rounds) shows no regression; (4) the image workflow publishes the three debug files, `SHA256SUMS` lists them and `verify.sh` checks them; (5) the size gate still passes; (6) the docs say how to read a stack with it.
  - *Done 2026-11-16:* the Dockerfile links once with line tables and splits with `llvm-objcopy` (the debug file is kept out of `/out`, which the image copies whole); `image.yml` publishes `telltale-<arch>-linux.debug.zst` (zstd 10, 21 to 25 MB) under the signed sums; `verify.sh` checks them and nightly warns if two debug files differ. Measured on the build VM with the real Docker build: the shipped binary is 5,232 bytes larger (not byte-identical code), `bench-smoke` ten alternating runs each shows no regression (cache-hot median 531k and 527k qps, miss-heavy p50 217 and 223 µs), and the images grew by about 0.04 MiB (amd64 14.70 MiB of the 15 MiB gate: the margin is 0.30 MiB). Checked on the published edge build 0.7.0-edge.369: the three files are in `SHA256SUMS`, the binary's debuglink CRC matches the downloaded debug file, and `addr2line` resolves a watchdog function to `watchdog.rs:519`.
  - *Open:* `verify.sh`'s debug-file check runs on a stable release only, so its first run is the 0.7.0 tag's build; a build ID (the zig linker writes none).
- [x] **T21.2 The watchdog.** *(OPS-012, OPS-006, CLU-004; ADR-163)* `[watchdog]` (node-local, restart-only), a thread outside the runtime that checks that the runtime runs a task and that the node's own UDP listener answers `probe.telltale.invalid`, writes a dump and exits with status 70 after `stall_secs` without a pass, with a restart limit; metrics; the newest dump in the support package; docs.
  - *AC:* (1) a stuck runtime is seen and a healthy or busy one isn't; (2) the stall is dumped once, the restart is limited per hour, `action = "dump"` never exits; (3) the dump is `0640` in a `0750` directory, the newest five kept; (4) the probe is asked of a real listener and any answer passes; (5) nothing is added to the query path; (6) a real process built with `wedge-test` is dumped and exits with status 70, and the next run says why (`deploy/watchdog/e2e.sh`, in CI).
  - *Done 2026-11-16:* `[watchdog]` (node-local, restart-only), `watchdog.rs` (the tracker, the two checks, the restart limit, the dump and its rotation, the metrics), the newest dump in the support package, docs and the FAQ, and `deploy/watchdog/e2e.sh` in CI (it passed there on the stripped `bench-fast` build). Tests: 22 in `watchdog_tests.rs`, config limits, the package. A `support_log` rotation test no longer trusts a two-second flush.
  - *Open:* the reporter's own hang has no known cause yet (the watchdog and the stacks are how the next one is read); a systemd `WatchdogSec` integration; the dump's build ID.
- [x] **T21.3 Stacks in the dump.** *(OPS-012, NFR-003; ADR-163)* Each thread reports its own return addresses when the watchdog signals it (`telltale-net::stacks`, the only `unsafe`, no new crate).
  - *AC:* (1) a blocked thread's stack holds its own function; (2) a thread that can't answer is listed as not answering within the 5 s wait; (3) a second capture works; (4) the dump's addresses resolve with the debug file (the e2e on a debug build).
  - *Done 2026-11-16:* `telltale-net::stacks` (SIGUSR2 and `_Unwind_Backtrace` declared by FFI, no new crate); two tests; the real-process e2e resolves the blocked workers to the wedge's function with `addr2line` on a debug build.
  - *Open:* **untested on armv7**: it builds and links in CI and the stripped binary runs in the QEMU smoke test, but nothing has taken a stack on a 32-bit ARM machine. The capture is bounded (5 s) and a thread that doesn't answer is listed, so the worst case is a dump with fewer frames. On **arm64** the CI test job (a real aarch64 runner) runs the two stack tests and passes them: a blocked thread's stack holds its own function there too (checked 2026-11-16 on `73ba8f2`).

- [x] **T21.4 Federated reads never hold a runtime worker (upstream #7).** *(CLU-004, CLU-002, OPS-012, API-002; ADR-946)* `call_one` and `gather` run their wait under `block_in_place` on a multi-thread runtime; every async handler's federated read runs through `blocking()`. Upstream #7's root cause: two concurrent reads to a peer that doesn't answer wedged a two-worker runtime.
  - *AC:* (1) concurrent requests on two workers to a replica whose primary never answers all finish within 10 s (`clu_004_concurrent_reads_on_a_wedged_peer_all_finish`), and it fails on the code before this task; (2) every async caller of a federated read is off the runtime (the list is in ADR-946); (3) a current-thread runtime still answers (`clu_004_a_current_thread_runtime_still_answers`); (4) request semantics are unchanged (ETags, If-Match, agent groups), by the existing tests and the cluster e2e scripts.
  - *Done 2026-11-17:* `federated.rs` (`run_off_the_worker`, used by `call_one` and `gather`), `federated_wedge_tests.rs` (two tests on a real two-node cluster, the fixture from the credential tests), `lib.rs` and `config_api.rs` (the handlers, through `blocking()`). The first test hung past its bound before the fix and passes after it, in 3.2 s. Results: `telltale-api` 165 passed; `telltale` binary 590 passed, 1 ignored (the `clu_` filter 87, `cluster` 10, `federated` 8 of them); `telltale-cluster` 58 and 1 passed when run with `--test-threads=1`; `deploy/cluster/e2e.sh` PASS (23 checks) and `failover-e2e.sh` PASS (6); `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings` clean. No hot-path crate changed, so `bench-smoke` wasn't needed. No dependency added, so the image delta is 0. No user-visible behavior changed, so no user doc changed. *Review fixes (same day):* the wedge tests run on a bounded thread (a hang fails the test, it no longer blocks CI); a direct federated read on a worker is covered (`clu_004_a_direct_federated_read_leaves_the_worker_free`); federated reads call `block_on` directly on a multi-thread runtime, with no extra thread; the ETag and config routes are in the wedge test; ADR-946 lists `slo_parts`. Open follow-ups: the group-restricted write in the wedge test, and concurrent waits in `clients` and `lists`.
  - *Open:* the worker floor (upstream #7's second suggestion) is not added: its Pi RSS can't be measured here, and the fix doesn't need it (ADR-946). `telltale-cluster`'s `net_tests` fail under the default parallel run with "Connection refused": their fixed 100 ms wait for `serve` to bind races the other tests. The crate is unchanged here; the fix is a readiness wait like the one in `federated_wedge_tests.rs`.

- [x] **T21.5 A three-node test cluster, run on every change to the cluster code.** *(CLU-001, CLU-002, CLU-003, CLU-004, CLU-005, OPS-007, API-016, DNS-001; ADR-947, ADR-948)* `deploy/cluster3/`: a primary and two replicas (one with 2 CPUs) from the same image as the Docker Desktop bundle, in their own compose project (`telltale-cluster3`, DNS 2153/2253/2353, API 8153/8253/8353, metrics 9163/9263/9363), with every credential made when the test runs. `deploy/cluster3/e2e.sh` runs the checks below and always tears down; `.github/workflows/cluster3.yml` runs it on `ubuntu-latest` for changes to the cluster, federated, replication, secrets, routers, discovery and API code, the Dockerfile, and the test.
  - *AC:* (a) all three nodes join, and the primary's `/api/v1/cluster` lists three connected nodes; (b) a record edited on the primary answers on all three nodes' DNS within 5 s; (c) a write on a replica's API is forwarded to the primary and reaches the other replica; (d) a secret set on the primary is readable on both replicas, and no read surface nor log of any node shows its value; (e) a router added on the primary appears on all nodes, and the first `[discovery]` write passes with all three up and with a member stopped (ADR-928 as decided, ADR-948); (f) queries sent to each node show up on the primary's query log labelled by node, and in the top clients per node; (g) upstream #7 on the 2-CPU replica: with the primary paused, 20 concurrent reads each return within 15 s, `/livez` and `/metrics` keep answering, DNS answers a steady loop with no timeouts, and replica-a's runtime starts with two workers (`runtime_workers` in its log); the primary is unpaused after; (h) failover: the node holding the lease is killed, a survivor is promoted 5 to 40 s later, the other follows, DNS on both survivors answers with 0 SERVFAIL, an edit on the new primary reaches the other survivor, and the killed node, started again, steps down and follows; (i) a rolling restart of each node in turn keeps at least two nodes answering DNS throughout.
  - *Done 2026-11-17:* the run found one product bug: after a promotion, a replica that had connected before the election followed the dead primary and missed every later version. The fix (`420532c`, ADR-947): the heartbeat carries the primary flag, and the primary repeats its key share every 30 s, since a refused share was never sent again. Tests: `clu_005_a_promotion_reaches_a_replica_in_the_heartbeats`, `clu_005_a_key_share_is_sent_again_after_its_interval`, `clu_005_the_heartbeat_primary_flag_is_optional_both_ways`; `telltale-cluster` 62 tests pass with `--test-threads=1`, and `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings` are clean. Final run (`deploy/cluster3/e2e.sh`, exit 0, 3 min 16 s, from an empty state): (a) three connected nodes; (b) the record answered on all three within 1.4 s; (c) the forwarded record on the primary and on replica-b; (d) the secret readable on both replicas, and its value on no read surface or log; (e) the router on all three, and the first `[discovery]` write accepted with all three up and with replica-b stopped; (f) 18 queries in the primary's log, 6 of them from replica-a, and per-node top clients; (g) twenty concurrent reads at replica-a with the primary paused, all 200, the slowest 3.4 s (bound 15 s); DNS 495 queries, 0 timeouts, 0 SERVFAIL; `/livez` 7 of 7 and `/metrics` 7 of 7; replica-a logs `workers=2` and has 9 `telltale-rt` threads against 26 on the 24-CPU primary; (h) replica-b is primary at the first poll, 0 SERVFAIL, loss 0.00% (0 of 1560), replica-a follows, the edit reaches it, and the old primary follows; (i) at least two of three nodes answer at every one of 47 samples while each node is restarted in turn.
  - *Review fixes (2026-11-17):* the review's HIGH and MEDIUM items are fixed, except the one in Open (a). (1) Check (h) had killed a replica, because check (h) turned automatic failover on after the primary had been deposed by an election the test itself caused (the product issue below); now failover is on from the bring-up, and the node that holds the lease (`telltale_cluster_lease_held` 1) is killed, with the promotion required 5 to 40 s later. (2, 3) The runtime's own worker count is logged (`runtime_workers`) and asserted (2 on replica-a); the thread count against a 24-CPU host is gone. (4) Every host port is bound to `127.0.0.1`. (5) Check (e) writes `worker_memory_mib = 48` and reads it back on replica-b. (6) The rolling-restart monitor is a daemon stopped in a `finally`, and each restart has a 120 s timeout. (7) The failover probe fails on any timeout or on more than 1% unanswered queries. (8) The CI time limit is 60 minutes. (9) `set-failover auto` prints a warning, and `docs/running.md` says to turn it on before the replicas join. (10) Check (d) asserts 200 on every read surface. (11) The CI paths cover `cluster.rs`, `forward.rs`, `settings_kinds.rs`, `managed.rs`, `api_backend.rs`, `main.rs`, `telltale-store`, `telltale-config` and `Cargo.lock`. (12, part) The key-share send is one tested decision (`clu_005_c`), and an unchanged pending CA key isn't rewritten (`clu_005_b`); the refusal WARN is still repeated (Open, item (b)). (13) An interrupted run unpauses the primary before it tears down. Found on the way, fixed: a `[discovery]` the primary sets was refused by every replica at its defaults (ADR-948 addendum, CLU-010, `clu_010_a_discovery_section_is_shared_both_ways`); and a fresh election stood at once against a primary whose lease was not yet granted, so the primary now stands first and a replica waits one lease period (ADR-947 addendum, `clu_005_d`). Tests: `telltale-cluster` 64 unit and 1 integration pass with `--test-threads=1`; `telltale-config` all pass; the `telltale` binary's 591 unit tests pass; `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings` are clean on both crates. Final run, the committed `deploy/cluster3/e2e.sh` unmodified (exit 0, 3 min 36 s, from a cold cluster; the image built first); a second run on the merged tree also passed (exit 0, 3 min 28 s, replica-a promoted 11.6 s after the kill): (a) three connected nodes; (b) the record answered on all three within 1.7 s; (c) forwarded; (d) the secret readable on both replicas, its value on no read surface or log, every read surface 200; (e) the router on all three, the first `[discovery]` write accepted with all up and with replica-b stopped, and replica-b reads 48 MiB; (f) 18 queries in the primary's log, 6 from replica-a; (g) twenty concurrent reads at replica-a with the primary paused, all 200, the slowest 3.5 s; DNS 491 queries, 0 timeouts; `/livez` 7 of 7, `/metrics` 7 of 7; replica-a `runtime_workers=2`, the primary `runtime_workers=24`; the pause lapsed the primary's lease, and replica-a was promoted (the cluster doing its job); (h) the lease holder replica-a killed, replica-b promoted 8.76 s later, 0 SERVFAIL, loss 0.00% (0 of 1574), the edit reaches the other survivor, and replica-a, started again, steps down and follows; (i) at least two of three nodes answer at every one of 50 samples while each node is restarted in turn.
  - *Open, from the review:* (a) **`set-failover auto` under a running cluster** (review 9): the replicas adopt `auto` from the signed records at once, while the primary keeps `manual` and renews no lease, so a replica can elect a new primary within seconds and depose the healthy one. The CLI warns and `docs/running.md` says to turn it on before the replicas join; the product fix is the owner's choice (the running primary adopting the mode without a restart, or a replica not standing against a primary whose records say `manual`). It needs a fork issue (not filed from this track) and an ADR (fork ADRs 947 and 948 are this track's, both in use). (b) The refused key share is still repeated every 30 s until the peer learns the new primary, and the receiver logs the refusal each time (review 12). Stopping that needs per-stream state; not fixed here. (c) *Fixed after the merge:* `telltale-cluster`'s parallel run failed with "Connection refused" because each test slept a fixed 100 ms before connecting; the tests now wait until the listener accepts (`listening()` in `net_tests.rs`), and six parallel `-j 4` runs pass. (d) A survivor's poll in check (h) got no 200 once in the runs (the first failed run); signing in again before the kill fixed it, and the probe now reports each survivor's poll status. The cause isn't pinned down: a session that lapsed when that node stepped down during (g) is the likely one.
  - *Open:* (1) replicas in manual failover mode report each other as not heard from (`peers_up` fails, `healthy: false` on the replica's Cluster page), since the replica mesh runs only in automatic mode; the primary says all three are up. Evidence: the first run's replica-a `/api/v1/cluster`. (2) The owner's wording of check (f) names `/api/v1/clients`, which lists configured devices and has no node labels; the check uses `/api/v1/stats/top?kind=clients&scope=node:<name>`. (3) *Decided:* the gate's first write for a dev cluster and for a stopped member (ADR-948, accepted; the dev version is T21.6). (4) Replicas without upstreams log `config: upstream: none configured` as a warning, which is correct but noisy. (5) *Fixed:* see (c) above.
- [x] **T21.6 A dev build reports a readable version.** *(CLU-005, OPS-007; ADR-948)* Owner decision on ADR-948: when `TELLTALE_BUILD_VERSION` isn't set, `crates/telltale/build.rs` stamps `<CARGO_PKG_VERSION>-dev` instead of `dev`. Every reader of a peer's version (the `[discovery]` gate `members_too_old`, the members view, the version-skew checks) parses the `-dev` suffix as that version. AC: a unit test that `0.8.0-dev` passes the `DISCOVERY_SINCE` gate and a bare unreadable version still refuses; `/api/v1/version`, `telltale --version` and the UI show the new string; `deploy/cluster3` drops its `TELLTALE_BUILD_VERSION=0.8.0` build arg and check (e) still passes; docs and the help topic that mention `dev` are updated.
  - *Done 2026-11-17 (ADR-948):* `build.rs` stamps `<CARGO_PKG_VERSION>-dev` when `TELLTALE_BUILD_VERSION` is unset (the Dockerfile arg defaults to empty, so an image is `-dev` too). The gate reads a version by its core (`forward::version_at_least` drops the `-dev` suffix); the own-node `dev` special case (`gate_version`) is gone, since this node reports a readable version now. The Cluster page's versions check counts a `-dev` build and its release as one version (`cluster::versions_check`); the updates check still says a local build is "newer than the index". `/api/v1/version`, `telltale --version` (`telltale 0.8.0-dev (commit …)`), the sidebar and Settings show the string the server sends, so the UI needed no change; no Playwright test matched the old `dev` string. `deploy/cluster3` drops `TELLTALE_BUILD_VERSION=0.8.0` (compose and README). Docs: `docs/running.md`, `site/guide-upgrades.html`, the OpenAPI description (`docs/api/openapi.json`, and `ui/src/lib/api-types.ts` by hand with the same text the generator writes). The help topics in `docs/help/topics.json` never named the `dev` string, so none changed. Tests: `clu_005_dev_build_reports_a_readable_version` (`forward`), `clu_005_dev_build_is_its_release_in_the_versions_check` (`cluster`), `dsc_001_n1_member_refuses_first_write` extended with `0.8.0-dev` (passes) and `0.7.9-dev` (refused), `dsc_001_current_build_passes_the_gate` now uses the real build version, `ops_004_versions_compare_within_a_channel` pins `0.8.0-dev`. Verified: the telltale unit tests named by those filters pass (56, 7 and 8 tests), `api_001_committed_openapi` regenerated and passing, clippy `-D warnings` clean for `telltale` and `telltale-api`, rustfmt clean. `deploy/cluster3/e2e.sh` (no stamp) passes checks a to i, check (e) included (three nodes connected; replica-b promoted 11.41 s after the kill, 0.00% loss). No new ADR: the choices follow ADR-948.
- [x] **T21.7 Turning on automatic failover in a running cluster is safe.** *(CLU-005; ADR-947 addendum, T21.5 open (a); a new fork ADR)* Owner decision 2026-11-17: the running primary adopts `auto` as soon as it signs the failover record, with no restart, and starts renewing its lease at once. A replica stands for election only after it has seen a lease from the current primary and that lease has lapsed; a primary that has never held a lease under `auto` is not deposed. Also fix T21.5 open (1): replicas in either mode report each other's health from the heartbeats they relay, so `peers_up` isn't false in manual mode. AC: unit tests for the switch on a live primary (lease renewed within one interval, no election while it renews) and for a replica that never saw a lease; `deploy/cluster3/e2e.sh` turns failover on after the replicas join (the order the CLI warning forbids today) and check (h) still passes with no deposition before the kill; the CLI warning and `docs/running.md`'s ordering note are removed; the Cluster page shows the mode change without a restart (Playwright).
  - *Done 2026-11-17:* ADR-960 (Proposed). `election.rs` (`stand_only_after_a_lease`, `lease_seen`, `renew_now`), `failover.rs` (a primary resumes its epoch whenever its election starts, `cluster.json` change reloads on the next tick, `begin_lease`), `net.rs` and `wire.rs` (heartbeat tag 41 `peers`, `RelayedMember`, `relayed_members`), `telltale/src/cluster.rs` (relayed members shown up with link `relayed`; the CLI warning removed), `docs/running.md` and `site/guide-cluster.html` (ordering note removed), OpenAPI and UI types regenerated (link doc only), the Cluster page (tooltip on `relayed`). Tests: `clu_005_t21_7_a_running_primary_adopts_auto_and_is_not_deposed` (lease at the first tick, one epoch, no replica stands over 60 s of renewals), `clu_005_t21_7_a_replica_stands_only_after_a_lease_it_saw_lapses` (a replica that never saw a lease waits 600 s without asking; one that did stands once it lapses), `clu_005_t21_7_a_stored_ballot_counts_as_a_lease_seen`, `clu_001_a_replica_learns_that_another_replica_is_up_from_the_primarys_heartbeats`; Playwright `clu_005 the failover mode changes on the Cluster page without a restart` (the whole file: 167 passed). `telltale-cluster` 72 + 1 sim test pass; `telltale` filter `clu_ cluster maintenance failover ops_010` 98 pass; `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings` clean. `deploy/cluster3/e2e.sh` now turns failover on after the replicas join, with no restart: new check a2 (each replica reports all three up in manual mode; after `set-failover auto` all three adopt it, the primary holds a lease, keeps epoch 1 and its role for 45 s, no replica stands), and check h still passes (replica-b held the lease and was killed, replica-a promoted 9.68 s later, loss 0.00%, the old primary follows); the full run passed all checks. CI found two effects of the rule in `deploy/cluster/failover-e2e.sh`: a replica killed-for never granted the primary a renewal (the script killed the primary within 5 s of the join), and a deposed primary whose successor died before it had granted that successor a renewal never stood (its own old lease refuses renewals for a lease period); the second is fixed in the rule (a ballot for itself plus a newer epoch counts as a lease seen, `clu_005_t21_7_a_stored_ballot_counts_as_a_lease_seen`), and both older scripts now wait for the replica's first renewal. T21.5 open (a) and open (1) are closed. Image delta: no dependency added, 0. No hot-path crate changed (`telltale-cluster` and the binary only), so no `bench-smoke`.
  - *Open:* (1) a replica that has never seen a lease does not stand, so `set-failover auto` while the primary is already down elects nobody (promote by hand first; ADR-960 §2, a conservative choice). (2) A primary rebuilt by a voter change pauses publishing for one round trip (it resumes with no lease). (3) The Docker build's shared cargo cache keys on file mtimes: after another track's build, a changed crate can be taken as fresh; touching the changed sources before the run (as done here) works around it.

## M20 — Quick rule Edit (GitHub issue #57)

- [x] **T20.1 Edit a quick rule in place.** *(FLT-005, OBS-024, API-002; GitHub issue #57; ADR-162)* An **Edit** action on each quick rule made through the UI or API: the form opens filled in, with the same dry-run preview, and saves under the rule's own ID.
  - *AC:* (1) **Edit** opens the form from the rule (action, domain, scope, who, note) and offers **Keep the current end** (selected) for a rule with a live end time; a new duration counts from now. (2) Saving replaces the rule on the next query: the old domain is no longer allowed and there is no second rule. (3) The rule keeps its ID, who made it and when (`createdBy`, `created`); the audit entry (`rule.put`) has the rule before and after. (4) The preview simulates the replacement, not an addition. (5) The confirmation stays on the page after the row is redrawn. (6) No Edit for rules from a config file, for an expired rule, or for a rule with several devices or groups (the form holds one scope). (7) Docs, the site guide and the trace are updated.
  - *Open:* editing a rule with several devices or groups from the page (a multi-select scope).
  - *Done 2026-11-16:* `rule_of` keeps `createdBy`/`created` when a write replaces a rule (no new operation, no schema change); `QuickRuleForm` takes a `rule` copy (starts from it, saves under its ID, **Keep the current end**, simulates under the same ID, Cancel); the Quick rules page's **Edit**, its edit row and the page-level notice; `endsAt` moved to `format.ts`; docs (`running.md`, the help topic, the site's blocking guide). Tests: `flt_005_editing_a_rule_keeps_its_maker_and_creation_time`, e2e `flt_005 edit a quick rule in place` (found that the notice named the old domain: fixed). Verified on the build VM: clippy clean, `svelte-check` clean, 77 e2e tests.

## M19 — Anomaly mutes (GitHub issue #38)

- [x] **T19.1 Mute a kind of anomaly finding for a device.** *(OBS-038, OBS-014, API-002, AGT-001; GitHub issue #38; ADR-161)* A shared `[[anomaly_mute]]` entry (name, devices, groups, kinds, optional `expires`, note) with the usual configuration surfaces; the anomaly engine matches it when it creates a finding; a muted finding is kept and listed but not counted; the Anomalies page offers **Mute for this device…**, a **Show muted** switch, and the list of mutes.
  - *AC:* (1) a finding of a muted kind for a muted device (by address, network, device name or group) is recorded with `muted` and `mutedBy`, and does not count in `telltale_anomalies_total`, the badge, the `anomaly` alert or the health level, while the same kind for another device still does; (2) `telltale_anomalies_muted_total{kind}` counts what was muted; (3) an `expires` in the past mutes nothing; (4) `PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/anomaly-mutes/{name}` and the MCP tools dry-run, check, audit, are refused with 409 `gitops_managed` under Git, and a mute set on the primary reaches every node; (5) an unknown kind, a name that matches no device or group, or an empty list is a 422 with a hint, and a MAC address says why it isn't accepted; (6) the page's **Mute for this device…** pre-fills the finding's device and kind and shows the preview before saving; **Show muted** lists muted findings with their mute; (7) replaying the same queries with the same mutes gives the same findings; (8) docs, the help topic, OpenAPI, MCP tools and the trace are regenerated.
  - *Open:* matching a device by MAC (the address changes, and the neighbour table isn't read on the telemetry thread); muting one domain's findings rather than a kind.
  - *Done 2026-11-15:* `AnomalyMuteConfig` and `telltale_config::mute` (validation and the resolved networks, shared by the check and the engine); `MuteTable` and `Finding::muted_by` in the engine, decided once in `Engine::push` against the window start (`total_muted` beside `total`); the managed `anomaly_mute` kind (`PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/anomaly-mutes/{name}`, `config/entries?kind=anomaly_mute`, forwarded from replicas, audited); `muted`/`mutedBy` on the API and federation merge, `muted=true` on the list, the alert rule skips muted findings; `telltale_anomalies_muted_total{kind}`; MCP `list_anomaly_mutes`, `plan_set_anomaly_mute`, `plan_remove_anomaly_mute`, `find_anomalies` `muted`; agent scope `config:write:anomalies`; the Anomalies page (**Mute for this device…** with a dry-run preview, **Show muted**, the mutes list), help topic, `docs/running.md`, the site's monitoring and agents pages. Tests: engine (muted not counted, kinds, networks, expiry, replay, history kept), config validation, API list, agent scope, e2e `obs_038`.
  - *Deferred:* (none of the acceptance criteria). Judged: the shared-configuration test for a mute reaching a replica rides on the existing managed-entry replication (CLU-003) and is not repeated here.

## M14 — Visual observability pack (owner request 2026-10-16)
From the design session of 2026-10-16: nine visual views over data the node already has, each with a design doc in `docs/design/`. Same condition as M11 and M13: nothing on the query path; everything runs on the telemetry thread, a background task, the API, or the browser. Order: T14.7 (every later chart overlays its markers) → T14.1 → T14.3 → T14.4 → T14.6 → T14.2 → T14.5 → T14.8 → T14.9 (composes the others).

- [x] **T14.9 Wall display (kiosk mode).** *(OBS-035, ADR-135; design `docs/design/wall-display.md`)* `#/wall` without navigation: rotating panels (traffic map with tweens, flow with particles, SLO rings, latency heatmap, rhythm heatmap) from `?panels=` and `?every=`, the live tail ticker, keyboard control, reduced motion; a **Wall** link on the dashboard.
  - *AC:* (1) No navigation; `?panels=slo,flow` shows those two in order; `every=5` rotates in 5 s. (2) Tweens equal both layouts at t = 0 and 1. (3) 200 links keep ≤ 60 particles. (4) Reduced motion or `?motion=off`: no animation classes, no loop. (5) A hidden tab stops the loop; visible resumes it. (6) The ticker keeps the newest 50 under 10,000 events. (7) The pack's bundle growth reported. (8) Playwright `obs_035 wall`; docs; site.
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* `ui/src/lib/wall.ts` (`parsePanels`, `parseEvery`, `motionAllowed`, `lerpBoxes`, `ease`, `dotBudget`, `Particles`, `Ticker`, `Loop` with a visibility-aware host), `pages/Wall.svelte` (rendered by `App.svelte` without the layout; rotation restarted by each move, title fading after 3 s, progress hairline, clock and mark, Space / arrows / Esc / click, the tail ticker), panels `components/wall/WallMap` (squarify + 600 ms tweens, pulse on growth), `WallFlow` (sankey layout, ≤ 60 particles along the bands), `WallSlo` (rings, burn needle, top 3 spenders), `WallHeat` (the week, 15-minute slots), and `LatencyHeatmap`'s `wall` prop; a **Wall** link on the dashboard (Advanced). Tests: Playwright `obs_035 wall helpers` (the module imported by the spec: tweens at 0 and 1, 200 links ≤ 60 dots, 10,000 events → the newest 50, the loop stopping while hidden) and `obs_035 wall` (no navigation, `?panels=` order, a 5 s rotation, every panel, Space, `motion=off`, Esc). Gate on the VM: 792 tests, 60 Playwright, clippy; UI 198.9 KiB gzipped. **The pack's bundle growth: 162.9 → 198.9 KiB gzipped, +36.0 KiB** (target < 60 KB). No bench (UI only). *Deferred:* the Pi 4 Chromium CPU figure; the demo screenshot and GIF; a viewer role or kiosk session (design §8, proposed to the owner).
- [x] **T14.8 Device "day in the life" timeline.** *(OBS-034, ADR-134; design `docs/design/device-day-timeline.md`)* `GET /api/v1/clients/{id}/timeline` (lanes per domain with queries, blocked, slow; latency lane; events; markers; cached 30 s), MCP `device_timeline`, the Clients page's device drawer **Timeline** (canvas swimlanes, events rail, tooltips, table), links from anomaly findings and the heatmap.
  - *AC:* (1) A day with `ntp.org` every 2.5 s from 14:00 to 18:30, `example.com` 10 an hour, and 3 blocked bursts: the `ntp.org` lane is zero before 14:00 and 120 per 5 minutes after; 3 `blockedBurst` events; no `other` with 3 domains under 30 lanes. (2) Truncation (see ADR-134). (3) Privacy 1: no domains; privacy 2: refused. (4) Federation (see ADR-134). (5) The same request within 30 s reads once. (6) Playwright `obs_034 device timeline`. (7) Docs, site, help topic `device-timeline`, OpenAPI/MCP.
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* `telltale_api::timeline` (`GET /api/v1/clients/{id}/timeline`: `bins` (whole minutes, epoch-aligned), `texts` (three vqlog reads with the limit raised to 100,000 groups), `fold` (lanes, `other`, queries and p99 per bin), `bursts`, first-seen events from `Backend::new_domains`, the device's findings, markers from `Backend::annotations`, `coverageFrom` from the new `Backend::qlog_retention_days`, `cached` (30 s, 64 answers, `READS` for the test)), agents `querylog:read`, MCP `device_timeline` (24 bins, 10 lanes). UI: `ui/src/lib/timeline.ts` (geometry, hit, square-root shading on `--hm-ink`, blocked underline, slow tick, the latency lane, greyed coverage, table spans), `DeviceTimeline.svelte` (window 6 h–7 d, canvas lanes, HTML events rail and marker ticks, "new" tags, tooltips, lane labels to the query log, table, theme redraw), the Clients page's device drawer (**Rhythm** | **Timeline**, `#/clients?device=…&view=…`, **Day** on Seen rows), *See the day* on findings, *Day timeline* on a device's heatmap; help topic `device-timeline`. Tests: `obs_034_a_day_folds_into_lanes_and_bursts`, `obs_034_the_same_request_reads_once`, `obs_034_the_reads_parse`, `obs_034_bins`, Playwright `obs_034 device timeline`. Gate on the VM: 792 tests, 58 Playwright, clippy; UI 193.0 KiB gzipped (the two svelte-check warnings of the device toggle's `role=tab` fixed after the gate: 0 warnings). No bench (no query-path or telemetry crate touched). *Deferred:* identity-change events (`changedAt` in `devices.json`); a slow-reader truncation test with the design's 500k / 5 s bounds (vqlog's own apply: ADR-134); screenshots.
- [x] **T14.5 Rhythm strip for periodic traffic.** *(OBS-031, OBS-013, ADR-131; design `docs/design/beacon-rhythm-strip.md`)* A log-spaced inter-arrival histogram (24 bins, ms) per tracked pair with a decayed copy; `GET /api/v1/clients/{id}/rhythm` (federated), MCP `get_client_profile.rhythm`, beacon findings' `rhythm`; `RhythmStrip.svelte` in the Clients page's Rhythm drawer and on beacon findings.
  - *AC:* (1) A device asking `pool.ntp.org` every 2.5 s ± 0.1 s for an hour has ≥ 95 % of the hour in bin 3 (2.2–3.3 s); random gaps to `example.com` leave no bin above 30 %; the golden anomaly fixture is unchanged. (2) At the hour's close `recent` is `floor(prev/2) + hour`, saturating at 65,535. (3) Privacy (see ADR-131). (4) Two nodes sum bins by domain; a down node is in `missingNodes`. (5) Bench: per-event cost unchanged within noise; memory at 1,024 devices × 16 pairs reported. (6) Playwright `obs_031 rhythm strip`. (7) Docs, site, help topic `rhythm`, OpenAPI/MCP.
  - *Done 2026-10-17:* `telltale_telemetry::anomaly`: `RHYTHM_EDGES_MS`, `rhythm_bin`, `Tracked::{last_ms, rhythm, rhythm_long}` (serde defaults, so older `anomaly.json` files load), `fold_rhythm` at each hour's close, `Engine::observe_query` (µs; `observe_answer` wraps it), `Engine::rhythm`, `Engine::beacon_rhythms` + `beacon_rhythm` beside the findings (the golden file unchanged), `RHYTHM_BYTES`; the server's sink passes `ts_us`. `telltale_api::rhythm_api` (`GET /api/v1/clients/{id}/rhythm`, `merge`, `finish`, `period_text`; 422 `privacy_level` from level 1), `Backend::client_rhythm` (`ApiBackend`: by address or named device; `Federated`: `Read::ClientRhythm`, summed by domain), `AnomalyFinding.rhythm`, agents `analytics:read`, MCP `get_client_profile.rhythm` (`bins=false`). UI: `RhythmStrip.svelte` (SVG cells shaded by share of the row's busiest bin, dominant outlined at ≥ 50 %, period words, usual / new rhythm badges, recent / this hour, table, sticky labels), the Clients page's **Rhythm** drawer, the strip on beacon findings; help topic `rhythm`. Tests: `obs_031_a_time_sync_loop_is_one_bin_and_a_browser_a_smear`, `obs_031_the_hour_folds_into_the_decayed_copy`, `obs_031_rhythm_memory_at_1024_devices` (anomaly state 5.25 MiB at 1,024 devices × 16 pairs, of which the rhythm 1.63 MiB), `obs_031_nodes_sum_bins_by_domain`, `obs_031_period_text`, Playwright `obs_031 rhythm strip`. Gate on the VM: 788 tests, 57 Playwright, clippy; UI 188.8 KiB gzipped; bench-smoke cache-hot 464k q/s (two reruns 450k and 538k: noise), miss-heavy p99 479 µs. *Deferred:* the "chatty periodic" finding (design §8, proposed to the owner); both-theme screenshots.
- [x] **T14.2 Node odd-one-out comparison.** *(OBS-028, ADR-128; design `docs/design/node-comparison.md`)* `GET /api/v1/cluster/compare` (every node's window numbers, Wilson intervals, sparkline series, `odd`/`note` findings with `oddSince`), MCP `compare_nodes`, the Cluster page's **Compare** tab (small multiples, odd cell outlined, notes, table with intervals, phone layout, tab count), Grafana *Nodes* row.

(2) 2 nodes, Pi 15 bad / 38k and pods 14 / 300k: no `odd`, one `note` "same bad count… smaller base"; Pi 150 / 38k: `odd`. (3) The same fixture twice gives byte-identical JSON. (4) A missing node is in `missingNodes`; one node: no findings, "One node; nothing to compare". (5) Playwright `obs_028 node compare`. (6) Docs, site, OpenAPI/MCP.

Grafana: *SERVFAIL answers (range)* and *by node*. Docs: `running.md` *Who spent it?*, site monitoring page. Tests: `obs_027_bad_answers_are_broken_down_and_only_they_reach_the_sketches` (aggregator), `obs_027_bad_answers_round_trip_and_roll_up`, `obs_027_top_lists_sum_over_hours` (store), `obs_027_the_fixture_is_attributed`, `…_older_nodes_are_partial_not_errors`, `…_privacy_levels`, `…_nodes_sum`, `…_objectives_parse` (API), Playwright `obs_027 budget attribution`; the `Federated` guard test passes. Gate on the VM: 766 tests, 52 Playwright, clippy; bench-smoke cache-hot 458k q/s, miss-heavy p50 263 µs / p99 471 µs (bad answers only touch the new counters). Also: the marker lists key by position (two pauses resumed in one second are two equal lines). *Not done:* per-row disabled Query log links for rows older than the log (a note says the log keeps fewer days); a three-node federation test (covered by the `Federated` merge, the guard test, and the two-node cluster e2e); the demo screenshot. *Open (reported, not fixed, design §8):* whether a SERVFAIL should be cached and served as `refreshed` at all.
- [x] **T14.7 Change markers.** *(OBS-033, AGT-004, AGT-009, ADR-133; design `docs/design/change-markers.md`)* `<data_dir>/annotations.jsonl` (20,000 lines, 90 days) written by the background tasks that see each event; `GET`/`POST /api/v1/annotations` (federated `Read::Annotations`; notes with scope `ops:annotate`, audited); MCP `list_annotations`, `annotate`, `get_overview.changes`; `telltale_config_seq`, `telltale_annotations_total{kind}`; `Chart.svelte` markers from one shared store, a Changes toggle and "Note…" form on the dashboard, Settings → System → Changes, node pages; Grafana annotations.
  - *AC:* (1) Applying configuration 313 from GitOps writes one `config.applied` line with `seq: 313`, `source: "gitops"`; a restart writes `node.restarted`, and `build.started` only when the build changed (both cases); a list refresh that changes nothing writes no line. (2) 25,000 synthetic lines compact to the newest 20,000 at start; lines older than 90 d are dropped; a write error gives one warning and the service is unaffected. (3) The federated read merges two nodes newest first; `missingNodes` for a down node; `truncated` at the cap. (4) `telltale_config_seq` equals the applied sequence on every node; `telltale_annotations_total{kind}` increments per line. (5) A posted note appears in the federated read from another node with the writer's `node`; 201 characters → 422 `annotation_text`; a future time → 422 `annotation_time`; `PUT` and `DELETE` → 405; an agent token without `ops:annotate` → 403; the audit log has `annotation.note` with the agent's owner. (6) Playwright `obs_033 change markers`: after a config change the dashboard's Queries chart shows a marker whose hover names the sequence; the toggle hides it; Settings → System lists it; the "Note…" form adds a marker that shows the actor on hover. (7) Docs: `running.md` *Change markers* (incl. notes), `docs/monitoring` Grafana annotations, site page, help topic `change-markers`; OpenAPI/MCP regenerated; the Helm chart's dashboard JSON rebuilt.
  - *Done 2026-10-16:* `crates/telltale/src/annotations.rs` (`Annotations`: append with `O_APPEND`, no fsync, a failed write counted and logged once an hour; compaction to 20,000 lines and 90 days at start, daily, and at twice the cap; `started` (`node.restarted`, `build.started` when the build differs from the last restart's), `set_provenance`/`reloaded`/`published` for `config.applied`, `breaker` (one line per upstream per 5 min, the state that held), `lists_line`, `changed_sections`, `spawn` for the breaker poll every 5 s and the daily compaction); hooks in `server::serve` and `server::reload` (`config_marker`), `api_backend` (`provenance` before each API write's reload), `replication::apply` (replica: `gitops`/`cluster`, the manifest's `seq` and filter version), the publish loop (`published`, Git commit → `gitops`), `gitsource` (author), `lists` compile loop, `maintenance` start/end, the rollout stages (`rollout_marker`: canary, promoted, failed, aborted, pinned, unpinned), the pause, resume, and flush handlers (`annotations_api::mark`, naming the nodes); `datadir::unclean_start`. `telltale_api::annotations_api` (`GET /api/v1/annotations` with `from`, `to`, `kinds`, `scope`, `limit` ≤ 2,000, `before`, `truncated`, `nextBefore`; `POST` with `check_text`/`check_time`, operator, audited `annotation.note`; `PUT`/`DELETE` 405 `method_not_allowed`), problem codes `annotation_text`, `annotation_time`, `method_not_allowed`, `time::parse_time_ms`, model `Annotation`, `AnnotationFilter`, `AnnotationPage`, `NoteRequest`; `Backend::annotations`/`annotate`, `Read::Annotations` in `Federated` (labelled, merged newest first); scope `ops:annotate` (operator, not default); MCP `list_annotations`, `annotate`, `get_overview.changes` (count and the latest three); `telltale_config_seq`, `telltale_annotations_total{kind}`, `telltale_annotation_write_failures_total`. UI: `annotations.svelte.ts` (one fetch per window, refetched at most once a minute; merges one kind and `seq` across nodes), `Chart.svelte` markers (focusable buttons over the plot, a line and a glyph per kind, notes in their own colour, hover/focus tooltip with what, who, and nodes, click opens `href`, `×N` past 40 in view, rows in the table view), `ChangesControls.svelte` (the Changes toggle, Note…, kind chips in Advanced), the dashboard's and Cache page's charts, Settings → System → Changes (the last 200, by kind and text), `--mk-ink`/`--mk-note`, help topic `change-markers`. Grafana: dashboard annotations from `changes()` on `telltale_config_seq`, `telltale_filter_snapshot_version`, and `telltale_process_starts_total`. Docs: `running.md` *Change markers*, the metrics table, scopes, and MCP tools; site monitoring and agents pages. Tests: `obs_033_config_applied_from_gitops`, `…_restarts_and_new_builds`, `…_compaction_caps_lines_and_age`, `…_a_write_error_warns_once`, `…_lines_are_counted_per_kind`, `…_reads_filter_and_sort`, `…_breakers_are_rate_limited`, `…_list_refreshes`, `…_changed_sections` (store), `obs_033_notes_are_checked`, `…_read_parameters`, `…_pages_merge_newest_first`, `…_sources_from_the_actor`, `…_millisecond_times`, `…_annotation_routes_need_their_scope`, `obs_033_notes_over_http` (API), `deploy/cluster/e2e.sh` *change markers across the cluster* (a forwarded change is one marker per node with one `seq`, the primary's with the author and the replica's `cluster`; a replica's note in the primary's read labelled `r`; a one-marker page truncated with `nextBefore`; `telltale_config_seq` equal on both nodes; the replica's note counted; the stopped replica in `missingNodes`), Playwright `obs_033 change markers`. Gate on the VM: 758 tests, 51 Playwright, clippy, cluster e2e pass; UI 167.3 KiB gzipped. Bench-smoke interleaved with main (no query-path change): tree 420k/380k q/s cache-hot, miss-heavy p99 655/655 µs; main 419k/435k, 639/607 µs. Also: the site's decisions page split into an index and pages of 50 (ADR-136; it had reached the 500 KiB budget). ADR-133 Proposed with the owner's seven start decisions. *Not done:* `ref.audit` (an audit entry's ID on the line: the audit append returns none; markers link to Settings → System instead); the design's `config {{seq}}` Grafana text (the sequence is a value, not a label: the text names the instance); node pages with their own charts don't exist (the dashboard's node picker shows one node's markers); the sanitized demo-network screenshot.
